# Performance Triage

This document tracks benchmark-driven follow-up work for the long-term product
goal. It records both successful optimizations and rejected changes so the
project does not repeat unproductive work.

## Current High-Value Gaps

| Area | Local Rust evidence | Current Alea evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Full-covariance multivariate normal | No matching sampler in the local `rand_distr 0.6.0` baseline; this is a Zig-native above-Rust workflow | Corrected benchmarks observe all output coordinates and move checksums outside timed regions. Same-host 1GiB rows after f64 1–3D transform and full-rank 3D batch specializations are about 123.5M f64 direct, 182.5M f64 batch, 159.6M f32 direct, and 173.0M f32 batch vectors/s, all with checksum `-1052222.473`. Baseline generic rows were about 81.4M/83.9M f64 and 158.8M/75.8M f32 direct/batch. | Adopted. The small-dimension specializations preserve repeated-sample output and final engine state for f32/f64 full-rank and singular f64 cases. A two-pass bulk-normal candidate was rejected because extra memory traffic regressed f64/f32 batch throughput. |
| Static fixed-dimension multivariate normal | No matching local Rust sampler; dynamic Alea 8D sampling is the local baseline | `StaticMultivariateNormal(f64, 8)` stores state inline and lets Zig unroll the transform. Same-host 1GiB rows are about 46.0M static versus 31.9M dynamic vectors/s with matching checksum `4473.057` and final RNG state in focused parity tests. | Adopted as a separate allocation-free/comptime API. Keep dynamic `MultivariateNormal(T)` for runtime dimensions/configuration rather than forcing static code size on all users. |
| Poisson `lambda = 20` | Filtered `rand_distr poisson`: about 67-68M samples/s | After inlining the reusable `Poisson.sampleFrom` / `fillFrom` and Ahrens-Dieter `sampleFrom` hot paths, focused Alea rows are about 58M facade top-level, 77M FastPrng top-level direct, 85M ScalarPrng top-level direct, 75-76M cached FastPrng, 83M cached ScalarPrng, 76-77M FastPrng fill, and 84M ScalarPrng fill. `poisson-probe -- 4194304` confirms the same Ahrens-Dieter branch mix for both engines: about 67.3% first normal accept, 29.2% squeeze accept, 0.54% exact accept, and 3.0% fallback accept. | Closed for direct/cached/fill workflows, all now above filtered Rust; facade top-level remains lower and isolated as `Rng` facade/context overhead, not branch mix or reusable sampler overhead. |
| Poisson top-level inline attempt | Tried marking `poisson` and `poissonFrom` inline after reusable hot-path inlining to see whether the remaining facade top-level gap was wrapper/codegen overhead | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "poisson"` only moved facade top-level to about 59M while direct/cached/fill rows stayed high at about 75-85M; `poisson-probe` direct rows stayed about 78M FastPrng and 85M ScalarPrng. | Rejected. Keep top-level wrappers normal; the remaining facade top-level gap is `Rng` facade/context overhead, not top-level inline. |
| Poisson max-lambda validation | Local `rand_distr::Poisson::new` rejects finite `lambda > 1.844e19` with `ShapeTooLarge`; Alea intentionally keeps `lambda == 0` as a no-consume point-mass extension. | Scalar/vector checked helpers, reusable `Poisson` / `VectorPoisson`, fills, and unchecked/assert-fast helpers now share the same finite upper-bound guard while retaining the zero-lambda extension. Focused tests cover invalid no-consume paths and acceptance at the exact local Rust max value. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` constructor compatibility; this is an input-validation parity fix rather than a throughput change. |
| Geometric `p = 0.25` | `rand_distr geometric`: about 44M samples/s and returns failures before first success | `alea geometric failures`: about 117M samples/s; direct-source fill about 128M | Closed for current local Rust evidence; Alea now exposes both one-based trial-count and rand-style failure-count semantics |
| StandardGeometric `p = 0.5` | `rand_distr standard-geometric`: about 913M samples/s | `alea standard-geometric`: about 748-758M facade, about 1263M scalar direct, and about 994M direct-source fill | Closed for scalar-fast and direct-source bulk workflows; use `ScalarPrng` for high-volume p=0.5 failure-count sampling |
| Geometric failure-count p=0 compatibility | Local `rand_distr::Geometric::new(0.0)` and `new(-0.0)` accept zero probability and return `u64::MAX` without drawing once `pi == 1.0`; tiny finite probabilities with `1.0 - p == 1.0` share the same branch. | `GeometricFailures` scalar/vector reusable, checked, unchecked, and fill helpers now return/fill max-`u64` without consuming randomness for those failure-count states. One-based `Geometric` keeps `p == 0` invalid and saturates tiny positive probabilities to max-`u64` instead of overflowing `failures + 1`. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` failure-count compatibility; this is an input/edge-semantics fix rather than a throughput change. |
| Hypergeometric `(100, 30, 10)` | `rand_distr hypergeometric`: about 50M samples/s | `alea hypergeometric`: about 51M facade after HIN inverse-transform fast path, about 49M fill, about 48M direct-source fill | Closed for current local Rust evidence on this HIN parameter set; broader H2PE coverage is tracked by the balanced and skew-large rows below |
| Hypergeometric large `(5000, 2500, 500)` | `rand_distr hypergeometric large`: about 21M samples/s | Alea H2PE production rows are about 20.7M facade/fill and about 23.1M direct-source fill; `distcheck` covers the large-parameter mean around 250 | Closed for current local Rust evidence on this balanced large H2PE parameter set; skew-large coverage is tracked by the row below |
| Hypergeometric skew-large `(10000, 1000, 2000)` | Added a second large-parameter H2PE comparison that is asymmetric rather than the existing 50/50 `(5000, 2500, 500)` row; focused Rust `rand_distr hypergeometric skew-large` is about 21.7M samples/s | Added Zig and Rust benchmark rows plus a `distcheck` mean gate around 200. Focused Alea rows are about 21.3M facade, 21.3M fill, and 23.8M direct-source fill. | Closed for this broader H2PE parameter check; H2PE coverage now includes both balanced and skewed large-parameter rows with local Rust parity-plus direct-source evidence. |

| Exponential `f64` focused row | Latest filtered `rand_distr exp1`: about 438M samples/s; `rand_distr exponential`: about 443M historical full row | Fresh filtered `bench -- 268435456 "standard-exponential"` reports `alea standard-exponential` about 563M sampler / 573M raw and f32 about 558M; isolated ziggurat-probe hot path reaches about 615M | Closed for focused scalar standard-exponential evidence; unfiltered all-suite order/context remains benchmark hygiene |
| Standard normal/exponential `f32` focused rows | Latest filtered Rust rows: `rand_distr standard-normal f32` about 454M and `exp1 f32` about 438M; historical full rows include `normal f32` about 450M and `exponential f32` about 441M | Fresh filtered Alea default rows show `standard-normal f32 scalar direct` about 595M and `standard-exponential f32 scalar direct` about 558M. Explicit opt-in native-f32 rows are faster in focused rows, about 663M standard-normal and 620M standard-exponential scalar direct, but intentionally use a separate output mapping. | Closed for focused standard f32 evidence. Defaults already exceed local Rust evidence; `StandardNormalNativeF32` / `StandardExponentialNativeF32` add a versioned opt-in fast f32 profile rather than replacing the stable f64-backed f32 defaults. |
| Standard normal/exponential `f32` bulk rows | Needed to distinguish scalar f32 gap from high-volume fill workflows | Added Alea `fillStandardNormal f32` / `fillStandardNormal f32 scalar direct` and `fillStandardExponential f32` / `fillStandardExponential f32 scalar direct` rows, then added matching FastPrng direct rows so facade, FastPrng direct, and ScalarPrng direct are separated. Before the vector8 chunk fill specialization, focused rows were about 376.0M facade / 382.6M FastPrng direct / 449.8M ScalarPrng direct for normal and 371.6M / 372.1M / 441.1M for exponential, versus focused Rust rows about 426.7M `standard-normal f32` and 432.0M `exp1 f32`. After adopting the f32-only vector8 chunk fill path, focused rows improve to about 400.0M / 400.9M / 475.7M for normal and about 382.3M / 381.3M / 467.1M for exponential, with matching checksums. Native-f32 opt-in scalar-direct fill rows in the same run are about 475M normal and 483M exponential but intentionally use a versioned output mapping. | Adopted for default f32 standard normal/exponential fills only. ScalarPrng direct f32 bulk now exceeds focused Rust scalar rows by a wider margin; FastPrng/facade f32 bulk remains below Rust but is narrowed, so the residual gap is engine/profile and fill-loop context rather than missing direct-source coverage or default f32 ziggurat algorithm alone. |
| Dedicated f32 ziggurat attempt | Tried f32-specific normal/exponential ziggurat paths using f32 table casts and u32-derived uniforms instead of f64 ziggurat plus cast | An early production-default attempt was rejected after an unfiltered full-suite run kept `standard-normal f32 scalar direct` around 433M samples/s and `standard-exponential f32 scalar direct` around 417M, with unrelated noisy regressions. A later isolated native-f32 probe using the same separate output-mapping idea was clearly faster, around 680M normal and 656M exponential, so the concept was adopted only as explicit `StandardNormalNativeF32` / `StandardExponentialNativeF32` opt-in APIs. Focused throughput rows for the opt-in profile are about 663M/620M scalar direct and 499M/488M fill. | Rejected as a production default, adopted as an explicit versioned f32 profile. Keep stable f32 defaults on the existing f64-backed mapping; use native-f32 APIs when callers explicitly prefer throughput over matching the default f32 output stream. |
| Table-bound f32 normal ziggurat candidate | The f64 `table-bound normal candidate` was competitive in one ziggurat probe, so checked whether casting it to f32 was a better f32 normal path than the existing f64-backed raw/cast default | Fresh `ziggurat-probe -- 4194304` shows `table-bound normal f32 cast candidate` about 523M samples/s versus standard f32 raw and ratio f32 cast around 596-597M, with matching checksum. | Rejected. Keep f32 normal routed through the existing f64-backed ziggurat/cast path. |
| Weighted dynamic update/push/pop/sample | `rand_distr weighted tree`: about 52M ops/s for integer weights; focused `rand_distr weighted tree f64 update+sample` about 42.7M ops/s | `alea weighted int tree`: about 52M ops/s; `WeightedIntTree.sampleFrom` about 52M ops/s; generic `WeightedTree(f64)` focused rows are about 45.7M facade update+sample, 45.1M direct update+sample, and 70.6M direct-source fill. `WeightedTree` and `WeightedIntTree` expose update, push, pop, sample, and fill APIs with root/empty/error-path coverage | Closed for unsigned integer and f64 weights. Integer weights match local Rust evidence via `WeightedIntTree`, and generic float weights now exceed matching local Rust f64 evidence in focused update+sample rows. |
| Uniform/open/range float comparison | New local Rust rows: `random::<f32>` about 758M, `Open01 f32` about 773M, `OpenClosed01 f32` about 756M, `float range f32` about 726M; latest local `f64` Rust rows are `random::<f64>` about 855M, `Open01 f64` about 852M, and `OpenClosed01 f64` about 778M in the latest focused rerun, `float range f64` about 801M | Added matching Alea scalar rows. S4-M1223 corrected ordinary f64 StandardUniform to the local Rust 53-bit `n / 2^53` grid across facade/direct/bulk/vector paths. S4-M1224 then recovered throughput with a split bitcast-plus-half-ULP expression; S4-M1225 switches the remaining low half-ULP contribution to a bitcast, widens ordinary f64 fill/range chunks to 8 lanes, and adds scalar stream-shape coverage. Fresh focused same-host S4-M1225 rows are about 778M scalar facade / 786M scalar direct, 804M fill facade/direct, 769M fillRange facade / 771M direct, and 893M `fillVector f64x4` lanes/s. Strict `Open01` and `(0, 1]` f64 rows keep their endpoint-specific paths. | Correctness is aligned for ordinary f64 StandardUniform, and a substantial amount of pre-S4-M1223 throughput is recovered without changing the grid. Keep future f64 uniform optimizations semantics-preserving; local Rust scalar/range parity remains a possible follow-up depending on fresh same-host Rust rows. |
| Direct-source `fillFrom` / `fillRangeFrom` | Rust float rows are monomorphized over `SmallRng`; Alea needed direct-source bulk rows to distinguish facade overhead from core generation cost | Added `Rng.fillFrom(source, T, dest)` and `Rng.fillRangeFrom(source, T, dest, min, max)` for scalar/vector bulk workflows. Local rows showed `fill f64 direct` and `fillRange f64 direct` essentially matched facade rows, confirming the old f64 gap was not facade dispatch. | Adopted for API consistency and direct-engine workflows. The follow-up f64 bitcast uniform path closed ordinary/ranged f64 bulk gaps; open/open-closed f64 remain separate semantics work. |
| Direct-source ergonomic helper sweep | Stage 4 direct-engine workflows still had several facade-only convenience APIs after bulk fill/range support landed | Added direct-source `From` helpers across structured values/iterators, scalar and vector checked draws/fills, durations, `Rng` collection helpers, sequence collection sampling, weighted no-replacement sampling, fixed and checked index arrays, checked choice/choice-pointer/enum/value helpers, reusable choice iterators/convenience iterators, ASCII/Unicode string generation, checked charset helpers, top-level Bernoulli/normal/exponential/Poisson helpers including the large-lambda Ahrens-Dieter path, checked top-level discrete/continuous/rank helpers, and root `hash` constructors. Unit tests and API-reference drift checks cover the new symbols; performance-sensitive rows remain on the existing benchmarked kernels rather than claiming new speed wins. | Adopted for API consistency and comptime-known engine ergonomics; this is a surface-completeness follow-up, not a separate performance claim. |
| Charset diagnostics | `Charset` retained its source byte slice but did not expose it, empty checks, or safe single-byte lookup for configuration echo/debugging | Added `bytesValue`, `len`, `isEmpty`, and checked `byteAt`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable string-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Charset membership diagnostics | Charset users often need to check whether a byte belongs to the configured reusable sampler without sampling | Added `indexOf` and `contains`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable string-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Charset probability diagnostics | `Charset` implies uniform byte probabilities but did not expose single or bulk probability diagnostics for configuration echo/debugging | Added `probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, and `probabilitiesInto`, with bounds, length, and empty-charset unit coverage. Single-byte charsets now return/fill the only byte without consuming randomness. | Adopted for reusable string-sampler diagnostics and deterministic point-mass string generation. |
| `Uniform(T)` reusable sampler fill APIs | `Uniform(T)` had `sampleFrom` but no reusable `fill` / `fillFrom`, while direct range fill paths already had SIMD/bulk optimizations | Added `Uniform(T).fill` / `fillFrom`; non-inclusive bulk fill reuses `Rng.fillRangeFrom` so it inherits optimized range generation. Local row: `Uniform(f64).fillFrom direct` about 825M samples/s, matching `fillRange f64 direct`. | Adopted for API consistency and high-throughput reusable uniform range workflows. |
| Floating-point inclusive `Uniform(T)` semantics | `uniformInclusive` / `Uniform(T).initInclusive` for floats reused half-open `floatRangeFrom`, so it could not produce the upper endpoint despite the inclusive API name | Floating-point inclusive uniform now uses a closed `[0, 1]` unit grid and tests force raw-min/raw-max engines to return the exact lower and upper endpoints. Non-inclusive `Uniform(f64).fillFrom` still uses the optimized `fillRangeFrom` path at about 828M samples/s. | Adopted as a semantics fix aligned with Rust `Uniform::new_inclusive` expectations; closed-interval float stream mapping is versioned-stable rather than scalar `floatRange` compatible. |
| Floating-point `Uniform(T)` constructor validation | Float `Uniform(T).init` / `initInclusive` checked ordering but did not explicitly reject non-finite endpoints | Float uniform constructors now reject NaN/inf endpoints through the range predicates, matching `Rng.floatRangeChecked` and Rust `UniformFloat` expectations. | Adopted as a semantics and input-validation fix; integer range behavior is unchanged. |
| Degenerate range/uniform sampling | Float ranges and one-value integer ranges can be valid point masses, but several helper paths still evaluated random draws or looped per element before returning the deterministic endpoint | `Rng.floatRange*`, scalar/vector `fillRange*` point masses, scalar/vector integer ranges with exactly one exclusive value, inclusive integer ranges/durations, and distribution `uniform*` / reusable `Uniform(T)` / inclusive scalar/vector uniform helpers now return or fill the single endpoint immediately when the range collapses. Focused no-consume tests cover scalar, vector, checked, fill, distribution, and duration paths. | Adopted as an edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid point-mass ranges intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate vector probability fills | Vector chance/ratio fills with p=0/1 or numerator 0/denominator are valid point masses, but the bulk helpers still looped over each output vector before delegating to scalar no-consume vector sampling | `Rng.fillVectorChanceFrom` and `Rng.fillVectorRatioFrom` now fill all-false/all-true vector slices directly for deterministic probabilities, with checked facade coverage. | Adopted as an edge-case throughput win; deterministic valid probability fills intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate discrete distribution sampling | Several discrete distributions have valid point-mass parameter sets, but some reusable/bulk/vector wrappers still looped through the sampler even when the outcome was deterministic | Added no-consume fast paths and focused tests for scalar/vector Bernoulli, Binomial, BinomialPoissonApprox, Poisson, NegativeBinomial, Hypergeometric, Geometric, GeometricFailures, and finite/infinite degenerate Zipf and infinite-exponent Zeta cases. Hypergeometric now records a constant method when `minValue == maxValue`, and fill/sample wrappers return or fill that constant without consuming randomness. | Adopted as an edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid discrete distributions intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate multivariate sampling | One-category, zero-trial, and single-positive Multinomial cases plus one-dimensional Dirichlet and single-infinite-alpha Dirichlet parameter sets are valid point masses, but their sample-into paths previously reached generic binomial/gamma or per-sample batch machinery before producing deterministic outputs | `Multinomial.sampleIntoFrom` now fills the sole/constant category or zero counts directly, and `Dirichlet(T).sampleIntoFrom` now fills one-dimensional simplexes or the finite-dimensional vertex selected by exactly one infinite alpha; their batch paths fill flat deterministic outputs directly, so direct/facade checked single-sample and batch sample-into wrappers inherit deterministic no-consume behavior. Focused tests cover `sampleIntoFrom`, `sampleIntoChecked`, `sampleManyIntoFrom`, checked batch wrappers, and Dirichlet vertex diagnostics for both samplers. | Adopted as an edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid multivariate point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Normal and LogNormal coefficient-of-variation constructors | Local `rand_distr` exposes mean/CV constructors for Normal and LogNormal, while Alea users previously had to hand-convert these parameterizations | Added Zig-native `Normal(T).initMeanCv` and `LogNormal(T).initMeanCv`. Normal uses local `rand_distr`-compatible `mean * cv` as the stored standard deviation, including unrestricted/non-finite mean edge cases; LogNormal converts linear-space mean/CV to log-space `mu`/`sigma` with `log1p(cv^2)` and mirrors the local zero-CV branch ordering, including the degenerate `mean=0, cv=0` case. Unit coverage checks valid construction, positive log-normal output, the zero-degenerate log-normal state, and invalid CV/mean cases; `distcheck` now includes a `log-normal-mean-cv` mean gate. | Adopted for constructor ergonomics and parity-plus parameterization; this is not a throughput change. |
| Normal coefficient-of-variation accessor | `Normal(T).initMeanCv` accepts CV parameters, but reusable samplers only exposed mean/stddev after construction | Added `coefficientOfVariationValue`, returning `null` for `mean == 0` because CV is undefined/not recoverable there, with unit coverage. | Adopted for constructor/sampler parameter diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Degenerate Normal/LogNormal sampling | Local `rand_distr 0.6.0` accepts finite zero standard deviations but still samples `StandardNormal` through `Normal.sample`; Alea can use Zig-native deterministic stream semantics for point-mass distributions | Scalar, vector, exact LogNormal, and bounded-approx f32 LogNormal helpers now return/fill `mean` or `exp(mean)`/the approximation transform immediately when `stddev == 0`. Focused no-consume tests cover checked/unchecked top-level helpers, `Rng` facade helpers, scalar/vector bulk fills, and reusable `Normal` / `LogNormal` / approximation samplers. | Adopted as an edge-case semantics and throughput win for point-mass distributions; this intentionally avoids consuming randomness for deterministic valid inputs. |
| Degenerate Exponential sampling | Exponential rate controls mean width; `rate == inf` is a deterministic boundary point mass at `0`, while previous checked paths rejected it and unchecked/reusable paths would otherwise draw a standard exponential before dividing by infinity | `Rng`, distribution namespace, scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept infinite rate, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed `maxValue() == 0` with zero mean/variance. | Degenerate Gamma/Erlang sampling | Gamma and Erlang scale controls all support width, so positive finite shape with `scale == 0` is a valid deterministic point mass at `0`, while local `rand_distr` also accepts Gamma `shape == inf` or `scale == inf` as an infinity-valued edge state | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose `maxValue() == 0` for collapsed reusable samplers; Gamma additionally accepts infinite shape/scale and returns or fills `inf` without consuming randomness. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including shape-one Gamma, infinite Gamma, and Erlang. | Adopted as parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput wins; deterministic valid Gamma/Erlang point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate ChiSquared/Chi sampling | Central ChiSquared and Chi with zero degrees of freedom are deterministic point masses at `0`, and local `rand_distr::ChiSquared::new` accepts infinite dof through Gamma's infinite-shape path | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `dof == 0`, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed `maxValue() == 0` with zero mean/variance; ChiSquared and Chi additionally accept infinite dof and return or fill `inf` without consuming randomness. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors for both distributions. | Adopted as parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput wins; deterministic valid ChiSquared/Chi point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| FisherF infinite-dof sampling | Local `rand_distr::FisherF::new` accepts one-sided and both-sided infinite degrees through ChiSquared/Gamma composition; samples are NaN while consuming the underlying draw shape. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept infinite d1/d2 combinations, produce NaN, and preserve the corresponding Gamma/ChiSquared stream shape. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, facade/direct-source paths, and invalid no-consume cases. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility; this supersedes the previous Alea-only deterministic both-infinite point-mass extension. |
| Beta infinite-shape sampling | Local `rand_distr::Beta::new` accepts one-sided and both-sided infinite shape parameters; Cheng-algorithm sampling may return endpoints or NaN depending on the finite counterpart while consuming Open01 draws. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept one-sided and both-sided infinite shapes, route through a rand_distr-compatible infinite-parameter Cheng branch, and preserve the corresponding endpoint/NaN output and stream shape. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, facade/direct-source paths, and invalid no-consume cases. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility; this supersedes the previous Alea-only no-consume one-sided point-mass extension and both-infinite rejection. |
| Degenerate LogLogistic sampling | LogLogistic scale controls the positive-support width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `0`; `shape == inf` is also a deterministic limit at the scale/median. Previous constructors rejected these cases or ratio/generic paths could draw strict-open uniforms before collapsing. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0` and infinite shape, return or fill the deterministic endpoint without consuming randomness, expose collapsed support bounds, and report finite point-mass mean/variance. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including shape=1 and infinite shape. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid LogLogistic point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Frechet sampling | Frechet scale controls the positive tail width above `location`, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `location`; `shape == inf` is also a deterministic limit at `location + scale`. Previous constructors rejected these cases or transform paths could draw open-closed uniforms before collapsing. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0` and infinite shape, return or fill the deterministic endpoint without consuming randomness, expose collapsed upper support, and report finite point-mass mean/variance even when nearby non-degenerate shapes would have undefined moments. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including shape=1 and infinite shape. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Frechet point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Pareto sampling | Pareto scale is the lower support bound and multiplicative width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `0`; `shape == inf` is also a deterministic lower-bound limit at `scale`. Previous constructors rejected these cases or transform paths could draw strict-open uniforms before collapsing. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0` and infinite shape, return or fill the deterministic endpoint without consuming randomness, expose collapsed upper support, and report finite point-mass mean/variance even when nearby non-degenerate shapes would have undefined moments. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including shape=1 and infinite shape. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Pareto point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| PERT collapsed/infinite-shape sampling | Local `rand_distr` rejects collapsed ranges but Alea keeps collapsed PERT as a no-consume point-mass extension; local `rand_distr` accepts finite-range `shape == inf` only for interior mode and samples via Beta `(inf, inf)`, producing NaN. | Collapsed PERT remains deterministic and no-consume. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, builder, direct-source, invalid endpoint/mean, and collapsed no-consume paths. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility while retaining the documented Alea collapsed-range extension. |
| InverseGaussian zero-mean and infinity semantics | InverseGaussian is positive-supported with mean as its location-scale-like point target; Alea keeps `mean == 0` as a deterministic boundary point mass, while local `rand_distr::InverseGaussian::new` accepts positive infinite mean/shape and samples them through the ordinary StandardNormal plus uniform composition, producing NaN. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers retain the `mean == 0` no-consume point mass, but positive infinite mean and/or shape now consume the local `rand_distr` draw shape and produce NaN instead of treating finite-mean `shape == inf` as a no-consume point mass. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, facade/direct-source paths, accessors, invalid NaN rejection, and stream consumption. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility while retaining the documented Alea zero-mean extension; this is an edge-semantics fix rather than a throughput change. |

Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/reusable rejection, invalid no-consume behavior, and large finite-alpha construction. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility; this removes the former Alea-only alpha-infinity point-mass extension. |
| Degenerate SkewNormal sampling | SkewNormal scale controls all support width, so finite location/shape with `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `location`, while the previous constructors rejected it and scalar/vector paths would otherwise draw one or two normals before multiplying by zero | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `location` without consuming randomness, expose collapsed support bounds, and report zero variance. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including symmetric and skewed shapes. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid SkewNormal point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| SkewNormal unrestricted-location compatibility | Local `rand_distr::SkewNormal::new` treats `location` as unrestricted while keeping scale finite positive and shape finite; samples with non-finite locations propagate `+inf`, `-inf`, or `NaN` after consuming the ordinary normal draw shape. | Scalar/vector checked, unchecked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept unrestricted locations and preserve finite-location draw shape; Alea still retains its zero-scale point-mass extension. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, facade/direct-source paths, accessors, invalid scale/shape rejection, and stream consumption. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility while retaining the documented Alea zero-scale extension; this is an edge-semantics fix rather than a throughput change. |
| Degenerate HalfNormal sampling | HalfNormal is `abs(Normal(0, scale))`, so `scale == 0` is a valid deterministic point mass even though the previous constructors rejected it | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose `maxValue() == 0` for the collapsed reusable samplers. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid half-normal point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Rayleigh sampling | Rayleigh scale controls all support width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass but previous constructors rejected it and the log/sqrt transform would otherwise draw strict-open uniforms | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose `maxValue() == 0` for the collapsed reusable samplers. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Rayleigh point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Weibull sampling | Weibull scale controls support width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `0`; `shape == inf` is also a deterministic limit at `scale`. Previous constructors rejected these cases or generic/shape=1 paths could consume randomness before collapsing. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0` and infinite shape, return or fill the deterministic endpoint without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed support bounds and zero variance for reusable samplers. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors, including shape=1 and infinite shape. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Weibull point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Maxwell sampling | Maxwell scale controls all three normal components, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass but previous constructors rejected it and scalar/vector paths would otherwise draw normals | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `0` without consuming randomness, and expose `maxValue() == 0` for the collapsed reusable samplers. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Maxwell point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Laplace/Logistic sampling | Laplace and Logistic scale controls the distribution width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at `location`, but previous constructors rejected it and scalar/vector paths would otherwise draw strict-open uniforms | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill `location` without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed support bounds on reusable samplers. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors for both distributions. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid location-scale point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Cauchy/Gumbel sampling | Cauchy and Gumbel scale controls distribution width, so `scale == 0` is a deterministic point mass at median/location, but previous constructors rejected it and scalar/vector paths would otherwise draw strict/open-closed uniforms | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `scale == 0`, return or fill the median/location without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed support bounds on reusable samplers; Cauchy also reports finite expected value and zero variance only for the collapsed case. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus support/moment accessors for both distributions. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid location-scale point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Triangular sampling | Local `rand_distr` accepts collapsed `min == max == mode` triangular distributions, but the generic inverse-CDF sample path still draws a uniform before returning the only possible value | `Triangular(T).init(point, point, point)` and scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept the collapsed support and return or fill `point` without consuming randomness. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus moment/median accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid triangular point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Arcsine sampling | Alea's bounded Arcsine sampler is part of the broader continuous distribution set; collapsed `min == max` support is a deterministic point mass but previously required `min < max` and would otherwise draw strict-open uniforms before transforming | `Arcsine(T).init(point, point)` and scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept the collapsed support and return or fill `point` without consuming randomness. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus moment/median accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid bounded point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate PowerFunction sampling | `PowerFunction(min, max, shape)` is bounded support; collapsed `min == max` and the `shape == inf` upper-endpoint limit are deterministic, but shape-specialized and generic paths could consume randomness before producing the only possible value | `PowerFunction(T).init(point, point, shape)` and `PowerFunction(T).init(min, max, inf)` plus scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now return or fill the deterministic endpoint without consuming randomness, including shape=1, shape=2, and infinite-shape method tags. Focused tests cover scalar and vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus moment/median accessors. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid bounded point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Degenerate Kumaraswamy sampling | Kumaraswamy has deterministic infinite-shape boundary limits: `alpha == inf` with finite positive beta collapses to `1`, and finite positive alpha with `beta == inf` collapses to `0`; both infinite shapes remain invalid because the limit depends on approach ratio. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept exactly one infinite shape, return or fill the endpoint without consuming randomness, and expose collapsed support/moment/mode/median accessors. Focused tests cover scalar/vector no-consume sample/fill paths plus reusable sampler accessors and the both-infinite invalid path. | Adopted as a parity-plus edge-case semantics and throughput win; deterministic valid Kumaraswamy point masses intentionally do not consume randomness. |
| Normal and LogNormal z-score helpers | Local `rand_distr` exposes z-score conversion helpers for correlated normal/log-normal draws | Added `Normal(T).fromZScore`, `meanValue`, `stddevValue`, plus `LogNormal(T).fromZScore`, `logMean`, and `logStddev`. Unit coverage checks affine normal conversion and log-normal z-score/log-space accessors. | Adopted for reusable-sampler ergonomics and correlated-sample workflows; this is not a throughput change. |
| LogNormal value-named accessors | `LogNormal(T)` exposed `logMean` / `logStddev`, while newer reusable samplers use `*Value` names for parameter diagnostics | Added `logMeanValue` and `logStddevValue` as naming-consistent aliases, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection consistency; this is not a throughput change. |
| LogNormal linear-space accessors | `LogNormal(T).initMeanCv` accepts linear-space mean/CV parameters, but reusable samplers only exposed log-space parameters after construction | Added `linearMeanValue`, `medianValue`, `modeValue`, and `coefficientOfVariationValue`, including the degenerate zero-mean/zero-CV and positive deterministic cases, with unit coverage. | Adopted for constructor/sampler parameter round-trip diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| LogNormal moment accessors | `LogNormal(T)` exposed linear-space mean/CV diagnostics but not the naming-consistent theoretical `expectedValue` / `varianceValue` or support-bound accessors used by most other reusable samplers | Added `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, including zero-degenerate and positive deterministic coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection consistency; this is not a throughput change. |
| Exponential parameter/moment accessors | `Exponential(T)` caches inverse rate for sampling efficiency, leaving users without a direct way to inspect the original rate parameter, moments, median/mode, or support bounds | Added `Exponential(T).rateValue`, `inverseRateValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `medianValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, plus matching fixed standard-exponential accessors, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler diagnostics and parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| `Open01` / `OpenClosed01` reusable sampler fill APIs | Strict-interval reusable samplers had direct single-sample methods but no bulk fill methods | Added `Open01.fill` / `fillFrom` and `OpenClosed01.fill` / `fillFrom`, reusing `Rng.fillOpenFrom` and `fillOpenClosedFrom`. Local rows remain on the existing fast paths: `fillOpen f64` about 883M samples/s, and the main throughput harness now includes facade, FastPrng-direct, and ScalarPrng-direct `fillOpenClosed f64` rows, with latest focused rows about 771M/775M/773M on 1GiB versus Rust about 778M. | Adopted for API consistency and strict-interval bulk workflows; `fillOpenClosed f64` is now at the latest focused Rust boundary but stays precision-sensitive because it preserves exact `(0, 1]` semantics. |
| Strict-interval sampler accessors | `Open01` and `OpenClosed01` encode fixed endpoint inclusion semantics but did not expose them for diagnostics | Added `lowValue`, `highValue`, `includesLow`, and `includesHigh` to both reusable samplers, with unit coverage. | Adopted for strict-interval sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Strict-interval moment accessors | `Open01` and `OpenClosed01` encode continuous uniform `(0,1)` / `(0,1]` semantics but did not expose their theoretical mean/variance | Added `expectedValue` and `varianceValue` to both reusable samplers, with unit coverage. | Adopted for strict-interval sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Scalar engine choice | Rust `SmallRng` is the baseline scalar engine | `wyhash64 next`: about 2515-2530M next/s; `normal` on `wyhash64`: about 426-427M samples/s; `fillNormal` on `wyhash64`: about 393-397M samples/s; `exponential` on `wyhash64`: about 422-426M samples/s; `fillExponential` on `wyhash64`: about 400-405M samples/s; `poisson` on `wyhash64`: about 76M samples/s | Use `ScalarPrng` for scalar-heavy distribution workloads; `FastPrng` remains best for bulk byte fill |
| Rust scalar engine throughput | Local Rust rows previously lacked a raw `SmallRng.next_u64` baseline for interpreting normal/exponential gaps | Added `rand SmallRng next_u64`: about 1130M next/s. | Ziggurat branch-frequency audit | Normal/exponential performance work needed evidence on whether rare rejection/tail branches dominate | Added `zig build ziggurat-stats`. | Ziggurat fast-path branch hints | `ziggurat-stats` showed the fast path dominates, but the hot branch had no explicit hint | Added `@branchHint(.likely)` to normal/exponential fast-path returns and `@branchHint(.unlikely)` to tail branches. Local rows: `normal wyhash64 direct` improved from about 415-418M to about 440M samples/s; `standard-normal scalar direct` from about 414-428M to about 439M; `fillStandardNormal scalar direct` from about 350-354M to about 400M; `skew-normal scalar direct` from about 218M to about 255M. Exponential direct rows stayed roughly in the 414-420M range. | Adopted. This narrows normal-derived gaps but standard normal and exponential still trail Rust `rand_distr`. |
| Ziggurat ratio-threshold fast path and standard raw helpers | Full benchmark runs are noisy enough that small ziggurat expression changes are hard to evaluate, and standard distributions were still routed through generic mean/rate wrappers | Added `zig build ziggurat-probe`, precomputed fast-path ratio tables, `Rng.standardNormalFastFrom`, and `Rng.standardExponentialFastFrom`; `StandardNormal` / `StandardExponential` now use raw standard helpers. Probe rows: normal generic about 561M vs standard/raw ratio about 595M, exponential generic about 546M vs standard/raw ratio about 553M. Full bench rows: `standard-normal scalar direct` improved to about 449M and f32 to about 439M; exponential was roughly neutral/noisy around 420M. | Adopted. Normal-derived rows get a small measurable win; exponential remains watch because full benchmark improvement is not durable yet. |
| Ziggurat probe rerun after API sweep | Rechecked the remaining normal/exponential hot-path candidates before attempting more production changes | A fresh `zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dcpu=native ziggurat-probe -- 4194304` still favors the current ratio-threshold normal path: standard/raw normal about 654M samples/s, inline ratio normal about 656M, mantissa-threshold normal about 580M, mantissa-range normal about 631M, and table-bound normal about 626M. Exponential still favors the adopted mantissa-threshold path at about 615M versus raw/generic about 609-611M and table-bound about 562M. Vector-repair candidates remain lower at about 452M normal f64x4 lanes/s and 438M exponential f64x4 lanes/s. | No production change. This confirms the earlier rejected ziggurat expression shapes remain slower on the current native build. |
| Full-benchmark raw standard normal/exponential rows | Needed to check whether reusable sampler wrapper dispatch explained the gap between ziggurat-probe hot-path rows and full benchmark rows | Added `alea standard-normal raw scalar direct` and `alea standard-exponential raw scalar direct` rows that call `Rng.standardNormalFastFrom` / `standardExponentialFastFrom` directly in the same full-benchmark harness. After fixing `bench -- [bytes] [filter]` argument parsing, focused filtered runs show raw rows still match reusable sampler rows but run much faster than the all-row suite: default filtered `bench -- "standard-normal"` reports about 623M raw vs 596M sampler, default filtered `bench -- "standard-exponential"` about 553M raw vs 521M sampler, and equal-sample filtered `bench -- 268435456 "standard-normal"` / `"standard-exponential"` reports about 627M/566M raw vs 614M/569M sampler. Filtered Rust comparison rows are about 407-466M for `standard-normal` and about 423-443M for `exp1` in the same focused mode. | Gamma scalar profile | `rand_distr gamma`: about 125M samples/s in the latest native run | `alea gamma`: about 137M with `FastPrng`; about 158M with cached `Gamma.sampleFrom(ScalarPrng)` | Closed for current local Rust evidence |
| Gamma shape=1 exponential fast path | `Gamma(1, scale)` is exactly exponential with mean `scale`, but Alea previously routed it through the general Marsaglia gamma path | Added `zig build gamma-shape-probe` and routed `gammaFrom` / `Gamma.sampleFrom` through `standardExponential * scale` for `shape == 1`. | Gamma shape=0.5 normal-square fast path | `Gamma(0.5, scale)` is equivalent to `0.5 * scale * N(0,1)^2`, but the generic boosted Gamma path is slow for this common half-shape | Extended `gamma-shape-probe` and routed `gammaFrom` / `fillGammaFrom` for `shape == 0.5` through the normal-square form while leaving the reusable `Gamma(T).sampleFrom` Marsaglia hot path intact for ordinary shapes. | Gamma bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillGamma`, `fillGammaFrom`, and `Gamma.fill` / `fillFrom` as direct named bulk APIs. | Gamma parameter/moment accessors | `Gamma(T)` caches derived Marsaglia constants but did not expose the original shape/scale parameters, moments, mode, or support bounds for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Gamma(T).shapeValue`, `scaleValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Direct reusable bulk fill | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | `Rng.fillSample` / `fillSampleFrom` now dispatch to a sampler's bulk `fill` / `fillFrom` methods when their signatures match the requested output type, while preserving scalar-loop fallback for samplers without typed bulk methods. Gamma remains on its existing bulk path, about 142-144M facade and about 165M ScalarPrng direct in fresh focused rows. Strict-interval reusable samplers now also inherit their specialized bulk paths through `fillSample`: `fillSample Open01 f64` is about 881M samples/s and `fillSample OpenClosed01 f64` is about 772M, matching the named `fillOpen` / `fillOpenClosed` rows instead of degrading to repeated scalar sampling. | Adopted for direct bulk reusable-sampler workflows; generic `fillSample` now gets the same allocation-free high-throughput behavior as named sampler fills where available. |
| Reusable sampler iterator bulk fill | `sampleIter(...).fill(dest)` still used repeated `nextValue()` calls even after `Rng.fillSample` learned to delegate to sampler bulk fills | `SampleIterator.fill` now delegates to `Rng.fillSample`, and direct-source `SampleIteratorFrom.fill` delegates to `Rng.fillSampleFrom`. Packed bulk policies for `bool`, `f32`, `u8`, and sub-64-bit integers intentionally stay on the scalar fallback so `fill` matches repeated `nextValue()`. Focused rows show `valueIter f64 fill` and `valueIterFrom f64 fill` about 872M samples/s, matching the named `fill f64` rows. | Adopted for iterator ergonomics and high-volume value workflows without changing scalar iterator stream shape; structured tuple/array iterator fills also preserve scalar `value` order. |

| Multivariate allocation-returning direct source workflows | Allocation-returning multivariate samples had facade `sample(allocator, rng)` but no direct-source convenience wrapper | Added `Multinomial.sampleFrom(allocator, source)` and `Dirichlet(T).sampleFrom(allocator, source)`, both delegating to the existing allocation-free direct-source sample-into paths after allocation. | Adopted for API consistency; high-volume callers should still prefer allocation-free `sampleIntoFrom` / `sampleManyIntoFrom`. |

Local rows: multinomial many direct about 1.1M samples/s, matching direct single-sample; Dirichlet many direct about 51M vs direct about 46M. | Adopted for allocation-free batch workflows; benchmark evidence distinguishes ergonomics from large speed claims. |

The throughput harness separates single-sample facade/FastPrng-direct/ScalarPrng-direct and bulk facade/FastPrng-direct/ScalarPrng-direct rows: latest filtered rows are about 76.6M facade, 76.7M FastPrng direct, 84.1M ScalarPrng direct, 74.9M facade fill, 75.1M FastPrng direct fill, and 73.8M ScalarPrng direct fill versus filtered `rand_distr cauchy` about 69.1M. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free bulk workflows; Cauchy single-sample and all bulk rows now exceed filtered local Rust evidence while preserving strict-open draw shape. |

Fill rows remain about 76M FastPrng and 75M ScalarPrng. | Adopted the `-cos/sin` single-sample expression. Keep staged vector strict-open conversion for bulk fills; Rust-shaped remains rejected because it changes stream/endpoint shape. |

Keep Cauchy wrappers normal; remaining FastPrng single-sample gap is engine/profile plus transcendental transform cost, not wrapper inlining. |

A faster `scale / Exp(1)` candidate changes the random stream shape, while reciprocal-log preserves the existing open-closed uniform transform | Extended `frechet-probe` and routed `fillFrechetFrom(shape == 1)` through vector reciprocal-log conversion after filling open-closed uniforms. Local fill rows improved from about 40M to 193M for both FastPrng and ScalarPrng, with matching checksums versus the existing transform. The faster reciprocal-exponential candidate was rejected for now because it changes stream shape; ordinary `Frechet(shape=3)` fill stayed in range at about 47M. | Adopted narrowly for parameterized bulk fill. Keep single-sample Frechet and reciprocal-exponential variants out of default paths until stream-shape/reproducibility tradeoffs are explicitly accepted. |
| PERT bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillPert`, `fillPertFrom`, and `Pert.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. Local rows: facade fill about 67M samples/s and direct-source fill about 77M, above scalar facade repeated sampling around 70M in this run. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free bulk workflows; direct-source fill is a useful high-volume path. |
| PERT range-builder ergonomics | Local `rand_distr::Pert` uses a `new(min, max).with_shape(...).with_mean/with_mode(...)` builder workflow, while Alea only exposed direct constructors | Added Zig-native `Pert(T).initRange(min, max).withShape(shape).withMode(mode)` / `.withMean(mean)` builder-style construction, with unit coverage matching the direct `init` / `initMean` parameterization. | Adopted for reusable-sampler ergonomics and parity-plus constructor workflows; this is not a throughput change. |
| PERT parameter accessors | `Pert(T)` stores derived `min`/`range`/`alpha`/`beta` values but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `minValue`, `maxValue`, `shapeValue`, `modeValue`, `alphaValue`, and `betaValue`, with unit coverage. `modeValue` returns `null` for the `shape == 0` uniform-equivalent case because the original mode is not recoverable from the stored `alpha=beta=1` parameters. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| PERT moment accessors | `Pert(T)` exposed builder and parameter diagnostics but not the beta-backed theoretical mean/variance | Added `expectedValue` and `varianceValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| PERT builder parameter accessors | `PertBuilder(T)` carries range/shape state across builder calls but did not expose it for diagnostics before final sampler construction | Added `minValue`, `maxValue`, and `shapeValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for builder workflow introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| PERT shape=0 uniform-equivalent audit | `PERT(min, mode, max, shape=0)` reduces to `Uniform(min, max)`, so checked whether it needed a dedicated path beyond the `Beta(1,1)` specialization | Added `zig build pert-probe`. Single-sample rows already match uniform: about 951M FastPrng and 1250M ScalarPrng. Bulk fill is mixed: uniform fill is faster for FastPrng, about 834M versus current 671M, but slower for ScalarPrng, about 685M versus current 819M. Checksums match. | No production change for now. The current Beta-backed fill remains the better generic default because `Rng`/direct-source callers may use ScalarPrng; revisit only with engine-aware or no-regression fill dispatch. |
| Standard normal/exponential reusable samplers | Rust exposes `StandardNormal` / `Exp1` fast-path distribution types and local rows are about 466M / 452M samples/s | `StandardNormal(T)` / `StandardExponential(T)` added; after raw standard helpers, ratio-threshold normal, and mantissa-threshold exponential fast paths, focused filtered scalar direct rows are about 638M standard normal, about 597M f32 standard normal, about 568M standard exponential, and about 566M f32 standard exponential on the current Linux host | Adopted for feature parity and scalar-fast workflows; focused rows now exceed local Rust evidence, while unfiltered full-suite context remains a benchmark-harness watch |
| Standard normal/exponential bulk fill APIs | Rust exposes zero-parameter distribution types and users often fill repeated standard samples | Added `fillStandardNormal`, `fillStandardNormalFrom`, `fillStandardExponential`, `fillStandardExponentialFrom`, and sampler `fill` / `fillFrom` methods. After the exponential mantissa-threshold fast path, `standard-fill-probe` showed the fill helpers match direct loops in isolation around 466M standard normal and 442M standard exponential, and now also tracks vector-chunk candidates. The full throughput harness separates facade/FastPrng-direct/ScalarPrng-direct fill rows: after the f32-only vector chunk specialization, focused rows are about 380.5M/405.3M/490.1M for f64 standard normal, 376.1M/373.4M/444.6M for f64 standard exponential, 400.0M/400.9M/475.7M for f32 standard normal, and 382.3M/381.3M/467.1M for f32 standard exponential. | Adopted for explicit zero-parameter bulk workflows and clearer facade-vs-engine evidence. Keep f64 production fill helpers on scalar loops because the broad vector4 production retry was mixed in the main harness; f32 uses the vector8 chunk path because it preserves checksums and improves focused rows. |
| Standard normal/exponential parameter accessors | Fixed-parameter standard samplers encode `mean=0,stddev=1` and `rate=1`, but did not expose those semantics for diagnostics | Added `StandardNormal(T).meanValue` / `stddevValue` and `StandardExponential(T).rateValue` / `inverseRateValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for fixed-parameter reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Normal moment accessors | `StandardNormal(T)` and `Normal(T)` exposed mean/stddev parameters but not the naming-consistent theoretical `expectedValue` / `varianceValue`, median/mode, or support-bound accessors used by most other reusable samplers | Added `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `medianValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue` to both samplers, including degenerate `stddev == 0` support bounds for `Normal(T)`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection consistency; this is not a throughput change. |
| Route standard normal/exponential fill through `Rng` raw standard helpers | Tried adding `Rng.fillStandardNormalFrom` / `fillStandardExponentialFrom` and routing distribution-layer standard fills through those helpers | Rejected after benchmark: `fillStandardNormal scalar direct` dropped from the recent 400M range to about 289M samples/s; exponential was roughly neutral around 369M. | Rejected. Keep the distribution-layer fill loops calling `standardNormalFrom` / `standardExponentialFrom` directly until a better bulk strategy is found. |
| Standard fill vector-chunk candidate | Tested whether filling scalar slices via vector-sized chunks, while preserving the same scalar ziggurat lane order and checksums, improves standard normal/exponential bulk throughput | Extended `standard-fill-probe` with f64 vector4 and f32 vector8 chunk rows. Initial probe rows showed matching checksums and candidate wins, but a broad f64+f32 production retry was mixed in the main throughput harness and regressed f64 scalar-direct context, so production was narrowed to f32 only. Focused f32-only production rows improve `fillStandardNormal f32` from about 376.0M/382.6M/449.8M facade/FastPrng/ScalarPrng direct to about 400.0M/400.9M/475.7M, and `fillStandardExponential f32` from about 371.6M/372.1M/441.1M to about 382.3M/381.3M/467.1M, all with unchanged checksums. | Adopted only for default f32 standard normal/exponential fills. Rejected for f64 production until a no-regression main-harness variant appears. |
| Cached-`Rng` f32 standard fill probe | Checked whether caching the `Rng` facade `ptr` and `nextFn` fields before the f32 vector-chunk standard normal/exponential fill loop reduces the remaining FastPrng/facade f32 standard-fill gap | Extended `standard-fill-probe` with cached-`Rng` facade rows. Focused `standard-fill-probe -- 16777216` shows current/cached f32 standard-exponential facade both about 392.1M and current/cached f32 standard-normal about 412.4M/412.2M, with matching checksums. | Rejected. The remaining facade f32 standard-fill boundary is not caused by repeatedly loading the `Rng` function-pointer fields; keep the simpler facade route. |
| `Rng.fillNormal(f32, mean=0, stddev=1)` vector-chunk attempt | Checked whether the f32 vector8 chunk standard-fill win transfers to the generic `Rng.fillNormalFrom(..., f32, 0, 1)` standard-argument branch | A production retry preserved stream shape in focused tests but regressed the main throughput harness: `bench -- 268435456 "fillNormal f32"` fell from a fresh baseline around 363.5M to about 298.8M samples/s. | Rejected and reverted. Keep `Rng.fillNormalFrom` on its existing scalar loop for the standard f32 branch; the distribution-layer f32 standard fill win does not transfer through this generic branch/codegen shape. |
| Normal/Exponential reusable sampler fill APIs | `Normal(T)` / `Exponential(T)` reusable samplers had `sampleFrom` but lacked `fill` / `fillFrom`, unlike most other reusable samplers | Added `Normal(T).fill` / `fillFrom` and `Exponential(T).fill` / `fillFrom`, with unit coverage and benchmark rows. Current rows track the direct fill helpers: `Normal.fillFrom scalar direct` remains around the direct normal fill profile, while `Exponential.fillFrom scalar direct` is about 388M after the exponential mantissa-threshold fast path. A later `Exponential.fillFrom` reroute through `Rng.fillExponentialFrom(..., rate=1)` plus scaling measured about 369M on `zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dcpu=native bench -- 16777216`, below `fillExponential scalar direct` around 387M. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free reusable-sampler workflows; keep the direct per-sample `sampleFrom` loop for `Exponential.fillFrom` because the staged scaling reroute regresses the scalar-direct fill row. |
| Beta/Fisher reusable samplers | `rand_distr beta`: about 16M samples/s | `alea beta`: about 77M single-shot, about 87M cached; `alea fisher-f`: about 72M facade, about 80M direct-source/cached | Closed for current local Rust evidence; cached/direct Gamma composition is substantially faster |
| Beta(1,1) uniform fast path | `Beta(1,1)` is exactly `Uniform(0,1)`, but Alea previously sampled two Gamma variates and normalized them | Added `zig build beta-special-probe` and routed parameterized `betaFrom(1,1)` / `fillBetaFrom(1,1)` through `Rng.floatFrom` / `Rng.fillFrom`. A follow-up routed reusable `Beta(1,1).sampleFrom/fillFrom` through matching method tags instead of the Gamma-backed sampler path. Local parameterized rows improved single-sample `Beta(1,1)` from about 237M to 1140M FastPrng and from about 289M to 1505M ScalarPrng; fill improved from about 216M to 873M FastPrng and from about 130M to 706M ScalarPrng. Reusable `Beta(1,1).sampleFrom` improved from about 241M to 868M FastPrng and from about 302M to 1298M ScalarPrng, with fill now around 597M/722M. Ordinary `Beta(2,5)` rows stayed in range: about 72M facade, 82M cached, 73M fill, and 84M scalar fill. `distcheck` includes `beta-unit`. | Adopted for parameterized and reusable edge APIs. Cached `Beta(2,5)` remains on the Gamma-backed sampler path. |
| Beta(alpha,1) inverse-CDF fast path | `Beta(alpha,1)` can be sampled as `U^(1/alpha)`, and the common `Beta(2,1)` case reduces to `sqrt(U)` | Extended `beta-special-probe` and routed parameterized `betaFrom(alpha,1)` / `fillBetaFrom(alpha,1)` through inverse-CDF sampling, with a dedicated `sqrt` branch for `alpha == 2`. Local `Beta(2,1)` rows improved single-sample from about 115M to 664M FastPrng and from about 135M to 664M ScalarPrng; fill improved from about 112M to 316M FastPrng and from about 132M to 348M ScalarPrng. The opposite `Beta(1,5)` inverse-CDF candidate was rejected because it dropped to about 44-46M versus current 119-143M single-sample rows. Ordinary `Beta(2,5)` stayed in range. `distcheck` now includes a `beta-alpha-two` mean gate. | Adopted narrowly for parameterized APIs. Cached `Beta(2,5)` remains on the Gamma-backed sampler path; do not use the slower `Beta(1,beta)` complement-root form as a default. |
| Beta(1,2) complement-sqrt fast path | `Beta(1,2)` can be sampled as `1 - sqrt(U)`, a cheap special case distinct from the slower generic `Beta(1,beta)` complement-root form | Extended `beta-special-probe` and routed parameterized `betaFrom(1,2)` / `fillBetaFrom(1,2)` through the complement-sqrt form. Reusable `Beta(1,1)`, `Beta(2,1)`, and `Beta(1,2)` now share method-tag dispatch for these edge cases. Local rows improved single-sample from about 116M to 663M FastPrng and from about 139M to 663M ScalarPrng; fill improved from about 73M to 316M FastPrng and from about 76M to 348M ScalarPrng. The generic `Beta(1,5)` complement-root candidate remains rejected because it is slower than the current Gamma-backed path. `distcheck` includes `beta-one-two`. | Adopted for parameterized and reusable edge APIs. Do not generalize this to arbitrary `Beta(1,beta)` without stronger evidence. |
| Beta bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillBeta`, `fillBetaFrom`, and `Beta.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume Beta slices. Local rows: facade fill about 73M samples/s and direct-source fill about 84M, above single-shot facade sampling around 71M and close to cached repeated sampling around 81M. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free bulk workflows; direct-source fill is a useful high-volume path. |
| Beta parameter/moment accessors | `Beta(T)` stores alpha/beta parameters and cached Gamma samplers but did not expose the original shape parameters, moments, mode, or support bounds for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Beta(T).alphaValue`, `betaValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. `modeValue` returns `null` for non-unique mode cases. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| ChiSquared/StudentT reusable samplers | `rand_distr chi-squared`: about 125M samples/s; `rand_distr student-t`: about 104M | `alea chi-squared`: about 137M single-shot, about 159M cached; `alea student-t`: about 104M facade, about 123M cached, about 125M direct-source after `studentTFrom` | Closed for current local Rust evidence; single-shot `studentT` now routes through direct-source composition |
| Fisher-F and Student-T bulk fill APIs | `rand_distr fisher-f`: about 66M; `rand_distr student-t`: about 104M | Added `fillFisherF`, `fillFisherFFrom`, `fillStudentT`, `fillStudentTFrom`, and reusable sampler `fill` / `fillFrom` methods. | FisherF and StudentT parameter accessors | `FisherF(T)` and `StudentT(T)` cache derived Gamma/ChiSquared samplers but did not expose the original degrees-of-freedom parameters, finite moments, or support bounds for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `FisherF(T).d1Value`, `d2Value`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, plus `StudentT(T).dofValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. Moment and unbounded support accessors return `null` when undefined or unbounded. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| StudentT infinite-dof sampling | Local `rand_distr::StudentT::new` accepts infinite degrees through ChiSquared/Gamma composition; samples are NaN while consuming a standard-normal draw followed by the infinite-ChiSquared/Gamma draw shape. | Scalar/vector top-level, checked, reusable, and fill helpers now accept `dof == inf`, produce NaN, and preserve the corresponding StandardNormal plus ChiSquared/Gamma stream shape. Focused tests cover scalar/vector checked/unchecked, fill, reusable, facade/direct-source paths, and invalid no-consume cases; infinite-dof moment accessors return `null`. | Adopted for local `rand_distr` compatibility; this supersedes the previous Alea-only standard-normal limit extension. |
| FisherF(2,2) uniform-ratio candidate | `FisherF(2,2)` is distribution-equivalent to the ratio of two exponentials and to `U / (1 - U)`, so tested whether the parameterized path should use the cheaper uniform ratio | Added `zig build fisher-f-probe`. | StudentT dof=1 Cauchy-equivalent candidate | `StudentT(1)` is distribution-equivalent to standard Cauchy, so tested whether `studentTFrom(dof == 1)` / `fillStudentTFrom(dof == 1)` should route through Cauchy | Added `zig build student-t-probe`. Cauchy-equivalent fill is faster, about 75M FastPrng and 73M ScalarPrng versus current StudentT fill around 31M/34M, but single-sample Cauchy is much slower at about 62M/68M versus current StudentT around 230M/273M, and checksums differ because the random stream shape changes. | Deferred/rejected as a default path for now. Keep StudentT on its current normal/chi-squared composition until the stream-shape tradeoff is explicitly accepted or a no-regression fill-only API is designed. |
| ChiSquared bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillChiSquared`, `fillChiSquaredFrom`, and `ChiSquared.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. | ChiSquared moment accessors | `ChiSquared(T)` exposes degrees of freedom but did not expose the closed-form mean/variance/mode or support bounds for diagnostics | Added `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| ChiSquared and Chi parameter accessors | `ChiSquared(T)` and `Chi(T)` cache Gamma/ChiSquared samplers but did not expose the original degrees-of-freedom parameter or support bounds for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `ChiSquared(T).dofValue` and `Chi(T).dofValue` plus `Chi(T).minValue` / `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Chi moment accessors | `Chi(T)` exposed its degree-of-freedom parameter but not the theoretical mean/variance/mode used for diagnostics | Added `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, and `modeValue` using closed forms, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |

Cached `ChiSquared(dof=4)` remains on the Gamma-backed sampler path. |
| ChiSquared dof=2 exponential-equivalent audit | `ChiSquared(2)` is exactly `2 * Exp(1)`, so checked whether it still needed a dedicated path after the `Gamma(1, scale)` specialization | Extended `chi-probe`. | Chi bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillChi`, `fillChiFrom`, and `Chi.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. | Chi dof=1 absolute-normal fast path | `Chi(1)` is exactly `abs(N(0,1))`. | Chi dof=2 Rayleigh-equivalent audit | `Chi(2)` is Rayleigh with scale 1, and also `sqrt(2 * Exp(1))`. | Erlang small-k product/vector-log candidate | For integer shape `k`, Erlang can be sampled as `-scale * log(product(open_uniform[0..k]))`, so tried staging open uniforms and applying vector `log` for small shapes | Added `zig build erlang-probe`. | LogNormal f32 Rust comparison row | Local Rust harness previously only had an f64 log-normal row, while Alea had f32 probe evidence but no direct Rust comparison; fresh 1GiB filtered Rust f32 row is about 155.3M samples/s | Added `rand_distr log-normal f32` and matching Alea scalar/raw f32 rows. After the f32 standard-fill vector8 chunk path was adopted and `fillLogNormalFrom(..., f32, mean=0, stddev!=1)` was narrowed to use it for direct-source calls only, focused `bench -- 268435456 "fillLogNormal f32"` reports about 138.5M facade, 144.2M FastPrng direct, and 148.9M ScalarPrng direct with matching checksums. Native/approximation opt-ins remain faster in selected profiles but intentionally use separate mappings. | Adopted for direct-source exact f32 LogNormal bulk fills only. Added Alea bulk rows too: exact f64 facade/FastPrng-direct/ScalarPrng-direct about 72.2M/74.4M/76.6M, and exact f32 about 76.7M/77.1M/80.2M. | Evidence only. | InverseGaussian/NormalInverseGaussian reusable samplers | `rand_distr inverse-gaussian`: about 77M; `rand_distr normal-inverse-gaussian`: about 67M | `alea inverse-gaussian`: filtered rows about 72.8M single-shot, about 72.7M FastPrng direct, about 78.0M cached after sampler constant caching, and 231M/267M fill facade/direct; `alea normal-inverse-gaussian`: about 63.7M single-shot, about 63.1M FastPrng direct, about 68.0M cached, about 67.2M scalar direct, and 155M/172M fill facade/direct | Closed for cached inverse-Gaussian, cached/scalar-direct NIG, and bulk workflows; FastPrng direct rows show facade dispatch is not the inverse-Gaussian/NIG single-shot bottleneck, and inverse-Gaussian single-shot remains a small watch item versus filtered Rust around 77M. |
| InverseGaussian-family parameter accessors | `InverseGaussian(T)` and `NormalInverseGaussian(T)` cache derived parameters but did not expose the original user-facing parameters for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `InverseGaussian(T).meanValue` / `shapeValue` and `NormalInverseGaussian(T).alphaValue` / `betaValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| InverseGaussian moment accessors | `InverseGaussian(T)` exposed mean/shape parameters but not a naming-consistent expected value, theoretical variance, or support bounds for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `expectedValue` as a mean alias, `varianceValue = mean^3 / shape`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| NormalInverseGaussian moment accessors | `NormalInverseGaussian(T)` exposed alpha/beta but not the derived gamma parameter, finite moments, or unbounded support for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `gammaValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue` for the current `mu=0`, `delta=1` parameterization, with unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| InverseGaussian `(mean=1, shape=2)` scalar specialization | Tried a narrow parameter branch using constants `0.25`, `8`, and `1 / x` in `inverseGaussianFrom` for the benchmarked `(1,2)` shape. Filtered rows improved FastPrng direct modestly from about 72M to 73-74M and NIG scalar direct slightly, but facade single-shot stayed about 72M and the branch is too benchmark-specific. | Rejected as a default path. Keep the generic Michael-Schucany-Haas expression; prefer cached samplers or bulk fills for high-volume `(1,2)` workflows. |
| InverseGaussian sample construction/order probes | Checked whether the remaining single-shot gap is due to reusable sampler constant access, local sampler construction/codegen, or drawing the uniform immediately after the normal before transform math | Extended `inverse-gaussian-probe` with sample rows. Fresh `inverse-gaussian-probe -- 1048576` rows: FastPrng current/cached/local/uniform-first about 74.2/74.6/75.0/73.7M, ScalarPrng about 77.9/78.6/80.4/78.5M, all with matching checksums. Fill rows in the same run remained strong at about 245M FastPrng and 268M ScalarPrng. | Evidence only. The small sample-row differences are not a durable production win and the local-construction row is a probe/codegen artifact; keep the current generic scalar path and recommend cached samplers or bulk fills for high-volume workflows. |
| InverseGaussian four-shape cache probe | Checked whether caching `4 * shape` in the reusable sampler removes a hot multiply from the Michael-Schucany-Haas sample path without specializing to `(mean=1, shape=2)` | Extended `inverse-gaussian-probe` with four-shape-cache sample rows. A focused `inverse-gaussian-probe -- 4194304` run shows FastPrng current/cached/four-shape/local/uniform-first about 74.7/74.4/74.8/73.3/74.4M and ScalarPrng about 77.8/78.9/78.5/79.5/77.6M, all with matching checksums; fill rows remain governed by the vectorized bulk path around 245M FastPrng and 266M ScalarPrng. | Rejected as a production default. Caching `4 * shape` is not a durable no-regression win over the current cached sampler/local codegen profile; keep the existing fields and scalar sample expression. |
| NormalInverseGaussian bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillNormalInverseGaussian`, `fillNormalInverseGaussianFrom`, and `NormalInverseGaussian.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. Initial local rows were about 47M facade fill and 60M direct-source fill. After vectorized InverseGaussian fill, `NormalInverseGaussian.fillFrom` stages inverse-Gaussian values and then combines with scalar normals. Latest `zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dcpu=native nig-probe -- 4194304` rows are about 156.0M current FastPrng fill versus 156.4M staged scalar combine, and about 168.5M current ScalarPrng fill versus 168.0M staged scalar combine; staged vector4 combine trails both profiles at about 142.4M/157.5M. | Adopted for API consistency, allocation-free bulk workflows, and a large high-volume speedup over local Rust evidence. Keep current combine shape until an engine-agnostic no-regression variant is found. |
| InverseGaussian bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillInverseGaussian`, `fillInverseGaussianFrom`, and `InverseGaussian.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. Initial rows were about 66M facade fill and 70M direct-source fill. `inverse-gaussian-probe` showed that staging standard normals then vectorizing the Michael-Schucany-Haas transform with lane-wise `sqrt`/`select` reaches about 235M facade and 250M scalar-direct in isolation; a fresh `zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dcpu=native inverse-gaussian-probe -- 4194304` run shows current production fill about 238.3M FastPrng and 258.0M ScalarPrng, roughly matching/slightly ahead of the staged vector4 candidate at about 239.0M/254.9M and far ahead of staged scalar around 80-82M. | Adopted. Bulk InverseGaussian now substantially exceeds local Rust evidence while single-sample APIs retain the scalar draw order. |
| SkewNormal scalar profile | `rand_distr skew-normal`: about 218M filtered samples/s for shape=1 and about 203M for shape=2 in the latest focused run; older Rust shape=1 evidence reached about 263M | After marking reusable `SkewNormal.sampleFrom` / `fillFrom` inline, focused rows now have wrapper/direct calls matching raw direct rows: about 228M facade, 229M FastPrng direct, and 293M ScalarPrng direct for shape=1, plus about 229M facade/FastPrng direct and 293M ScalarPrng direct for shape=2. Probe rows show current fill around 221M FastPrng and 263M ScalarPrng after shape-method branch hoisting and generic-fill delta precompute. | Closed for focused facade/direct/raw scalar evidence; wrapper/codegen overhead is fixed in the full benchmark harness, and FastPrng shape=1/2 fill now exceeds current filtered Rust evidence. |
| SkewNormal general-shape benchmark evidence | Added `shape = 2` rows to cover the generic linear-combination branch instead of only the `shape = 1` max-normal branch.
