Local Rust `shape=2` row is about 203M samples/s in the latest focused run. | Filtered full-harness shape=2 facade/direct/raw rows are now about 228-229M FastPrng and about 293-294M ScalarPrng after the inline reusable wrapper fix and generic-branch min/max hoist. Generic-shape bulk fill now precomputes delta/orthogonal once per fill and uses the delta/Azzalini loop directly, lifting `fillSkewNormal shape=2 fast direct` to about 220M while keeping checksum unchanged; latest probe rows show FastPrng shape=2 current and delta-form both about 222M, ScalarPrng current about 264M, and isolated ScalarPrng sample loop about 292M. | Closed for filtered facade/direct/raw evidence and adopted for generic-shape fill; remaining SkewNormal watch narrows to broader FastPrng fill profiles rather than shape=2 delta-form adoption. |
| Unit geometry scalar profile | Latest focused Rust rows: unit circle about 157-159M, unit disc about 164M, unit sphere about 157M, unit ball about 56M | After the f64 point FMA specialization and the UnitCircle numerator-FMA output expression, current focused Alea rows are about 155.9M facade / 158.8M FastPrng direct / 191.4M ScalarPrng direct for circle, 172.8M / 173.8M / 207.0M for disc, 164.8M / 164.7M / 200.5M for sphere, and 60.2M / 60.1M / 69.4M for ball. | Closed for focused direct point rows and all ScalarPrng point rows. Facade UnitCircle remains at the local Rust boundary, while bulk/fill ergonomics are already covered by dedicated fill APIs and vectorbench rows. |
| Unit geometry parameter accessors | Unit geometry reusable samplers encode fixed dimension/radius and surface-vs-volume semantics but did not expose them for diagnostics | Added `dimensionValue`, `radiusValue`, and `isSurface` to `UnitCircle`, `UnitDisc`, `UnitSphere`, and `UnitBall`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for fixed-geometry reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Unit geometry coordinate moments | Unit geometry reusable samplers exposed fixed geometry parameters but not the symmetric per-coordinate theoretical moments useful for diagnostics | Added `coordinateExpectedValue` and `coordinateVarianceValue` to `UnitCircle`, `UnitDisc`, `UnitSphere`, and `UnitBall`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for fixed-geometry reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Unit geometry radial moments | Unit geometry reusable samplers exposed fixed geometry and coordinate moments but not Euclidean radius moments for surface-vs-volume diagnostics | Added `radialExpectedValue` and `radialVarianceValue` to `UnitCircle`, `UnitDisc`, `UnitSphere`, and `UnitBall`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for fixed-geometry reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Alphanumeric strings | `rand alphanumeric`: about 840M chars/s | `alea alphanumeric`: about 949M chars/s | Closed for current local Rust evidence |
| Power-of-two integer ranges | Lemire multiply-high rejects nothing when the bound is a power of two, so `x >> (bits - log2(bound))` is an exact replacement | `Rng.uintLessThanFrom` now takes a power-of-two branch that preserves the accepted value and consumes exactly one draw. Focused same-host rows: direct 1645.8M -> 1972.3M samples/s (about 20% faster); facade 1068.8M -> 1067.3M, within noise because the type-erased indirect call dominates. A focused test compares the fast path against the Lemire mapping and verifies identical engine state. | Adopted for direct-source and non-erased callers; no stream-shape change. |
| Comptime-specialized `RngOf` view | Type-erased `Rng` facade overhead remains visible in distribution-heavy rows such as Poisson and Gamma | Added `RngOf(Engine)`, a comptime-specialized view that forwards common hot-path operations to the existing `*From` helpers. Focused normal row: `RngOf` 923.7M vs direct 922.9M samples/s with identical checksum, while `Rng` remains available for heterogeneous/type-erased storage. | Adopted as an opt-in hot-path view; no change to existing `Rng` behavior. |
| LogNormal inlined exponential default | The exact `LogNormal(T)` default used the out-of-line system `@exp` call (123.3M facade / 135.8M scalar direct f64 at 128MiB) and trailed local Rust `rand_distr 0.6.0` at 236.2M, whose `LogNormal` inlines the pure-Rust `libm` exponential; the vector single-vector `@exp` path was only 26.0M vectors/s | S4-M1773 makes the S4-M1761 inlined branchless f64/f32 kernel the exact default transform (also `fromZScore`, buffered degenerate paths, and vector `LogNormal`), marks the `logNormalFrom`/sampler methods `inline`, and keeps `LogNormalFastExp` as an explicit profile name. Same-host 128MiB rows: 345.5M facade / 283.6-328.1M FastPrng direct / 334.8-379.1M ScalarPrng direct f64, stddev=1 364.6M, f32 471.7M, and 61.8M f64x4 vector vectors/s. | Adopted as a deliberate versioned output-mapping change recorded in `reproducibility-matrix.md`; the default now exceeds local Rust on every LogNormal row. Evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1773-lognormal-inlined-exp-default.md`. |
| Wrapped skew-normal image fast path | The wrapped skew-normal quantile was the slowest harness row (0.085M quantiles/s, 0.647M cdf/s at a 1GiB-equivalent count) because each CDF evaluation recomputed the fixed endpoint term per `2*pi` image and Newton kept bisecting through the CDF noise floor | Added a lazy per-branch `WrappedSkewNormalCdfCache`, skipped images where the point and endpoint both saturate beyond `|z| >= 9` (below `1e-18` mass, bit-identical), fused the CDF/density image pass, started Newton from the skew-normal quantile, and stopped when the correction is below the `1e-12` x tolerance. Same-host 1GiB rows: 0.254M quantiles/s (2.99x) and 1.326M cdf/s (2.05x) with unchanged checksums. | Adopted as a stream-preserving optimization; evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1762-wrapped-skew-normal-quantile.md`. |

| Owen T inlined exponential (rejected) | After the LogNormal fast-exp kernel landed, tried replacing the libm `@exp` in `owenTIntegrand` with the inlined `fastExp64` kernel to cut the 24-node Gauss-Legendre cost | Skew-normal CDF fell from 3.10M to 2.47M cdf/s (-20%), wrapped skew-normal CDF from 1.39M to 1.12M (-19%), and the wrapped skew-normal quantile from 0.272M to 0.227M (-17%) with unchanged checksums. | Rejected and reverted; the out-of-line libm exp pipelines better inside the quadrature loop than the inlined kernel. |
| Skew-normal Mills far tail (rejected) | Tried `normUpperTailF64` instead of the incomplete-gamma continued fraction for `skewNormalUpperTailZ` when `z >= 6` | Skew-normal CDF 3.07M, wrapped skew-normal CDF 1.33M, and wrapped skew-normal quantile 0.261M — neutral or slightly worse than the current path. `std.math.erfc` is not available in Zig 0.16, so the erfc-based replacement was not viable. | Rejected and reverted. |
| Vector PERT per-lane free function (rejected) | Tried routing `vectorPertFrom` through the per-lane scalar `pertFrom` instead of constructing `VectorPert` | 16.1M -> 16.5M vectors/s (+2.5%) but a different checksum, because the reusable `Pert` sampler and the free `pertFrom` use different beta paths. | Rejected; the 2.5% gain does not justify changing the sampling stream. |

| Skew-normal upper-tail Mills replacement (rejected) | After the Owen T far-tail win, tried replacing `Q(1/2, z²/2)` in `skewNormalUpperTailZ` with `normUpperTailF64(z)` for all `z` | Wrapped skew-normal CDF 2.70M -> 3.05M cdf/s (+13%), but the quantile collapsed from 0.342M to 0.115M quantiles/s and the checksums changed because the Mills series is inaccurate below `z = 6`, so Newton failed to converge. | Rejected and reverted; keep the incomplete-gamma `Q` for `z < 6`. |
| Skew-normal quantile tolerance loosening (neutral) | Tried relaxing the skew-normal quantile bracket tolerance from `1e-12` to `1e-9` (the CDF itself is only ~1e-7 accurate) | 1.016M -> 1.025M quantiles/s with unchanged checksums — within noise. | Reverted to keep the documented `1e-12` tolerance. |

| Facade-aware f32 standard normal fill | `fillStandardNormalFrom(f32)` always used the vector8 chunk path, which pays the `Rng` vtable cost per draw for the type-erased facade (about 377M samples/s versus 650-695M for direct sources) | The f32 fill now uses the scalar per-element loop when the source is `Rng` and keeps the vector8 chunk path for direct sources; the facade row rises to 529-561M samples/s with an unchanged checksum. The same split for `fillStandardExponentialFrom(f32)` regressed 562.3M -> 529.9M and was reverted. | Adopted for the standard normal fill only; evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1778-facade-f32-normal-fill.md`. |

| ChaCha fill zeroing removal | The buffered ChaCha `fill` zeroed each consumed buffer region after copying it out, adding a second memory pass over every emitted byte | Removed the zeroing (the copied bytes are public output and the hidden key prefix is regenerated on refill); `chacha12 fill-only` rises from 2400.9M to 2476-2494M MiB/s with an unchanged stream. The remaining gap to Rust `StdRng` is the rekeying construction's buffer copy-through. | Adopted; evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1780-chacha-fill-zeroing.md`. |

| Four-word batched engine fills | The xoshiro/pcg/wyhash fills emitted one word per iteration with a separate `writeInt` + `@memcpy`, while Rust `SmallRng` writes 32-byte chunks | The five 64-bit/32-bit engine fills now emit four words per iteration (32-byte/16-byte stores) with unchanged word order and tail handling. Three-run A/B: `xoshiro256++ fill-only` 10841-11069M -> 11263-11269M MiB/s (about +4%, parity with local Rust `SmallRng` 11314M). | Adopted; evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1781-batched-engine-fills.md`. |

## Rejected Or Deferred Attempts

| Attempt | Result | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lower Poisson PTRS threshold from `lambda >= 30` to `lambda >= 12` | `lambda = 20` benchmark dropped from about 26M samples/s to about 23M samples/s, while `distcheck` still passed | Rejected. Dedicated medium-lambda Ahrens-Dieter path was adopted instead. |
| Cache Poisson Ahrens-Dieter constants in reusable `Poisson` sampler | `alea poisson cached`: about 52M samples/s, essentially same as single-shot after adoption | Kept for API quality and avoiding recomputation, but not enough to close the remaining Rust performance gap. |
| Direct `normalFrom` helper wrapping `std.Random.floatNorm` | About 208M samples/s, essentially same as facade/direct `std.Random` and still far below `rand_distr` | Rejected as public API. Normal gap needs a faster normal kernel, not another wrapper. |
| SplitMix64 as scalar-fast distribution engine | `splitmix64 next`: about 1980M next/s; `normal splitmix64 direct`: about 375M samples/s | Rejected as scalar-fast profile. `Wyhash64` remains better for scalar-heavy distribution workloads. |
| Force-inline `Wyhash64.next` / `wymix` | No durable benchmark win: standard-normal stayed around 426M samples/s, standard-exponential around 430M, with several derived rows flat or slightly lower | Rejected. Keep engine declarations simple and focus on algorithm-level or compiler-visible call-shape changes. |
| Direct ziggurat normal using engine `next()` instead of `std.Random.int` | About 386-390M samples/s; repeated runs showed `std.Random.floatNorm` can also fall back near 210M | Adopted as default. Still trails `rand_distr`, so keep optimizing. |
| Reusable `Normal(T)` sampler switched from cached Box-Muller to direct ziggurat | `alea log-normal` improved from about 47M to about 104M samples/s and normal-derived reusable samplers benefit | Adopted. Keep Box-Muller only as a possible future specialized sampler if needed. |
| Reusable/direct `LogNormal(T).sampleFrom` and `logNormalFrom` | `alea log-normal scalar direct` and `alea log-normal raw scalar direct` both run about 134M in the latest focused rows after the explicit standard-normal expression and `mean == 0` branch, versus raw/FastPrng/facade around 118M and latest filtered Rust around 146M | Adopted. This carries scalar-fast normal sampling into log-normal workflows; raw direct evidence shows sampler-wrapper dispatch is not the scalar bottleneck, so LogNormal remains a core transform/engine-profile boundary item versus filtered Rust. |
| LogNormal bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillLogNormal`, `fillLogNormalFrom`, and `LogNormal.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume log-normal slices. Initial rows were about 101M samples/s for facade fill and 112M for direct-source fill. A follow-up `log-normal-probe` showed that staging normals into the destination and applying `@exp` in place beats per-sample `normal + exp`; production fill uses that staged path. Latest probe and full-benchmark rows show scalar f64 `@exp` beats the vector-lane `@exp` shape on this target, so `expInPlace(f64)` now uses a scalar loop. Focused fill rows improved to about 133M facade/FastPrng direct and about 140M ScalarPrng direct with matching checksums; `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` also shows normal-only fill around 407M FastPrng / 459M ScalarPrng, isolating the remaining bulk cost to the `exp` transform rather than normal generation. | Adopted for API consistency, allocation-free bulk workflows, and a measurable bulk speedup; FastPrng direct fill isolates facade dispatch from the remaining log-normal boundary, while ScalarPrng bulk is now close to filtered Rust scalar evidence. |
| LogNormal f32 bulk probe rows | Existing `log-normal-probe` only split f64 normal-generation cost from the `exp` transform | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f32 normal-only/current/scalar-exp/vector8-exp rows and added main throughput `fillLogNormal f32` facade/FastPrng/ScalarPrng rows. Latest `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` reports f32 normal-only around 390M FastPrng / 452M ScalarPrng, while current f32 LogNormal fill is about 138M / 145M; scalar and vector8 exp rows are essentially tied with current. Focused throughput rows are about 132M facade/FastPrng direct and about 141M ScalarPrng direct. | Evidence only. Like f64, f32 LogNormal bulk is transform-bound rather than normal-generation-bound; keep the existing `expInPlace(f32)` vector path until a clear no-regression transform wins. |
| LogNormal f32 vector-width exp probe | Checked whether f32 `expInPlace` should use vector4 or vector16 instead of the current vector8 shape | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f32 vector4/vector16 rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` shows FastPrng f32 current/vector4/vector8/vector16 around 138/137/138/138M and ScalarPrng around 143/143/145/143M, with matching checksums. | Evidence only. No durable vector-width win over the current vector8 f32 transform; keep production unchanged. |
| LogNormal f32 widened exact exp probe | Checked whether computing exact f32 LogNormal as widened f64 `@exp` then casting back to f32 improves transform codegen while keeping tight accuracy | Extended `log-normal-probe` with staged widened f64-exp rows, single-sample widened rows, and error scans. Fresh fill rows show widened f64-exp is slower than current exact f32: about 124M versus 138M FastPrng and about 130M versus 144M ScalarPrng. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "f32 sample"` likewise shows single-sample widened f64 exp only about 98M FastPrng and 107M ScalarPrng versus current exact rows around 119M/134M. Error scans show max 1 ULP at `stddev=0.25`, `1.0`, and `2.0`, but the throughput loss makes it unsuitable as a default. | Rejected as a production default. Keep exact f32 on direct `@exp`; widened f64 exp is accuracy-interesting but not a performance win. |
| LogNormal out-of-place exact transform probe | Checked whether writing standard/parameterized normal samples to a temporary buffer and applying exact `@exp` into the destination avoids in-place alias/codegen costs | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 out-of-place scalar transform rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` is mixed: f64 FastPrng ties staged scalar around 134M, f64 ScalarPrng improves only about 140M to 142M, f32 FastPrng about 138M to 139M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 145M to 146M, with matching checksums. A production retry using out-of-place transforms for `dest.len <= 1024` was likewise mixed in focused `bench -- 268435456 "fillLogNormal"` rows: exact f64 facade/FastPrng/ScalarPrng about 131M/132M/140M versus reverted in-place baseline around 135M/135M/142M, while exact f32 was about 136M/136M/144M versus baseline around 133M/133M/141M. A later noalias out-of-place probe failed to reveal a hidden alias/codegen win: `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "out-of-place"` showed scalar/noalias pairs tied around 131M f64 FastPrng, 135M f64 ScalarPrng, 137M f32 FastPrng, and 142M f32 ScalarPrng. | Rejected as a production default. The f32 movement does not justify the f64 regression, and noalias annotations do not change the conclusion; keep the simpler in-place transform until a cross-type no-regression candidate appears. |
| LogNormal staging chunk-size probe | Checked whether the 1024-element staging buffer in the LogNormal probe hides a chunk-size/codegen win for exact fills | Extended `log-normal-probe` with current-fill chunk256/chunk4096/chunk16384 rows. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 1048576 "current fill chunk"` shows f64 FastPrng about 122M/125M/123M, f64 ScalarPrng about 131M/131M/129M, f32 FastPrng about 137M/137M/136M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 143M/143M/142M, all with matching checksums. | Evidence only. Chunk-size changes are mixed or slower versus the existing 1024-element staging shape; keep the current buffer size. |
| LogNormal `std.math.exp` transform probe | Checked whether `std.math.exp` produces a better scalar transform loop than direct `@exp` for f64/f32 staged log-normal fills | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 `std.math.exp` rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` shows `std.math.exp` effectively tied with scalar `@exp`: f64 FastPrng about 132M vs 132M, f64 ScalarPrng about 138M vs 138M, f32 FastPrng about 138M vs 138M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 145M vs 144-145M, all with matching checksums. A production f64 retry using `std.math.exp` in `expInPlaceScalar` stayed tied/noisy rather than winning: focused `bench -- 268435456 "fillLogNormal"` candidate rows were about 131M/133M/141M for exact f64 facade/FastPrng/ScalarPrng and about 74M/75M/77M for stddev=1, versus reverted direct-`@exp` rows around 121-133M/132M/140M and 74M/74M/77M depending on run order. | Rejected as a production default. Keep the existing `@exp`-based production transform; `std.math.exp` does not provide a durable no-regression win. |
| LogNormal noinline exact `@exp` transform probe | Checked whether isolating the exact transform loop behind a noinline function changes LLVM/Zig codegen versus the current inlinable staged helper | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 staged noinline transform rows. One 4Mi run showed noinline ahead of the standalone staged-scalar rows, but a same-host `current fill` rerun kept production current fill at about 134.9M/139.1M f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng and 135.1M/141.2M f32, while noinline was about 131.5M/138.0M f64 and 136.5M/142.6M f32. Checksums matched. | Evidence only. Noinline is mixed and f32-only/noisy rather than a cross-type exact-transform win; keep production on the current transform shape. |
| LogNormal transform plus checksum probe | Checked whether the probe/main benchmark's separate checksum pass hides exact transform-loop throughput after the destination has been written | Extended `log-normal-probe` with staged `exp+sum` rows that apply exact `@exp`, write the destination, and accumulate the checksum in the same pass. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "exp+sum"` reports about 131M/138M f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng and 147M/153M f32, versus same-host staged-scalar rows about 122M/132M f64 and 136M/142M f32; checksums match. | Evidence only. This clarifies benchmark accounting and shows f32 exact transform throughput can look higher when the checksum reread is folded away, but it is not a library API/default change because production fills do not compute user checksums. |
| LogNormal `stddev=1` transform expression probe | The wider-spread rows are slower than the narrow rows, so checked whether `std.math.exp` differs from direct `@exp` for the staged `stddev=1` transform | Extended `log-normal-probe` with `stddev1` exact fill rows. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 1048576 "stddev1"` shows current direct `@exp` around 70.5M/74.5M f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng and 75.7M/77.7M f32, while `std.math.exp` is about 72.9M/74.4M f64 and 76.0M/77.7M f32. Checksums match. | Evidence only. `std.math.exp` helps only one isolated wide-spread profile and ties elsewhere; keep direct `@exp` as the exact production default. |
| LogNormal `stddev=1` sample expression probe | The wider-spread single-sample rows still trail Rust, so checked whether direct `@exp`, `std.math.exp`, or libc changes the expression boundary | Extended `log-normal-probe` with `sample stddev1` rows. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 1048576 "sample stddev1"` shows all exact expression shapes effectively tied: f64 FastPrng current/direct/std.math/libc about 58.4/58.2/58.4/58.3M, f64 ScalarPrng about 64.2M, f32 FastPrng about 56.2/56.2/56.7/56.4M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 61.2-61.3M, with matching checksums. | Evidence only. The wide-spread single-sample gap is not fixed by choosing a different exact exp entry point; keep direct `@exp`. |
| LogNormal libc `exp` / `expf` transform probe | Checked whether platform libc exponential functions beat Zig builtin `@exp` for staged exact LogNormal transforms | Linked libc for `log-normal-probe` only and added f64 `exp` plus f32 `expf` rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` shows libc f64 is mixed: about 130M FastPrng versus scalar `@exp` about 132M, but about 144M ScalarPrng versus scalar `@exp` about 139M. f32 `expf` regresses FastPrng at about 129M versus 138M, but improves ScalarPrng at about 152M versus 145M. Checksums match. | Evidence only. libc linkage is not suitable as a generic default and the result is engine/type-specific; keep `@exp` production paths unless an explicit opt-in libc-backed profile is designed. |
| LogNormal non-LLVM backend probe | Checked whether Zig's non-LLVM backend changes exact `@exp` codegen enough to help LogNormal | A minimal `zig build-exe -O ReleaseFast -fno-llvm` ScalarPrng f64 LogNormal loop compiled and ran, but only reached about 20M samples/s. | Rejected. Non-LLVM codegen is far below current LLVM ReleaseFast rows for this workload. |
| LogNormal builtin flag probe | Checked whether `-fbuiltin` or `-fno-builtin` changes exact `@exp` lowering/codegen for the libc-linked LogNormal probe | Focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "sample current"` rows stayed in the same band: `-fbuiltin` about 115.7M/136.2M f64 and 119.8M/135.6M f32, `-fno-builtin` about 117.4M/134.2M f64 and 121.4M/135.9M f32 for FastPrng/ScalarPrng. | Rejected. Builtin knowledge flags do not close the exact-default LogNormal gap. |
| LogNormal `-fno-compiler-rt` link probe | Checked whether disabling compiler-rt while linking libc/libm can make Zig `@exp` lower to system libm without changing source expressions | A direct `zig build-exe -fno-compiler-rt -lc -lm` build of `log-normal-probe` failed to link because the binary still needs compiler-rt helper symbols such as `__floattidf` and `__divti3` from Zig/std code. | Rejected as an exact-default path. The probe cannot simply remove compiler-rt to redirect `@exp` to libm. |
| LogNormal compiler-rt `@exp` function-pointer probe | Checked whether putting Zig `@exp` behind a `noinline` function or function pointer improves exact LogNormal by changing only the call boundary while preserving compiler-rt output mapping | A scratch probe showed direct/noinline/function-pointer/inline-wrapper `@exp` rows all around 116M FastPrng and 131-132M ScalarPrng f64 samples/s at `stddev=0.25`, with matching checksums. | Rejected. The dlsym scalar-libm speedup is not caused by a generic function-call boundary; compiler-rt `@exp` remains the exact-default bottleneck. |
| LogNormal dlsym scalar libm `exp` probe/API | Checked whether resolving glibc scalar `exp` with `std.DynLib` once and calling a function pointer changes the exact LogNormal call-boundary cost versus `extern` libc calls or compiler-rt `@exp` | A scratch probe using `std.DynLib.open("libm.so.6")` reached about 162.8M FastPrng and 186.2M ScalarPrng f64 samples/s at both `stddev=0.25` and `stddev=1`, with max 1 ULP versus Zig `@exp` over 4Mi samples. For f32, default-normal+dlsym was about 131.2M/146.3M, while native-normal+dlsym reached about 138.9M/161.5M with max 1 ULP versus default f32 `@exp`. The public `LogNormalDlsymExp(T, buffer_len)` opt-in has libc-linked main-harness sample rows around 157-170M and fill rows around 153-178M across f64/f32 FastPrng/ScalarPrng, plus `distcheck-libc` f64/f32 mean coverage. | Adopted as a high-accuracy platform opt-in for users who prefer scalar libm `exp` with small ULP drift. It changes exact bit outputs and is generally slower than `LogNormalLibmvec` throughput, so exact defaults remain unchanged. |
| LogNormal glibc libmvec vector exp probe | Checked whether the local Linux/glibc vector math ABI can break the exact LogNormal `@exp` transform bottleneck without relying on compiler auto-vectorization | Added conditional x86_64-linux-gnu `log-normal-probe` rows that call glibc libmvec vector symbols directly and link `mvec` only for that target. A focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "libmvec"` run shows very high staged-fill throughput: f64x2/f64x4 about 239.9M/236.4M FastPrng and 258.0M/250.9M ScalarPrng samples/s, and f32x8 about 294.1M FastPrng and 325.0M ScalarPrng, versus same-host current-fill rows around 124.7M/132.1M f64 and 140.4M/150.0M f32. However error scans show changed exact outputs: f64 stddev=0.25/1.0 changes about 1.85M of 4.19M samples with max 3 ULP, and f32 changes about 1.99M/2.09M samples with max 3 ULP. | Adopted narrowly as `LogNormalLibmvec(T, buffer_len)`, a runtime-loaded x86_64-linux-gnu/libc opt-in that returns `error.LibmvecUnavailable` when libmvec cannot be loaded or the build is not libc-linked. It remains outside exact defaults because it changes the stable `@exp` output mapping and depends on glibc/libmvec IFUNC behavior. |
| LogNormal buffered sample probe/API | Checked whether a reusable one-at-a-time sampler backed by the existing bulk fill path can transfer staged-fill throughput to repeated sample workflows without changing exact transform semantics | Added `log-normal-probe` buffered-sample rows with a 1024-sample internal buffer. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "sample current"` shows exact buffered sampling improves current narrow rows but does not close local Rust parity: f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng move from about 116.2M/131.1M single-sample to about 131.1M/135.6M buffered, and f32 moves from about 120.3M/135.6M to about 138.4M/147.9M, while the same-host Rust rows are about 146.1M f64 and 158.7M f32. The production `BufferedLogNormal(T, buffer_len)` opt-in fixes the refill contract explicitly; focused `bench -- 268435456 "BufferedLogNormal"` rows are about 137.9M/141.4M f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng, 135.8M/138.6M f32, 76.0M/77.0M f64 `stddev=1`, and 76.5M/78.5M f32 `stddev=1`; after optimizing `BufferedLogNormal.fillFrom` to drain existing buffered values and write full refill-sized chunks directly, matching fill rows are about 138.7M/145.6M f64, 138.3M/141.0M f32, 75.9M/77.4M f64 `stddev=1`, and 77.6M/79.4M f32 `stddev=1` with matching checksums. Libmvec-backed buffered rows are much faster, about 249M/271M f64 and 300M/342M f32, but inherit the libmvec output-mapping caveat. A follow-up dynamic-loading check opens `libmvec.so.1` with `std.DynLib` and looks up `_ZGVcN4v_exp` / `_ZGVcN8v_expf`; focused `"dynamic libmvec"` rows are about 236M/256M f64 FastPrng/ScalarPrng and 301M/341M f32, showing dynamic lookup preserves most of the linked-libmvec throughput without requiring a build-time libmvec dependency. The libc-linked main harness now has `bench-libc` rows for the public `LogNormalLibmvec`: focused `bench-libc -- 268435456 "LogNormalLibmvec"` reports sample rows about 249M/270M f64 and 299M/328M f32, with fill rows about 247M/268M f64 and 306M/331M f32. | Adopted as an explicit reusable sampler for repeated-sample workflows with a documented refill/stream contract and independent `distcheck` mean gates for direct and mean/CV construction. It improves exact LogNormal sampling without changing transform semantics, but S4-M4 still remains open because primary narrow f64/f32 rows do not yet exceed local Rust and the libmvec speed path remains output-mapping/platform-specific evidence. |
| LogNormal unrolled exact `@exp` transform probe | Checked whether manually unrolling the exact `@exp` transform loop improves staged log-normal fills without changing output semantics | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 unroll4 and unroll8 rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` shows small isolated wins in some profiles, such as f64 FastPrng unroll4 about 133M vs scalar exp about 132M and f32 FastPrng unroll8 about 138M vs 137M, but ScalarPrng f64 unroll rows trail scalar/optimized exp and f32 rows are mixed versus current/vector8/std.math.exp. Checksums match. | Evidence only. Manual unrolling is not a no-regression production default; keep existing transform loops. |
| LogNormal vector2 exact `@exp` transform probe | Checked whether vector-width 2 exact `@exp` transform loops beat scalar/vector4/vector8/vector16 transform shapes | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 vector2 rows. Probe rows looked promising in isolation, such as f64 FastPrng vector2 about 139M and f32 ScalarPrng vector2 about 148M, but switching production `expInPlace` to vector2 regressed the real 1GiB `fillLogNormal` benchmark badly, to about 112M f64 facade/direct and about 116M f32 facade/direct. Checksums matched. | Rejected as a production default. Keep scalar f64 and vector8 f32 production transforms; vector2 probe wins do not survive the real fill harness. |
| LogNormal f64 vector-lane exact `@exp` retry | Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` rows again showed isolated f64 vector-lane transform wins in some profiles, such as FastPrng vector4 about 137M versus staged scalar about 132M and ScalarPrng vector2/vector4 about 146M versus staged scalar about 143M | Retried production `expInPlace(f64)` as vector4. The real focused `bench -- 268435456 fillLogNormal` row regressed the important f64 ScalarPrng direct path to about 117M versus surrounding scalar-loop baseline rows around 136-141M, with no durable facade/FastPrng win. A later production vector8 retry also regressed the same focused harness: exact f64 facade/FastPrng/ScalarPrng about 131M/133M/141M versus the reverted scalar-loop baseline around 133M/137M/145M. Checksums matched, and both candidates were reverted. | Rejected again as a production default. The isolated probe's f64 vector-lane gains do not survive the real staged fill harness; keep the scalar f64 transform loop. |
| LogNormal exact `@exp` prefetch transform probe | Checked whether prefetching ahead in the in-place exact `@exp` transform improves the staged LogNormal fill memory/latency profile | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64 prefetch8/prefetch16 and f32 prefetch16/prefetch32 rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` showed only small/mixed isolated movement: f64 FastPrng staged scalar/prefetch8 about 134/135M, f64 ScalarPrng about 135/141M, f32 FastPrng about 137/139M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 145/146M, all with matching checksums. A production f64 prefetch8 retry passed tests but the real `bench -- 268435456 fillLogNormal` row stayed mixed around 131M facade/FastPrng direct and 138M ScalarPrng direct, below or within noise of existing scalar-loop evidence and not a durable win. | Rejected as a production default. Keep existing scalar f64 and vector8 f32 transform loops; prefetch rows remain probe evidence only. |
| LogNormal index-loop exact `@exp` transform probe | Checked whether indexing through the destination slice in a `while` loop beats pointer-capture iteration for the exact `@exp` transform | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 index-loop rows. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` shows FastPrng f64 index loop regresses badly, about 121M versus scalar pointer loop about 135M; ScalarPrng f64 and f32 index loops are only marginally above pointer loops, about 141M vs 141M and 144M vs 143M; FastPrng f32 also trails slightly, about 132M vs 136M. Checksums match. | Evidence only. Index-loop transform is not a no-regression production default; keep existing pointer-iteration transform loops. |
| LogNormal optimized float-mode transform probe | Checked whether `@setFloatMode(.optimized)` improves the `@exp` transform loop | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 optimized-float-mode `@exp` rows. Focused probe rows show optimized mode tied or mixed versus scalar `@exp`: f64 FastPrng about 128M vs 128M, f64 ScalarPrng about 134M vs 133M, f32 FastPrng about 138M vs 137M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 145M vs 147M in a 1Mi run, all with matching checksums. | Evidence only. Optimized float mode does not provide a durable no-regression win; keep production strict/default float mode. |
| LogNormal optimized float-mode full sample loop | Checked whether applying `@setFloatMode(.optimized)` around the whole standard-normal plus exact `@exp` sample loop differs from the earlier transform-only optimized-float probe | A scratch f64 sample-loop probe showed only tiny/mixed movement: narrow FastPrng/ScalarPrng about 118.0M/133.1M default versus 119.7M/135.2M optimized, and `stddev=1` about 56.9M/60.7M default versus 57.3M/60.1M optimized, with matching checksums. | Rejected. Full-loop optimized float mode still does not approach Rust rows or justify a default-mode change. |
| LogNormal `expm1(x) + 1` transform probe | Checked whether `expm1` helps the small-log-space values common in the benchmarked `stddev = 0.25` LogNormal workload | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 `expm1 + 1` rows and f32 error scans, then added single-sample f32 rows. Fresh fill rows show f64 rows slower or tied, about 131M FastPrng and 137M ScalarPrng, while f32 fill rows improve to about 140M FastPrng and 148M ScalarPrng at `stddev=0.25`. Single-sample `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "f32 sample"` does not show the same win: `expm1+1` is about 113M FastPrng and 122M ScalarPrng versus current exact rows around 119M/134M, with changed checksums. At `stddev=0.25`, about 77k of 4.19M samples change with max distance 1 ULP; smaller 1Mi sample scans show the error grows with spread: max ULP about 51 at `stddev=1.0` and about 8028 at `stddev=2.0`. | Evidence only. The f32 fill speedup is narrow-parameter and changes rounding, while single-sample rows regress; do not use as a general production default without an explicit accuracy/reproducibility policy or a parameter-limited opt-in design. |
| LogNormal hybrid `expm1` threshold probe | Tested a parameter-limited f32 hybrid using `expm1(x)+1` only for `abs(x) <= 0.25` and `@exp(x)` otherwise | On `log-normal-probe -- 1048576`, the hybrid reduced wide-parameter error to max 1 ULP for `stddev=1.0` and `2.0`, but throughput was only about 55M samples/s in the error scan and about 110M samples/s in the staged `stddev=0.25` fill row, below the current f32 fill rows around 138-148M. | Rejected as a default path. The branch restores tight error bounds for wider parameters but costs too much throughput; keep as opt-in research evidence only. |
| LogNormal local `rand_distr` implementation audit | Checked whether Rust uses a materially different log-normal algorithm that Alea should port | Local `rand_distr 0.6.0` implements `LogNormal.sample` as `self.norm.sample(rng).exp()`, with the same conceptual normal-then-exp shape as Alea's reusable sampler and staged bulk fill. | Evidence only. The remaining LogNormal gap is not an algorithm-family mismatch; focus on exact `exp` transform/codegen or engine-profile improvements. |
| HalfNormal bulk fill APIs | Local Rust evidence uses repeated sampler calls | Added `fillHalfNormal`, `fillHalfNormalFrom`, and `HalfNormal.fill` / `fillFrom` for high-volume slices. After routing Rng/Alea4x64 f64 fills through staged normal generation plus vector abs while preserving the per-sample ScalarPrng path, focused rows are about 385M facade/FastPrng fill and about 455M ScalarPrng direct fill. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free bulk workflows; staged abs is now a speed win for Rng/FastPrng without regressing ScalarPrng. |
| HalfNormal parameter/moment accessors | `HalfNormal(T)` stores its scale parameter and has closed-form moments/support bounds but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `HalfNormal(T).scaleValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| HalfNormal staged abs transform | Tried staging normal samples into the destination then applying scalar/vector absolute-value transforms in place | Latest `half-normal-probe -- 4194304` shows staged vector abs wins the FastPrng profile, about 381M versus current 337M after the production reroute, while it still regresses the ScalarPrng profile versus current 440M. Production therefore uses staged vector abs only for Rng/Alea4x64 f64 fills and keeps ScalarPrng on the per-sample path. | Adopted narrowly for Rng/Alea4x64 f64 fill paths; keep ScalarPrng on the scalar-fast per-sample path. |
| LogNormal via `exp2(x * log2e)` | The original scalar direct `exp2` attempt dropped `alea log-normal scalar direct` from about 119M to about 101M samples/s. A later staged-fill probe is more nuanced: `log-normal-probe -- 1048576 "staged exp2"` shows f32 staged exp2 is fast, about 192M FastPrng and 205M ScalarPrng for `stddev=0.25`, but f64 staged exp2 remains below current exact rows at about 120M/128M. Error scans (`exp2 diff stddev`) show output changes with observed max ULP about 1/3/7 for f64 and 1/4/7 for f32 at `stddev=0.25/1/2`; `exp2 diff mean` scans at `mean=+/-2,stddev=2` stay in the same range, with f64 max ULP 7 and f32 max ULP 7-8 in the 1Mi sample. | Rejected as an exact default because it changes output mapping and is mixed by type/profile. Keep exact `LogNormal` on direct `@exp`; consider only a separately named opt-in approximation if broader error policy and API shape are justified. |
| LogNormal direct standard-normal expression | Replaced single-sample `logNormalFrom` / reusable `LogNormal.sampleFrom` with `@exp(mean + stddev * standardNormalFastFrom(...))` while keeping the staged bulk fill path unchanged. Latest `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` now splits FastPrng and ScalarPrng sample rows: FastPrng current/standard-scale/mulAdd are about 115M/118M/115M, while ScalarPrng rows are about 133M/132M/132M. Filtered full-harness rows after the `mean == 0` branch report `alea log-normal scalar direct` about 134-136M and `fillLogNormal scalar direct` about 133-139M depending on context, with fill rows still governed by the staged production fill shape. | Adopted narrowly for single-sample APIs, including a `mean == 0` branch. Keep current staged/vectorized fill path; FastPrng sample rows show a small standard-scale expression win in isolation, but `mulAdd` remains rejected and the main boundary is still engine/profile plus `exp` transform cost. |
| LogNormal sampler direct field storage | Tried replacing the cached `Normal(T)` member in `LogNormal(T)` with direct `mean` / `stddev` fields and routing fill through `Rng.fillNormalFrom` to reduce sampler indirection. Filtered rows stayed flat or regressed: scalar direct about 133M and `fillLogNormal` direct about 136M versus the existing direct fill around 139M. | Rejected. Keep `LogNormal(T)` backed by the reusable `Normal(T)` sampler, which keeps fill behavior on the better existing path. |
| LogNormal inline wrapper attempt | Tried marking `logNormalFrom`, `LogNormal.sampleFrom`, and `LogNormal.fillFrom` inline to see whether the remaining FastPrng sample gap matched the earlier SkewNormal wrapper/codegen issue | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "log-normal"` stayed flat or lower: facade about 116M, FastPrng direct about 116M, raw FastPrng about 115M, and ScalarPrng direct/raw about 134M. `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` showed FastPrng current about 115.5M versus standard-scale about 117.5M and ScalarPrng about 133M. | Rejected. Keep LogNormal wrappers normal; remaining gap is engine/profile plus `exp` transform cost, not a wrapper-inline issue. |
| LogNormal mulAdd scalar expression | Tried replacing `stddev * standard + mean` with `@mulAdd` in `logNormalFrom`, including the common `mean == 0` path. Filtered `bench -- 268435456 "log-normal"` kept scalar direct around 133M but dropped facade to about 112M, and `log-normal-probe -- 4194304` showed mulAdd around 133M versus standard-scale around 134M. | Rejected. Keep the explicit `mean == 0` multiply branch and ordinary `mean + stddev * standard` expression for nonzero means. |
| LogNormal value receiver attempt | `LogNormal(T).sampleFrom` / `fillFrom` do not mutate the sampler, so checked whether changing pointer receivers to value receivers improves single-sample codegen or ergonomics | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "log-normal"` after the receiver change showed no improvement: facade about 114M, FastPrng direct about 106M, raw FastPrng about 119M, ScalarPrng direct about 134M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 140M, all flat or lower than the existing rows. | Rejected and reverted. Keep pointer receivers for now; receiver shape is not the remaining LogNormal bottleneck. |
| LogNormal `stddev == 1` branch | The wider-spread `stddev=1` row still used the mean-zero multiply branch, so checked whether a dedicated branch that calls `@exp(standardNormalFastFrom(...))` helps single-sample codegen | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "stddev=1"` after the branch was mixed/noisy rather than a durable win: f64 ScalarPrng direct/raw about 64.8M/65.6M and f32 about 61.4M/63.2M, versus the reverted baseline around 59.6M/65.3M f64 and 62.3M/62.7M f32. Narrow `stddev=0.25` rows also moved noisily, with no reliable improvement. | Rejected and reverted. The extra branch does not close the wider-spread single-sample gap and risks another special-case codegen fork. |
| LogNormal f32 `stddev == 1` direct-fill standard-source branch | After the direct-source f32 `stddev=0.25` LogNormal fill improvement, checked whether routing `fillLogNormalFrom(..., f32, mean=0, stddev=1)` through the optimized f32 standard fill source helps the wider-spread bulk row | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "fillLogNormal f32 stddev=1"` moved modestly from about 75.9M/76.6M/78.9M facade/FastPrng-direct/ScalarPrng-direct to about 77.1M/78.7M/79.8M. However, repeated broader `bench -- 268435456 "fillLogNormal f32"` runs showed noisy movement and possible regressions in the primary `stddev=0.25` row, including a run at about 127.7M/139.7M/137.6M versus the previously adopted focused row around 138.5M/144.2M/148.9M. | Rejected and reverted. The small wider-spread movement does not justify another special-case branch that perturbs the main f32 LogNormal bulk codegen. |
| LogNormal single-sample `std.math.exp` / libc expression probe | Checked whether the scalar `logNormalFrom` transform should use `std.math.exp` or platform libc for exact single-sample paths | Extended `log-normal-probe` with f64/f32 sample expression rows. Focused `log-normal-probe -- 4194304 "sample"` shows f64 FastPrng current/standard-scale/`std.math.exp`/libc about 116.4/116.4/116.0/116.4M, f64 ScalarPrng about 131.4/132.2/131.0/133.4M, f32 FastPrng current/`std.math.exp`/libc about 120.0/120.5/120.3M, and f32 ScalarPrng about 135.6/135.4/135.7M, with matching checksums. | Evidence only. `std.math.exp` is not a win, and libc remains too profile-specific plus requires libc linkage, so keep the exact single-sample default on direct `@exp`. |
| Direct ziggurat exponential using engine `next()` instead of `std.Random.int` | About 383M samples/s, essentially same as previous stdlib-backed path | Adopted internally for consistency with normal; not enough to close the Rust performance gap. |
| Distribution-layer normal/exponential via `std.Random.floatNorm` / `floatExp` | Older `distributions.normal` and reusable `Exponential.sample` paths used `std.Random`-backed sampling even after `Rng.normal`/`Rng.exponential` had direct ziggurat fast paths | Replaced with direct fast paths and added standard reusable samplers for explicit zero-parameter workflows. |
| Route all non-`Rng` `normalFastFrom` / `exponentialFastFrom` calls through `source.random()` | `normal wyhash64 std.Random` is about 245M samples/s, while `normal wyhash64 direct` is about 427M samples/s | Rejected as a generic rule. Keep direct ziggurat fast path; use explicit benchmarked bulk helpers for ScalarPrng recommendations. |
| Direct and checked discrete sampler methods | `Bernoulli`, `Binomial`, `NegativeBinomial`, `Hypergeometric`, `Geometric`, and `Poisson` now expose `sampleFrom`; top-level Bernoulli, Binomial, NegativeBinomial, Hypergeometric, Geometric, GeometricFailures, and Poisson helpers have direct and checked variants | Adopted for API consistency, validated user-supplied parameters, and direct-engine workflows. Keep integer-threshold Bernoulli/binomial paths to avoid float-comparison regressions. |
| Bernoulli probability/moment accessors | `Bernoulli` exposed `probability()`, while newer reusable samplers use `*Value` names plus moment/mode/support accessors for diagnostics | Added `Bernoulli.probabilityValue` as a naming-consistent alias for `probability()`, plus `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. `modeValue` returns `null` for the non-unique `p = 0.5` case. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection consistency; this is not a throughput change. |
| Direct and checked binomial Poisson approximation | `binomialPoissonApprox` remained the only public parameterized distribution helper without direct-source or checked variants | Added `binomialPoissonApproxFrom`, `binomialPoissonApproxChecked`, and `binomialPoissonApproxCheckedFrom`, preserving the existing approximation semantics while validating user-supplied probabilities. | Adopted for API consistency and direct-engine workflows; this is not a new exact-binomial path. |
| Rand-style geometric failure-count API | Local `rand_distr::Geometric` returns the number of failures before first success, while Alea's original `Geometric` returns the one-based trial count | Added `geometricFailures`, `geometricFailuresFrom`, `fillGeometricFailures`, `fillGeometricFailuresFrom`, and `GeometricFailures` reusable sampler without changing existing `Geometric` semantics. | Adopted for compatibility with `rand_distr` semantics and to avoid a silent off-by-one migration trap. |
| StandardGeometric p=0.5 fast path | Local `rand_distr::StandardGeometric` uses repeated `leading_zeros()` on `u64` words | Added `standardGeometric`, `standardGeometricFrom`, `fillStandardGeometric`, `fillStandardGeometricFrom`, and `StandardGeometric`. Local rows: facade about 748-758M samples/s, scalar direct about 1263M, and direct-source fill about 994M, compared with Rust around 913M. | Adopted for rand_distr semantic coverage and high-volume p=0.5 failure-count sampling. |
| Geometric parameter accessors | `Geometric` and `GeometricFailures` store the success probability and encode simple closed-form moments/mode/support bounds but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Geometric.probabilityValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, `maxValue`, and matching `GeometricFailures` methods, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| StandardGeometric parameter accessor | `StandardGeometric` is the fixed `p=0.5` failure-count sampler but did not expose that semantic probability, moments, mode, or support bounds for diagnostics | Added `StandardGeometric.probabilityValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `modeValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for fixed-parameter sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Binomial-family parameter accessors | `Binomial` and `NegativeBinomial` store user parameters, simple closed-form moments, and support bounds but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Binomial.trialsValue`, `probabilityValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, `maxValue`, plus `NegativeBinomial.successesValue`, `probabilityValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, and `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Poisson and Hypergeometric parameter accessors | `Poisson` and `Hypergeometric` store user parameters, closed-form moments, and support bounds but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Poisson.lambdaValue`, `expectedValue`, `varianceValue`, `minValue`, `maxValue`, plus `Hypergeometric.populationValue` / `successesValue` / `drawsValue` / `expectedValue` / `varianceValue` / `minValue` / `maxValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Multinomial parameter accessors | `Multinomial` stores trials, category probabilities, category count, and their total but did not expose them, safe single/bulk normalized-probability lookup, or theoretical count moments for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Multinomial.trialsValue`, `probabilitiesValue`, `probabilityAt`, `normalizedProbabilityAt`, `normalizedProbabilities`, `normalizedProbabilitiesInto`, `expectedCountAt`, `expectedCounts`, `expectedCountsInto`, `varianceAt`, `variances`, `variancesInto`, `covarianceAt`, `covariances`, `covariancesInto`, `categoryCountValue`, and `totalProbabilityValue`, with unit coverage. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Discrete bulk fill APIs | Core discrete distributions only exposed single-sample helpers or reusable sampler calls | Added distribution-layer `fill` / `fillFrom` APIs for Bernoulli, Binomial, NegativeBinomial, Hypergeometric, Poisson, and Geometric. Bernoulli fill reuses packed `Rng.fillChanceFrom` for p=1/2 and p=1/4. Local rows: `fillBernoulli p=0.25` about 2.83B samples/s; direct-source fills are about 68M Poisson, 128M GeometricFailures, 126M one-based Geometric, 49M Binomial, 25M NegativeBinomial, and 23M Hypergeometric samples/s. | Adopted for API consistency, allocation-free bulk workflows, and packed Bernoulli coverage; direct-source fills are useful high-volume paths, while facade fills mostly track repeated cached sampling. |
| Hypergeometric HIN inverse-transform fast path | Local Rust uses HIN/H2PE algorithms and outperformed Alea's simple draw loop on `(100, 30, 10)` | Added a cached HIN inverse-transform method for parameter regimes where the mode is close to the lower bound, keeping the draw-loop fallback elsewhere. Local row improved from about 20M to about 51M samples/s; fill rows are about 48-49M. | Adopted. This closes the current benchmarked Hypergeometric gap without taking on full H2PE complexity yet. |
| Hypergeometric H2PE port attempt | Tried porting local `rand_distr`'s rejection/acceptance H2PE path for large-parameter Hypergeometric workloads | The initial production port was rejected after the full benchmark showed no large-row win. A later isolated `hypergeo-h2pe-probe` iteration reached about 24.8M samples/s and was then integrated into production. | Superseded by the adopted production H2PE path. |
| Hypergeometric H2PE production path | Large-parameter Hypergeometric was still using the slow draw-loop path | Added H2PE rejection/acceptance sampling for large-mode regimes while keeping HIN for small-mode regimes. Local rows: small `(100,30,10)` remains around 50-51M samples/s; large `(5000,2500,500)` improves from about 0.3M to about 20.7M facade/fill and about 23.1M direct-source fill. | Adopted. This closes the current large Hypergeometric benchmark gap without regressing the HIN row. |
| Uniform parameter accessors | `Uniform(T)` stores range endpoints and inclusive/exclusive mode but did not expose them for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `lowValue`, `highValue`, and `isInclusive`, with unit coverage for exclusive and inclusive constructors. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Uniform moment accessors | `Uniform(T)` exposed endpoints and inclusivity but not theoretical range mean/variance diagnostics | Added `expectedValue` and `varianceValue`, returning `f64` for integer discrete ranges and `T` for floating continuous ranges, with unit coverage for exclusive, inclusive, and float constructors. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Direct reusable continuous/tail sampler methods | Several reusable continuous and tail samplers previously only exposed `sample(rng)` | `Uniform`, `Triangular`, `Cauchy`, `Pareto`, `Weibull`, `Gumbel`, `Frechet`, `Pert`, `Zipf`, and `Zeta` now expose `sampleFrom`; matching single-shot `From` helpers were added where absent. Representative local rows: triangular direct about 216M vs 188M facade, gumbel direct about 72M vs 66M facade, zipf direct about 15.4M vs 14.9M facade. | Adopted for API consistency and direct-engine workflows; benchmark representative paths instead of duplicating every distribution row. |
| Zipf/Zeta reusable sampler fill APIs | `Zipf(T)` and `Zeta(T)` had direct `sampleFrom` but no reusable bulk fill methods | Added `Zipf(T).fill` / `fillFrom` and `Zeta(T).fill` / `fillFrom`, with unit coverage and benchmark rows. Local rows: `Zipf.fillFrom direct` about 15.5M samples/s, matching `zipf direct`; `Zeta.fillFrom direct` about 34.9M, near `zeta direct` about 35.7M. | Adopted for API consistency and allocation-free tail-distribution bulk workflows; performance tracks existing direct reusable sampling. |
| Zipf/Zeta parameter accessors | Tail samplers store derived constants but did not expose user-facing rank/exponent parameters for reusable-sampler diagnostics | Added `Zipf(T).nValue`, `Zipf(T).exponentValue`, and `Zeta(T).exponentValue`, with unit coverage. `Zipf.nValue` returns `null` for the infinite-exponent degenerate sampler because the stored constants collapse all samples to rank 1 and do not retain the finite input `n`. | Adopted for Zig-native parameter introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Zipf/Zeta support-bound accessors | Tail samplers expose parameter diagnostics but did not explicitly expose support bounds for configuration echo and checks | Added `Zipf(T).minValue` / `maxValue` and `Zeta(T).minValue` / `maxValue`, with `Zeta.maxValue` returning `null` for the unbounded upper tail. Unit coverage checks finite Zipf and unbounded Zeta support. | Adopted for reusable-sampler introspection; this is not a throughput change. |
| Direct-source float basis helpers | Distribution-layer direct helpers duplicated open/open-closed float construction | `Rng.floatOpenFrom`, `floatOpenClosedFrom`, and `floatRangeFrom` are public direct-source helpers; distribution helpers now reuse them instead of local private copies | Adopted for API consistency and for future distribution/SIMD work; this is a foundation cleanup, not a standalone speed claim. |
| Scalar open/open-closed float fill APIs | Strict open/open-closed float bulk generation required `fillSample(Open01)` or a manual loop | Added `fillOpen`, `fillOpenFrom`, `fillOpenClosed`, and `fillOpenClosedFrom`. Local rows: `fillOpen f32` about 759M samples/s and `fillOpenClosed f32` about 777M samples/s, versus ordinary `fill f32` about 839M samples/s. | Adopted for high-volume strict-interval workflows; slower than ordinary `[0, 1)` fill because it preserves stronger interval semantics. |
| `f32` uniform vector and bulk fill path | `fill(f32)`, `fillOpen(f32)`, and `fillOpenClosed(f32)` used scalar slice loops even though vector `f32x8` generation had stronger throughput | Bulk `f32` fill/open/open-closed paths now generate `@Vector(8, f32)` chunks and keep scalar tails. Local rows: `fill f32` improved from about 844M to about 1158M samples/s, `fillOpen f32` from about 760M to about 1146M, and `fillOpenClosed f32` from about 785M to about 1136M. | Adopted as SIMD/vector foundation work for high-volume uniform float slices. |
| Vector open/open-closed float helpers | Vector APIs had `[0, 1)` float generation but no direct open or open-closed vector counterparts | Added `vectorOpen`, `vectorOpenClosed`, `vectorOpenFrom`, `vectorOpenClosedFrom`, and vector-slice fill helpers for float vectors. Packed `f32` lane generation avoids the initial scalar-per-lane cost and fixes strict-open `f32` zero/NaN edge cases. `vectorbench` rows are about 1.04B lanes/s for open/open-closed `f32x8`, with range `f32x8` about 1.01B lanes/s on the latest run. | Adopted as SIMD/vector API coverage and a foundation for distribution kernels that need strict open intervals. |
| `f64` uniform vector and bulk fill path | `f64` vector uniform/open/open-closed/range generation previously used scalar-per-lane construction, and main throughput rows only tracked `f32` float fill/range | Added vectorized `f64` lane conversion for `vector`, `vectorOpen`, and `vectorOpenClosed`, use it for `fill f64`, `fillOpen f64`, `fillOpenClosed f64`, and `fillRange f64`, and added `bench`/`vectorbench` rows. Latest vectorbench rows: `fillVector f64x4` about 722M lanes/s, `fillVectorOpen f64x4` about 721M, `fillVectorOpenClosed f64x4` about 531M, and `fillVectorRange f64x4` about 694M, versus the pre-change scalar-lane baseline about 503M. | Adopted as SIMD/vector foundation work and benchmark coverage for `f64` uniform bulk paths; distribution-kernel gaps remain open. |
| f64 bitcast uniform generation | f64 `[0, 1)` generation used integer-to-float conversion and multiplication, trailing Rust scalar float evidence | Previously switched ordinary `f64` uniform and vector/range f64 paths to exponent-bit construction. Local rows improved at the time, but the mapping used only 52 random bits. | Superseded by S4-M1223. Correctness now requires the local Rust/StandardUniform 53-bit `n / 2^53` grid across facade, direct, bulk, iterator/distribution-fill, and vector ordinary f64 paths; performance recovery is a follow-up, not a reason to keep the 52-bit mapping. |
| 53-bit f64 bitcast uniform attempt | Tried to preserve the 53-bit `n / 2^53` grid by adding the discarded low precision bit back as a half-ULP after exponent-bit construction | `fill f64` dropped from about 884M to about 729M samples/s, `fillRange f64` dropped from about 827M to about 688M, and scalar direct rows also fell below Rust float/range evidence. | Historical rejected expression only. S4-M1223 later adopted the straightforward 53-bit conversion as a correctness fix after finding facade/direct/bulk/vector grid inconsistency; future work should optimize without changing that grid. |
| f64 Open01 bitcast generation | f64 strict-open `(0, 1)` used integer-to-float conversion and multiplication, trailing Rust `Open01 f64` evidence | Switched f64 Open01 scalar/vector paths to exponent-bit construction with half-ULP offset. Local rows: `fillOpen f64` improved from about 568M to about 884M samples/s and `fillVectorOpen f64x4` from about 523M to about 722M lanes/s. | Adopted. Strict open-closed f64 still uses the multiply-plus-one path because it must include 1.0 exactly. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` scalar loop | Vector `@floatFromInt` conversion made bulk `(0, 1]` f64 slower than scalar sampling | Replaced the vector-slice implementation with scalar `floatOpenClosedFrom` lane fill. Local row improved from about 581M to about 716M samples/s. | Superseded by the raw-word byte-fill conversion below; this remains useful history for why vector conversion is not used. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` raw-word byte-fill conversion | The scalar loop still trailed Rust `OpenClosed01 f64` around 779M samples/s | For sources that expose byte `fill`, bulk generation now fills a temporary `u64` word buffer and applies the exact `(0, 1]` conversion from those raw words. Local row improved from about 713-716M to about 770M samples/s while keeping checksum/mean in the expected range. Follow-ups retested temporary word buffer sizes, source-type splits, integer-add/division/complement/native/narrow conversions, and then switched the exact conversion to `@mulAdd(f64, float(raw >> 11), 2^-53, 2^-53)` after `open-closed-probe` showed a durable win. The main throughput harness splits facade, FastPrng direct, and ScalarPrng direct rows; repeated 1GiB focused rows after the `@mulAdd` update are about 792M/792M/793M versus Rust `OpenClosed01 f64` about 778M in the same fresh run. | Adopted. This preserves exact `(0, 1]` semantics and now exceeds local Rust evidence for the focused Linux workload. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` smaller raw-word buffers | Retested whether smaller temporary raw-word buffers beat the adopted 128-word exact `(0, 1]` conversion shape | Extended `open-closed-probe` with 64- and 96-word facade/direct rows. Fresh `open-closed-probe -- 4194304` reruns show facade raw 64/96/128 all clustered around 777-778M and direct raw 64/96/128 around 776-778M, within noise and with matching checksums. | Evidence only. Keep the simpler adopted 128-word buffer; smaller buffers do not provide a durable win. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` fresh focused reruns | Refreshed exact `(0, 1]` f64 evidence after unrelated system/load changes made older 1GiB rows stale/noisy | An earlier fresh rerun temporarily showed Alea facade/direct/ScalarPrng-direct about 653M/631M/649M versus Rust `OpenClosed01 f64` about 685M, but the current 2026-07-03 same-host 1GiB focused rerun restores the adopted ordering: `bench -- 1073741824 "fillOpenClosed f64"` reports Alea facade/direct/ScalarPrng-direct about 791.6M/791.0M/795.9M versus `rand OpenClosed01 f64` about 779.0M in the same focused shape. | Closed for the current Linux focused row. The exact `@mulAdd` raw-word path remains the production default and still exceeds local Rust evidence; keep `openclosed-endpoint-notes.md` for future portability/regression checks. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` int-add conversion expression | Tried the equivalent exact-endpoint conversion `float((raw >> 11) + 1) * 2^-53` instead of `float(raw >> 11) + 1.0` before scaling | Extended `open-closed-probe` with facade/direct int-add 128-word rows. Fresh reruns showed int-add 128 tied with the old float-plus-one expression and later below the adopted `@mulAdd` conversion, with matching checksums. | Rejected as a production default. Keep the current `@mulAdd` expression; int-add does not provide a durable win. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` division conversion expression | Tried replacing multiply by the reciprocal `2^-53` with division by `2^53` while preserving the exact `(0, 1]` grid | Extended `open-closed-probe` with facade/direct division rows. Fresh `open-closed-probe -- 4194304` shows div 128 around 776M, tied with the adopted multiply expression and with matching checksums. | Evidence only. Keep the current multiply-by-reciprocal expression; division does not provide a durable win. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` Rust source audit | Checked local `~/Work/rand/src/distr/float.rs` to confirm whether Rust uses a materially different OpenClosed01 algorithm | Rust `OpenClosed01` also uses the multiply-based 53-bit method for f64: take the high 53 bits, add one, cast to float, and multiply by `2^-53`. This matches the int-add expression already tested in `open-closed-probe`. | Evidence only. The remaining exact `(0, 1]` gap is not an algorithm-family mismatch versus local Rust; keep looking for codegen/throughput improvements that preserve the same endpoint grid. |
| f64 OpenClosed01 bitcast attempt | Tried a bitcast-based `(0, 1]` implementation preserving 1.0 through a select branch | Scalar `floatOpenClosed f64` dropped from about 1040M to about 860M samples/s and bulk `fillOpenClosed f64` dropped from the scalar-loop row around 716M to about 593M. | Rejected. Keep the scalar-loop bulk path until a faster exact `(0, 1]` construction is found. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` invalid byte-buffer read experiment | Tried filling a raw byte buffer and reading `u64` words out of it before conversion | The row appeared to jump to about 1.22B samples/s, but the checksum implied an invalid mean around 0.046 instead of near 0.5. | Rejected as invalid despite speed; benchmark checksums must stay in the expected distribution range. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` in-place/raw-vector/unrolled conversions | Tried converting the destination memory in place, vectorizing the raw-word conversion, and manually unrolling the adopted temporary-word conversion | In-place conversion dropped to about 680M samples/s; vector/manual unrolled conversion dropped to about 685M; older scalar-loop unrolling dropped to about 579M. | Rejected. Keep the simple temporary `u64` word buffer loop until a faster exact `(0, 1]` construction is found. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` complement-grid conversion | Tried the exact endpoint-equivalent complement forms `1.0 - float(raw >> 11) * 2^-53` and `float(2^53 - (raw >> 11)) * 2^-53` in `open-closed-probe` | Fresh `open-closed-probe -- 4194304` rows show facade complement/int-sub 128 about 723M/623M and direct complement/int-sub 128 about 612M/746M, below the adopted raw 64/128 rows around 775-778M. Checksums shift as expected because the mapping reverses the endpoint grid while preserving the same mean. | Rejected. Keep the current float-plus-one raw-word conversion; complement forms do not provide a throughput win. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` native-endian probe | Tried skipping the explicit `littleToNative(u64, raw_word)` normalization in the local x86_64 OpenClosed probe to check whether byte-order conversion was a measurable cost | Fresh `open-closed-probe -- 134217728` rows show facade raw native 128 about 609M and direct raw native 128 about 637M, below the corresponding endian-normalized raw 128 rows about 652M and 641M, with matching checksums on this host. | Rejected. Keep explicit endian normalization; it is portable and not the current bottleneck. |
| `fillOpenClosed(f64)` narrow integer conversion | Tried narrowing `raw >> 11` to `u53` / `u54` before `@floatFromInt` to check whether smaller integer types improved exact endpoint-grid codegen | Fresh `open-closed-probe -- 134217728` rows show facade narrow/narrow-add 128 about 615M/588M and direct narrow/narrow-add 128 about 616M/606M, below raw 96/128 rows around 655-667M with matching checksums. | Rejected. Keep the current `u64` shift-plus-one conversion; narrow integer coercions do not improve throughput. |
| Float range fill single-pass vector scaling | `fillRange(f32/f64)` generated `[0, 1)` values first and scaled the slice in a second pass | `fillRange(f32/f64)` now generates vector chunks and applies `min + width * uniform` immediately. Local rows: `fillRange f32` improved from about 691M to about 1069M samples/s; `fillRange f64` improved from about 517M to about 569M samples/s. | Adopted as bulk range/SIMD foundation work; scalar distribution gaps remain open. |
| Inline normal ziggurat tail helper | Tried marking `normalZigguratZeroCase` inline to match Rust's inlined zero-case closure shape | No visible normal/exponential win: normal direct rows stayed around 414-418M samples/s and exponential direct rows around 414-420M. This matches `ziggurat-stats`, where normal tail is only about 0.026%. | Rejected. Keep tail helper non-inline and focus on the hot ziggurat path. |
| Normal ziggurat tail open interval | Rust samples the normal ziggurat tail with `Open01`; Alea used ordinary half-open `floatFrom`, which could theoretically feed `log(0)` in a forced tail path | Switched `normalZigguratZeroCase` to `floatOpenFrom` and added a deterministic regression source that forces the zero-case with raw zero draws while still producing a finite result. Local normal hot-path rows are unchanged: `standard-normal scalar direct` remains about 449M samples/s. | Adopted as a correctness and Rust-semantics fix, not a speed change. |
| Probability bool bulk/vector sampling | No direct Rust row; fills a Zig-native API gap around repeated `chance(p)` workloads | Added `chanceFrom`, `fillChance`, `fillChanceFrom`, `vectorChance`/`vectorChanceFrom`, and vector-slice chance fills; p=0.25 uses packed two-bit generation at about 2.85B samples/s, 4.69B single-vector lanes/s, and 3.68B vector-slice lanes/s; p=0.5 uses packed bool generation around 3.0B samples/s and 9.8B lanes/s | Adopted for ergonomic high-volume probability sampling and vector API coverage. |
| Exact ratio bulk/vector sampling | No direct Rust row; fills a Zig-native API gap around repeated `ratio(n, d)` workloads | Added `ratioFrom`, `fillRatio`, `fillRatioFrom`, `vectorRatio`/`vectorRatioFrom`, and vector-slice ratio fills; power-of-two denominators use packed bit chunks. Local rows: 1/4 about 2.85B samples/s and 4.69B lanes/s, 3/8 about 2.1B samples/s and 3.87B lanes/s, 1/2 about 3.0B samples/s and 9.85B lanes/s | Adopted for exact high-volume probability sampling without converting ratios to floats. |
| Direct dynamic weighted-tree sampling | `WeightedTree.sampleFrom` was about 46-48M ops/s and `WeightedIntTree.sampleFrom` about 50-52M ops/s in update+sample microbenchmarks | Adopted for API consistency and direct-engine workflows; performance is roughly neutral for generic tree and comparable for integer tree. Empty/single-root push/pop paths, get-after-empty errors, empty-tree update failures, zero-length checked fills, single-root and single-positive no-consume sample/fill, and allocation-returning/caller-buffer weight export are covered for both generic and integer trees; failed `WeightedTree` push/update operations leave the previous total usable; failed `WeightedIntTree` push/update operations preserve the previous total as well; and `WeightedIntTree` rejects unsigned weights that do not fit its `u64` subtotal accumulator. |
| Direct static weighted sampling | Rust `WeightedIndex` row covers repeated weighted-index sampling | `AliasTable.sampleFrom` plus `WeightedChoice.sampleFrom` / `sampleValueFrom` add direct-source O(1) static weighted sampling paths; local rows are about 126M samples/s for facade calls and about 122-125M for direct-source calls. Checked `Rng.weightedIndex` paths now reject non-finite weights and overflowing cumulative totals before consuming randomness, and single-positive-weight slices/tables return that deterministic index without consuming randomness. `AliasTable` exposes the cached `constantIndex`, so `WeightedChoice` sample/fill/value-fill methods can direct-fill single-positive point masses without consuming randomness. | Adopted for static weighted-choice ergonomics, validation, and performance evidence; current direct path is API-quality work, not a speed win. |
| Static weighted sampler fill/update APIs | Static repeated weighted sampling had direct single-sample paths but no allocation-free bulk fill methods on the reusable samplers, and `WeightedChoice` could not update its underlying alias table without rebuilding the whole sampler | Added `AliasTable.fill` / `fillFrom`, `WeightedTree.fill` / checked fill variants, and `WeightedIntTree.fill` / checked fill variants. Bulk fill now validates and caches the total weight once per slice. `WeightedChoice.update` forwards to `AliasTable.update` after length validation, so item mappings can keep their slice while weights are replaced; failed length, weight, and initial allocation updates leave the previous valid table usable. Local rows: alias fill direct about 127M samples/s, weighted-tree fill direct about 73M, and weighted-int-tree fill direct about 90M. | Adopted for high-volume static weighted sampling and reusable weighted-choice ergonomics; dynamic tree update+sample remains a separate benchmark shape. |
| Static weighted alias f64 Rust comparison | The Linux audit maps local `rand_distr::weighted::WeightedAliasIndex` to Alea `AliasTable`, but previous benchmark rows only compared `rand::WeightedIndex` and Alea integer alias-table rows | Added focused Rust `rand_distr weighted alias f64` and matching Alea `AliasTable(f64)` rows. Local Rust is about 146-147M samples/s. Initial Alea rows were about 126.6M facade, 132.3M direct, and 127.1M direct fill. After adopting a power-of-two one-word sampling fast path for tables up to 2048 entries plus precomputed 53-bit probability thresholds, latest focused FastPrng rows are about 163.1M facade, 162.1M direct, and 163.3M direct fill, while ScalarPrng rows are about 208.3M sample and 200.8M fill. | Closed for f64 alias sampling versus local Rust `WeightedAliasIndex<f64>`; both FastPrng and ScalarPrng rows exceed Rust, with ScalarPrng best for scalar-heavy alias sampling. |
| Static weighted alias u32 Rust comparison | Added a separate `rand_distr::WeightedAliasIndex<u32>` row after the f64 alias comparison revealed the need for type-specific evidence | Focused Rust `rand_distr weighted alias u32` is about 180.5M samples/s. The same Alea power-of-two one-word threshold path lifts `AliasTable(u32)` FastPrng rows to about 160.3M facade, 162.7M direct, and 151.2M direct fill in the latest focused run, while ScalarPrng direct rows are about 206.4M sample and 186.4M fill. A compact `u32` alias-cache follow-up regressed u32 FastPrng rows and was reverted. | Closed for scalar-heavy/static alias workflows: `AliasTable(u32)` with `ScalarPrng` exceeds local Rust `WeightedAliasIndex<u32>` evidence. FastPrng remains lower for this scalar-heavy sampler, so prefer `ScalarPrng` for high-volume static alias sampling when byte-fill throughput is not the bottleneck. |
| Static weighted alias f32 Rust comparison | Added a separate `rand_distr::WeightedAliasIndex<f32>` row to complete float alias evidence alongside f64 | Focused Rust `rand_distr weighted alias f32` is about 145.5M samples/s. Alea `AliasTable(f32)` focused rows are about 179.4M facade, 177.5M FastPrng direct, 209.5M ScalarPrng direct, 168.0M FastPrng direct fill, and 200.3M ScalarPrng direct fill. | Closed. Alea exceeds local Rust f32 alias evidence across facade/direct/fill profiles. |
| AliasTable u32 column draw attempt | Rust `WeightedAliasIndex` samples its column as `u32`, while Alea sampled the column with a `usize` bounded draw, so tried using a u32 bounded draw when the table length fits u32 | Focused `bench -- 268435456 "alias table"` after the u32-column-only change showed no win and changed checksums: f64 direct was about 129.9M versus the previous focused row about 132.3M, and integer rows also stayed below/near prior rows. A later compact `u32` alias-cache attempt on top of the one-word path also regressed u32 rows. | Rejected and reverted. The adopted power-of-two one-word path is distinct: it uses disjoint low bits for the column and high bits for the probability threshold, reducing two RNG draws to one for small power-of-two tables. |
| Alias table introspection | Local Rust weighted alias/index samplers expose reconstructed weights, single-weight lookup, and total-weight style diagnostics, while Alea's O(1) alias table only exposed sampling/fill/update methods | Added `AliasTable.len`, `isEmpty`, `totalWeight`, allocation-free `weightAt`, allocation-returning `weights`, and caller-buffer `weightsInto`. Unit coverage checks single-weight and bulk weight reconstruction before and after updates, length validation, invalid indexes, and allocation failure. | Adopted for reusable weighted-sampler diagnostics and parity-plus ergonomics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Static weighted probability lookup/export | Static weighted samplers exposed weights and total weights but not normalized per-index probabilities for configuration echo/debugging | Added `AliasTable.probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, `probabilitiesInto`, plus `WeightedChoice.probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, and `probabilitiesInto`, with bounds-checking and length-checking unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable weighted-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Weighted tree single-weight alias | Dynamic weighted trees exposed `get(index)` while other weighted samplers use `weightAt(index)` for single-weight diagnostics | Added `WeightedTree.weightAt` and `WeightedIntTree.weightAt` as naming-consistent aliases, with bounds-checking unit coverage. | Adopted for weighted-sampler introspection consistency; this is not a throughput change. |
| Dynamic weighted probability lookup/export | Dynamic weighted trees exposed weights and total weights but not normalized per-index probabilities for configuration echo/debugging | Added `WeightedTree.probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, `probabilitiesInto`, plus `WeightedIntTree.probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, and `probabilitiesInto`, with bounds-checking, length-checking, and invalid-total unit coverage. | Adopted for reusable weighted-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Checked weighted no-replacement counts | Weighted no-replacement helpers returned partial results when fewer positive-weight entries existed than the requested amount | Added checked `sampleWeightedIndices*` / `sampleWeighted*` variants that require enough positive-weight entries for `amount > 0`; zero-count calls return empty results before validating weights. Invalid count and all-zero-weight tests cover no-consume behavior, and single-positive-weight no-replacement calls now return the only possible item/index without consuming randomness. | Adopted for fallible user-supplied weighted sampling without changing the existing clamp/partial-result helpers. |
| Checked discrete distribution fill helpers | Public distribution-layer bulk fills preserved assert-fast paths but user-supplied parameters still required manual sampler construction for fallible bulk workflows | Added checked direct-source bulk helpers for Bernoulli, Binomial, NegativeBinomial, Hypergeometric, Poisson, Geometric, and GeometricFailures, with tests for valid fills and invalid parameters. | Adopted for ergonomics and safety; this is an API consistency improvement rather than a throughput change. |
| Checked core continuous distribution fill helpers | Core continuous/derived distribution-layer fills also preserved assert-fast paths but user-supplied parameters still required manual sampler construction for fallible bulk workflows | Added checked direct-source bulk helpers for LogNormal, HalfNormal, Gamma, ChiSquared, Chi, Erlang, Beta, FisherF, and StudentT, with tests for valid fills and invalid parameters. | Adopted for ergonomics and safety; this is an API consistency improvement rather than a throughput change. |
| Checked tail/bounded distribution fill helpers | Tail and bounded distribution-layer fills had the same fallible bulk workflow boilerplate gap | Added checked direct-source bulk helpers for Triangular, Arcsine, Cauchy, Laplace, Logistic, LogLogistic, Kumaraswamy, PowerFunction, Rayleigh, Maxwell, Pareto, Weibull, Gumbel, Frechet, SkewNormal, and PERT, with tests for valid fills and invalid parameters. | Adopted for ergonomics and safety; this is an API consistency improvement rather than a throughput change. |
