| Checked inverse-Gaussian-family fill helpers | InverseGaussian and NormalInverseGaussian bulk fills had the same fallible workflow boilerplate gap after their staged/vectorized production fills were added | Added `fillInverseGaussianChecked` / `fillInverseGaussianCheckedFrom` and `fillNormalInverseGaussianChecked` / `fillNormalInverseGaussianCheckedFrom`, with tests for valid fills and invalid parameters. | Adopted for ergonomics and safety; this is an API consistency improvement rather than a throughput change. |
| Zipf/Zeta top-level fill helpers | Zipf and Zeta reusable samplers exposed `fill` / `fillFrom`, but there were no module-level bulk helpers matching the rest of the distribution surface | Added `fillZipf` / `fillZipfFrom` / checked variants and `fillZeta` / `fillZetaFrom` / checked variants, with tests for valid fills and invalid parameters. | Adopted for API consistency; this is a surface-completeness improvement rather than a throughput change. |
| Distribution-level normal/exponential fill wrappers | `Rng` exposed bulk normal/exponential helpers, but the distributions module only exposed reusable sampler fills and single-sample top-level helpers | Added `fillNormal` / `fillNormalFrom` / checked variants and `fillExponential` / `fillExponentialFrom` / checked variants as module-level wrappers over the existing optimized `Rng` bulk paths. | Adopted for namespace consistency; throughput remains governed by the existing benchmarked `Rng` kernels. |
| Distribution-level uniform fill wrappers | The distributions module exposed single-shot `uniform` / `uniformInclusive` and reusable `Uniform(T).fill`, while `Rng` exposed the corresponding optimized bulk range fills | Added `fillUniform` / `fillUniformFrom` / checked variants and `fillUniformInclusive` / `fillUniformInclusiveFrom` / checked variants for integer and floating-point ranges. | Adopted for namespace consistency; ordinary exclusive range throughput remains governed by the existing benchmarked `Rng.fillRangeFrom` path. |
| Distribution-namespace vector wrappers | Stage 4 vector ergonomics exposed `Rng` vector kernels but distribution users still had to leave the distribution namespace for high-volume vector uniform, strict-interval, normal, and exponential workflows | Added `vectorUniform*`, `fillVectorUniform*`, inclusive variants, `vectorStandardNormal*`, `fillVectorStandardNormal*`, `vectorNormal*`, `fillVectorNormal*`, `vectorStandardExponential*`, `fillVectorStandardExponential*`, `vectorExponential*`, and `fillVectorExponential*`; `Open01` and `OpenClosed01` now sample/fill float vector types as reusable strict-interval samplers, and `VectorUniform`, `VectorStandardNormal`, `VectorNormal`, `VectorStandardExponential`, and `VectorExponential` add reusable vector sampler forms. Unit tests cover facade/direct stream shape, support, invalid-parameter no-consume behavior, and zero-length checked fills. `vectorbench` now includes distribution-namespace wrapper rows; direct rows route to the existing kernels rather than introducing a new SIMD distribution kernel. | Adopted for API ergonomics and namespace consistency; throughput remains governed by the existing vector kernels and facade/direct context shape. |
| Distribution-namespace vector Bernoulli | Probability vector kernels existed under `Rng.vectorChance` / `fillVectorChance`, but distribution users had no Bernoulli-shaped bool-vector sampler or checked wrapper | Added `vectorBernoulli*`, `fillVectorBernoulli*`, checked variants, and reusable `VectorBernoulli(VectorType)` for bool vectors. Unit tests cover facade/direct stream shape, p=0/1 fast paths, invalid-probability no-consume behavior, and zero-length checked fills. Fresh `vectorbench` rows on this host show `fillVectorBernoulli boolx64 p=0.25` about 3.82B lanes/s, direct about 3.87B lanes/s, and reusable `VectorBernoulli.fill` about 3.87B lanes/s, matching the underlying packed probability kernels. | Adopted for distribution-namespace vector probability ergonomics; this is not a new dense SIMD distribution kernel. |

| Bounded f32 LogNormal approximation | The exact LogNormal f32 transform gap remained after wrapper/codegen and vector-width attempts, while `expm1(x) + 1` was faster only in the narrow `stddev=0.25` profile and changed rounding | Added opt-in `logNormalApproxF32*`, `fillLogNormalApproxF32*`, and reusable `LogNormalApproxF32` APIs using `expm1(x) + 1`, checked to `|mean| <= 0.25` and `stddev <= 0.25`. Exact `LogNormal(f32)` remains unchanged. Unit tests cover positivity, checked invalid/no-consume behavior, zero-length no-validate behavior, and a deterministic <=1 ULP comparison for the benchmark profile; `distcheck` now includes a separate-seed `log-normal-approx-f32` mean gate so the approximation does not perturb the shared continuous-distribution stream. Fresh `log-normal-probe -- 1048576` rows show f32 current/approx fill around 138.6M/142.8M FastPrng and 143.3M/149.6M ScalarPrng, with the same checksum for the narrow profile; the f32 error scan reports max 1 ULP at `stddev=0.25`, but still shows max 51 ULP at `stddev=1.0` and 8028 ULP at `stddev=2.0`. Focused throughput rows show approx single-sample about 91.8M facade and 121.9M scalar direct, plus approx fill about 131.2M facade, 138.0M FastPrng direct, and 151.3M ScalarPrng direct. | Adopted as an explicit approximation path only; this does not close the exact `@exp` transform/codegen watch item. |
| Bounded f32 LogNormal exp2 approximation | The `exp2(x * log2e)` transform is not exact-default material but probe rows show it is a fast f32 approximation with small observed ULP error over a wider profile than the `expm1` approximation | Added opt-in `logNormalExp2F32*`, `fillLogNormalExp2F32*`, vector helpers, and reusable `LogNormalExp2F32` / `VectorLogNormalExp2F32` APIs using `exp2(x * log2e)`, checked to `|mean| <= 2` and `stddev <= 2`. Exact `LogNormal(f32)` remains unchanged. Unit tests cover stable snapshots, facade/direct vector stream shape, and invalid/no-consume behavior; `distcheck` now includes a `log-normal-exp2-f32` mean gate. Probe evidence shows staged f32 exp2 around 192M FastPrng and 205M ScalarPrng for `stddev=0.25`, with changed outputs and observed max ULP about 1/4/7 at `stddev=0.25/1/2`, plus max ULP 7-8 in `mean=+/-2,stddev=2` scans. Focused throughput rows show exp2 f32 single-sample about 163M facade and 183M ScalarPrng direct, exp2 f32 fill about 191M facade, 193M FastPrng direct, and 212M ScalarPrng direct. After routing vector-slice fills through the packed scalar f32 bulk path, focused vectorbench rows are about 211M f32x8 facade/direct/reusable lanes/s with matching checksums. | Adopted as an explicit approximation path only. This broadens opt-in fast f32 LogNormal coverage but does not close the exact `@exp` transform/codegen watch item. |

| WeightedTree inline traversal probe | Generic `WeightedTree.fillFrom` remained slower than the unsigned integer tree, so tried inlining the sample traversal over `subtotals.items` in an isolated probe | Historical `weighted-tree-probe` showed current fill about 73.8M samples/s and inline traversal about 71.3M. A fresh rerun showed current about 71.1M and inline about 71.9M with identical checksum, while the main benchmark reports generic tree update+sample around 48M ops/s and fill around 72M samples/s. | Rejected for now. Results are mixed/noisy and do not close the generic float tree gap; keep the existing `sampleCheckedFrom` traversal until a durable update+sample and fill win appears. |
| WeightedTree update+sample inline traversal probe | The prior inline traversal check only covered static fill, so tested dynamic update+sample rows that sample immediately after each update and can reuse the post-update total | Extended `weighted-tree-probe` with current, total-cached, and inline update+sample rows. A focused `weighted-tree-probe -- 16777216` run shows current about 47.3M ops/s, total-cached about 47.6M, and inline about 47.8M with matching checksums; the same run still has static fill current about 72.0M versus inline traversal about 70.5M. | Evidence only. The dynamic update+sample movement is too small to justify duplicating traversal code in production, especially since the fill path still regresses; keep the existing `sampleCheckedFrom` traversal. |
| Choice / WeightedChoice fill APIs | Sequence reusable samplers exposed single-sample and iterator workflows but no allocation-free bulk fill methods | Added `Choice.sampleFrom`, `sampleValueFrom`, `fill`, `fillFrom`, `fillValues`, `fillValuesFrom`, plus `WeightedChoice.fill` / `fillFrom` / value-fill variants. Checked `Choice` / `chooseIter` constructors now return `error.EmptyInput` for user-supplied empty slices while preserving the original optional/null forms, single-item `Choice` paths are covered as direct no-consume point masses, and single-item `Choice` pointer/value fills now direct-fill without looping through the sampler; `WeightedChoice.update` keeps item mappings while replacing weights through the alias table. Local row: `weighted choice fill direct` about 129M samples/s, matching direct single-value sampling. | Adopted for high-volume repeated collection sampling and API consistency with distribution samplers. |
| Choice item introspection | Sequence reusable samplers retained their item slices but only exposed length and sampling methods, so diagnostics required users to keep separate item references | Added `Choice.isEmpty`, `itemsValue`, `itemAt`, plus `WeightedChoice.isEmpty`, `itemsValue`, and `itemAt`, with bounds-checking unit coverage. | Adopted for item-level reusable-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Choice probability lookup/export | Unweighted reusable choice samplers imply uniform probabilities but did not expose per-item probabilities for configuration echo/debugging | Added `Choice.probabilityAt`, `probabilities`, and `probabilitiesInto`, with bounds-checking and length-checking unit coverage. | Adopted for item-level reusable-sampler diagnostics; this is not a throughput change. |
| WeightedChoice introspection | `AliasTable` exposes weight reconstruction and total weight, but item-level weighted choices still required users to reach into the distribution layer for diagnostics | Added `WeightedChoice.totalWeight`, allocation-free `weightAt`, allocation-returning `weights`, and caller-buffer `weightsInto`, forwarding through the internal alias table while preserving item mapping. Unit coverage checks single-weight lookup, reconstruction, length mismatch, invalid indexes, update synchronization, and allocation failure. | Adopted for item-level weighted-sampler diagnostics and parity-plus ergonomics; this is not a throughput change. |
| Compact `IndexVec` iteration/conversion | Rust `rand::seq::index::IndexVec` exposes empty checks, exact-size iteration, and conversion to a plain `Vec<usize>`, while Alea's compact index vector required manual `len` / `at` loops | Added `IndexVec.isEmpty`, linear `indexOf` / `contains`, exact-size `iter` with `Iterator.remaining`, caller-buffer `copyInto`, and allocation-returning `toOwnedSlice`, with snapshot, native-backing, allocation-failure, length-mismatch, and zero-count tests. | Adopted for sequence-sampling ergonomics without changing index sampling algorithms or stream shape. |
| Iterator sampling direct-source and checked APIs | Iterator sampling helpers still required an `Rng` facade while index sampling already had direct-source `From` variants; empty streaming choices only had optional/null forms; and iterator sampling returned partial results when the source ended early | Added `chooseIteratorFrom`, `sampleIteratorFrom`, `chooseIteratorWeightedFrom`, `sampleIteratorWeightedFrom`, plus `chooseIteratorCheckedFrom` / `chooseIteratorWeightedCheckedFrom` for fallible empty-iterator handling and `sampleIteratorCheckedFrom` / `sampleIteratorWeightedCheckedFrom` for exact-count sampling. Unit coverage includes valid stream-shape and invalid no-consume paths; single-positive weighted iterator choice/sample helpers now return the only possible item without consuming randomness. Existing direct index/weighted benchmark rows continue to compile and run. | Adopted for comptime-known engine workflows over streaming iterators without collecting first and for user-supplied iterator validation. |

| Vector standard normal/exponential API wrappers | Stage 4 vector ergonomics needed named standard-distribution helpers so high-volume vector callers do not pass magic `(mean=0, stddev=1)` or `rate=1` parameters | Added `vectorStandardNormal`, `vectorStandardNormalFrom`, `fillVectorStandardNormal`, `fillVectorStandardNormalFrom`, `vectorStandardExponential`, `vectorStandardExponentialFrom`, `fillVectorStandardExponential`, and `fillVectorStandardExponentialFrom`, plus checked single-vector `vectorNormalChecked` / `vectorExponentialChecked` for user-supplied parameters. Dedicated `vectorbench` rows now cover facade and direct standard/parameterized wrappers: the 2026-07-03 direct standard/parameterized rows are about 501M/496M normal f32x8, 454M/413M normal f64x4, 471M/470M exponential f32x8, and 468M/466M exponential f64x4 lanes/s. Uniform vector-slice rows in the same run are about 1.04B open f32x8, 1.03B open-closed f32x8, 1.01B range f32x8, 722M fill f64x4, 722M open f64x4, 533M open-closed f64x4, and 698M range f64x4 lanes/s. | Adopted for API ergonomics and checked-parameter consistency; direct rows confirm wrappers track parameterized paths and isolate facade/context overhead, but this is still not evidence of a new true-SIMD distribution kernel. |
| Parameterized vector standard-argument branch | Tried routing `vectorNormal(..., mean=0, stddev=1)` and `vectorExponential(..., rate=1)` through the raw standard vector helpers so parameterized standard calls would share the standard wrapper path | The first broad branch regressed normal facade rows badly, to about 312M f32x8 and 301M f64x4 lanes/s while direct rows stayed high, so it was reverted. A later narrower non-facade branch inside `fillVectorNormalFrom` recovered the f64 direct codegen gap without changing facade behavior: focused `vectorbench -- 67108864 "fillVectorNormal f64x4"` shows f64 direct about 454M lanes/s, matching the local scalar candidate and up from previous 413-414M evidence, while f32 direct stays about 499M. The same non-facade standard-argument branch is now applied to direct single-vector `vectorNormalFrom`; focused `bench -- 268435456 "vector normal"` rows show direct f32x8 about 622M lanes/s and direct f64x4 about 671M lanes/s. Facade rows keep the existing non-branch path. | Adopted narrowly for non-`Rng` direct vector normal single-vector and fill workflows with `mean=0,stddev=1`. Keep explicit facade standard helpers separate; the broad facade branch remains rejected. |
| Vector exponential `rate=1` direct branch | Checked whether the same narrow non-facade standard-argument branch should also route `vectorExponential(..., rate=1)` through standard-exponential helpers | Added direct single-vector standard-exponential rows and tried the branch. Focused `bench -- 268435456 "vector standard-exponential"` was noisy and did not improve: f32x8 direct was about 571M baseline versus about 541-588M with the branch depending on run order, and f64x4 direct was about 623M baseline versus about 612-623M with the branch. Focused `vectorbench -- 67108864 "StandardExponential f64x4"` also kept direct rows around 468M while a local scalar candidate trailed around 419M. | Rejected and reverted. Keep vector exponential on the existing scalar lane-fill path; retain the new direct standard-exponential benchmark rows for future evidence. |
| Approx-log f32 vector exponential opt-in | Checked whether a vector-only approximate inverse-transform path can beat scalar ziggurat lane-fill without rejected-lane repair. The adopted f32 candidate samples midpoint open-uniform lanes and approximates `-log(U)` by extracting exponent/mantissa bits, reducing the mantissa to `[sqrt(0.5), sqrt(2)]`, and applying a short atanh odd-polynomial. | Initial `vectorbench -- 16777216 "StandardExponential f32x8"` / `"fillVectorExponential f32x8"` rows show benchmark-local approx-log candidates around 543M/540M lanes/s versus direct scalar ziggurat lane-fill around 472M/472M and native-f32 ziggurat vector rows around 503M. A shorter smoke after the mantissa reduction reported f32 standard/parameterized approx-log around 560M/651M lanes/s. The public API rows are stronger in the same real harness: `vectorbench -- 16777216 "ApproxLogF32"` reports `fillVectorStandardExponentialApproxLogF32` facade/direct/reusable around 646M/652M/652M f32x8 lanes/s and `fillVectorExponentialApproxLogF32(rate=2)` facade/direct/reusable around 648M/648M/596M lanes/s. An exhaustive midpoint-grid scan over all 24-bit f32 lanes found max observed error about 4 ULP, max absolute error about `9.54e-7`, max relative error about `3.16e-7`, and mean `0.9999999819` versus exact midpoint inverse-transform mean `0.9999999793`. | Adopted as an explicit vector-only f32 throughput opt-in: `vectorStandardExponentialApproxLogF32*`, `fillVectorStandardExponentialApproxLogF32*`, `vectorExponentialApproxLogF32*`, `fillVectorExponentialApproxLogF32*`, and reusable `VectorStandardExponentialApproxLogF32` / `VectorExponentialApproxLogF32`. It intentionally changes output mapping and is not a default replacement. S4-M4 still remains open because normal kernels and f64/default exponential dense kernels are unresolved. |

A later f32 direct-source refinement uses the optimized f32 standard-fill vector8 chunk source for `fillLogNormalFrom(..., f32, mean=0, stddev!=1)` when the caller is not the `Rng` facade; focused rows improve to about 138.5M facade, 144.2M FastPrng direct, and 148.9M ScalarPrng direct. Broad facade routing, fused affine/`@exp`, fused `std.math.exp`, and f64 scalar-scale variants remain below the best staged rows. | Adopted for `fillLogNormalFrom(..., mean == 0, stddev != 1)`, keeping vector scale for f64 and using the optimized f32 standard source only for direct-source f32 calls. This improves exact f32 direct fills but does not fully close the exact LogNormal transform/codegen gap. |
| VectorLogNormal mean-zero standard-normal staging | Tried the same mean-zero standard-normal staging specialization for `fillVectorLogNormalFrom` | Fresh `vectorbench` rows showed facade/reusable `fillVectorLogNormal f64x4` around 121M/121M lanes/s and direct around 140M lanes/s, compared with previous rows around 130M facade, 137M direct, and 126M reusable. Keep vector LogNormal on the existing parameterized vector-normal staging path; scalar slice and vector slice codegen differ enough that the scalar mean-zero fill win does not transfer. |

| Vector ratio-of-uniforms normal kernel | Checked a non-ziggurat normal candidate using Leva-style ratio-of-uniforms acceptance, both as per-lane scalar sampling and as a dense whole-block accept/retry vector shape | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "StandardNormal f32x8"` / `"StandardNormal f64x4"` reports ratio-of-uniforms per-lane candidates around 98M/100M lanes/s and dense-block candidates around 16M/28M lanes/s, versus same-run direct scalar ziggurat lane-fill around 479M/452M. | Table-quantile vector normal opt-in | Tested whether a vector-only lookup-table inverse-CDF approximation can provide a useful dense normal throughput profile without ziggurat repair. Unit snapshots cover reproducibility, and `distcheck` now covers f32/f64 table profiles with larger-sample mean/variance gates plus standard-normal CDF smoke gates at -2, -1, 0, 1, and 2. | Adopted as explicit vector-only approximate throughput opt-ins: `vectorStandardNormalTableF32/F64*`, `fillVectorStandardNormalTableF32/F64*`, `vectorNormalTableF32/F64*`, `fillVectorNormalTableF32/F64*`, plus reusable `VectorStandardNormalTableF32/F64` / `VectorNormalTableF32/F64`. They narrow the normal side for users who accept a table/discrete/truncated approximation contract, but do not replace default normal. |

These are far above f32/f64 ziggurat lane-fill and above the f32 approximate-log opt-in, with table/discrete/truncated output mapping. Unit snapshots cover reproducibility, and `distcheck` now covers f32/f64 table exponential profiles with larger-sample mean/variance gates plus standard-exponential CDF smoke gates at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, and 4; parameterized profiles inherit the same standard table source and have scaled mean/variance gates. | Adopted as explicit vector-only approximate throughput opt-ins: `vectorStandardExponentialTableF32/F64*`, `fillVectorStandardExponentialTableF32/F64*`, `vectorExponentialTableF32/F64*`, `fillVectorExponentialTableF32/F64*`, plus reusable `VectorStandardExponentialTableF32/F64` / `VectorExponentialTableF32/F64`. They narrow the exponential side for users who accept a table/discrete/truncated approximation contract, but do not replace default exponential. |
| Vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | Checked whether a branch-selected Acklam inverse-CDF approximation over vector open-uniform lanes can avoid ziggurat rejected-lane repair and become a useful dense opt-in normal kernel | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "StandardNormal f32x8"` / `"StandardNormal f64x4"` reports inverse-CDF candidates around 80M/75M lanes/s, versus same-run direct scalar ziggurat lane-fill around 479M/455M. | f32-only vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | The first inverse-CDF candidate widened f32 lanes to f64 for the polynomial; tested whether keeping the whole Acklam-style approximation in f32 vector math changes the conclusion | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "inverse-cdf f32"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized candidates around 100M/100M lanes/s, slightly above the widened inverse-CDF candidate but still far below current direct scalar ziggurat lane-fill around 479M. | Central-fast vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | The prior inverse-CDF candidates computed tail `log`/`sqrt` expressions for every vector even though most lanes are central; tested a branch that uses only the central rational polynomial when all vector lanes are in the central region and falls back to the full inverse-CDF otherwise | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "inverse-cdf central"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 212M/211M lanes/s and f64x4 around 202M/201M. | Tail-repair vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | The central-fast branch still falls back to the full vector inverse-CDF when any lane is in the tail; tested computing the central polynomial for all lanes and repairing only tail lanes with scalar inverse-CDF approximation | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "inverse-cdf tail-repair"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 245M/245M lanes/s and f64x4 around 207M/207M. Checksums match the inverse-CDF candidate at printed precision because the output mapping is the same approximation with less unnecessary tail work. | Rejected as a production default or opt-in dense kernel. Per-tail scalar repair avoids full-vector tail work but the central rational polynomial remains too expensive. |
| Tail-only vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | The previous tail-repair candidate called a full scalar inverse-CDF helper for tail lanes; tested repairing each known low/high tail lane with only the matching tail rational expression | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "inverse-cdf tail-only"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 388M/385M lanes/s and f64x4 around 295M/297M. Checksums match the inverse-CDF candidate at printed precision because the output mapping is unchanged. | Rejected as a production default or opt-in dense kernel. Tail-only repair confirms tail value generation was a major cost, but the valid inverse-CDF route still trails ziggurat lane-fill. |
| Reduced-degree vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | Checked whether lowering the f32 central rational polynomial degree, while keeping tail-only rational repair, can trade approximation quality for enough speed to become a named f32 opt-in | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "inverse-cdf reduced"` reports about 413M f32x8 standard and about 410M parameterized lanes/s on rerun; an earlier same command showed run-order noise with the standard row lower around 264M. | Central-mask vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | Tail-only repair checks every lane; tested a vector tail mask with an all-central fast return before running scalar tail-only repairs | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "central-mask"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 327M/326M lanes/s and f64x4 around 269M/268M, below the simpler tail-only rows around 388M/385M and 295M/297M and still below ziggurat lane-fill. | Rejected. | Ziggurat-tail vector inverse-CDF normal approximation | Since central-only inverse-CDF is fast and tail-only repair remains expensive, tested using the central inverse-CDF polynomial for central lanes and replacing only tail lanes with scalar ziggurat normal samples | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "ziggurat-tail"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 409M/409M lanes/s and f64x4 around 330M/329M. Mixing central inverse-CDF with ziggurat tail repair is closer for f32 but still not enough to beat scalar lane-fill. |
| Central-only inverse-CDF diagnostic probe | Tested the lower bound of the f32 inverse-CDF center polynomial by applying the central rational approximation to every lane, including tails, to separate polynomial cost from tail handling cost | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "central-only"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 662M/652M lanes/s, above current ziggurat lane-fill. It shows the central polynomial alone is cheap enough, but valid tail handling is the bottleneck; do not expose or use central-only inverse-CDF as a sampler. |
| Tail-zero inverse-CDF diagnostic probe | Tested the cost of detecting inverse-CDF tail lanes without computing valid tail values by applying the central polynomial and replacing tail lanes with zero | Focused `vectorbench -- 16777216 "tail-zero"` reports f32x8 standard/parameterized around 644M/673M lanes/s, again above ziggurat lane-fill, but this is not a valid normal sampler because tail outputs are deliberately wrong. Tail detection and vector select are cheap; producing statistically valid tail values is the expensive part of the inverse-CDF route. |

LLVM already hoists the loop-invariant setup for fixed-parameter sampling loops, so the extra struct fields add size without a measured win. |
| Zipf `invCdf` exp/log attempt | After the Zipf acceptance win, tried replacing the remaining `pow` in `invCdf` with `exp(q * log(base))` | Focused rows moved from a 45.9M mean to 46.7M with unchanged checksums, inside run-to-run noise. | Reverted to keep the change set limited to measured wins. |
| HIN `lgamma` fallback replacement | Replaced the `std.math.lgamma` fallback in `binomialLogFactorial` with the cheap Stirling-series `lnOfFactorial` already used by the hypergeometric sampler | `alea binomial large` (`n = 10000`) improves from about 19.7M to about 26.6M samples/s (+35%) with unchanged checksum; the fallback error is about `2e-11` in log-acceptance at the table boundary. | Adopted as S4-M1358. |
| Vector Kent small-`a` angle branch | After the scalar Kent Exp(1) race win, tried adding a vectorized small-`a` uniform-angle rejection branch to `vectorKentPointFrom`, with a scalar tail | `alea vector kent f64x4 3d` moved only from 4.1M to 4.2M vectors/s (within noise) while adding about 30 lines and a shadowing fix. | Rejected and reverted. The libm `log` is already cheap relative to the Kent rejection loop; the polynomial added 15 coefficients without a measured win. |
| Vector Kent Wood-proposal exponential race | After the scalar Kent race, tried replacing `log(u)` in `vectorWoodW` with `vectorStandardExponentialFrom`, matching the scalar Wood proposal | `alea vector kent f64x4 3d` stayed at about 4.2M vectors/s (4.1M baseline) with a changed checksum; the scalar row moved only within layout noise. | Rejected and reverted. | Student-t CDF shared reciprocal square root | Tried computing `x = ν/(ν+t²)` and `sin θ = t/√(ν+t²)` from one reciprocal square root to save a division per CDF call | `student-t copula 3d` stayed inside the 15.3-17.1M samples/s run-to-run band with a low-bit checksum change. | Rejected and reverted. The copula is `atan`-bound; division elimination does not move it. |
| Vector Kent per-lane cos/sin conditional | After the scalar Kent direct-cos/sin win, tried calling `kentConditionalCosSinFrom` per lane in `vectorKentPointFrom` instead of the vector von Mises plus vector cos/sin | Vector Kent improved 4.2M → 4.9M vectors/s (+15%), but the scalar `kent 3d` row dropped from about 27.5M to 24.0M through code layout even though its checksum was unchanged. | Rejected and reverted. | Inlined single-column matrix vMF dispatch | Tried dispatching `cols == 1` directly to the comptime 2D/3D vMF inside `sampleIntoFrom` | The 3×1 row improved 30.6M → 33.2M, but the 3×2 row dropped 34.2M → 27.5M through code layout even though its checksum was unchanged. | Fixed rather than reverted: moving the dispatch into a `noinline fn sampleExactK1From` restores 3×2 to about 34.2M and keeps 3×1 at 33.6M. | Bingham acceptance `log1p` removal | A stub replacing both `log1p` and `log(u)` suggested a large upper bound, so replaced only `std.math.log1p(x)` with `@log(1 + x)` in the scalar Bingham acceptance | `bingham 3d` 24.5M → 26.7M, `matrix fisher so3` 27.0M → 30.2M, `matrix fisher o3` 19.0M → 22.5M, all with unchanged checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1373. The libm `log` is faster than the branchy `log1p`; the absolute log-acceptance error stays around `1e-16`. |
| Vector Kent per-lane cos/sin retry | Retried the per-lane `kentConditionalCosSinFrom` vector angle with a `noinline` wrapper to protect the scalar layout | Vector Kent improved 4.8M → 5.5M vectors/s (+15%), but scalar `kent 3d` still dropped from about 26.9M to 23.7M with an unchanged checksum. | Rejected and reverted again. The scalar Kent row is the more valuable one; layout isolation did not prevent the regression. |
| Benchmark comparison hygiene | Full-suite runs showed 10-30% swings in rows untouched by recent changes (`BufferedLogNormal f32`, `standard-geometric`, `fillStandardNormal`, `skew-normal`), so compared full-suite deltas against focused A/B and worktree builds | Focused A/B at baseline `3d8f7fa9` vs current: `BufferedLogNormal f32 stddev=1 fast direct` 407.8M vs 401.3M, `standard-geometric` 1658.3M vs 1628.2M, `fillStandardNormal scalar direct` 604.2M vs 618.6M (1-2% differences), while `skew-normal` was a real 13% regression fixed by reverting S4-M1370. | Adopted as the S4-M1376 method: use full runs only as screening, confirm flagged rows with focused A/B, and bisect suspected commits with `git worktree` builds. |
| Precomputed Wood constants for exact single-column matrix vMF | The exact `k = 1` path called `vonMisesFisherPointFrom`, which recomputes `vmfWoodConstants` per draw even though the mean norm is fixed | `matrix von mises-fisher 3x1` improved 33.6M → 44.8M samples/s (+33%) with an unchanged checksum. Storing the constants in three new struct fields dipped `skew-normal` about 2.3% through layout; reusing the unused `h` buffer avoided the struct-size change and kept `skew-normal` at 574-582M. | Adopted as S4-M1377 with the `h`-buffer storage. |
| Single-sample multinomial breakdown | Profiled the 3-category single-sample path to find where its ~130 ns goes before attempting another optimization | One mixed HIN binomial alone measures about 80 ns; the multinomial's first draw uses a cached setup and the second pays the per-call HIN setup plus rejection loop. Category-loop bookkeeping and the `@memset` are negligible. | Next single-sample multinomial work should target the HIN setup or acceptance loop (for example a precomputed `lfx` table), not the multinomial wrapper. A 3-category unroll probe showed no measurable gain and was reverted. |
| HIN log-factorial call clobbering | The HIN loop body contained a `binomialLogFactorial` branch that could call `lnOfFactorial`; a stub showed the loop roughly halved when that possible call was removed, so split the helper into table-only and general forms and dispatch on `trials < 513` | Direct-engine probe: `binomial(100, 1/6)` 75.0 → 41.9 ns/op, `binomial(10000, 0.01)` 59.6 → 28.4 ns/op; `multinomial direct` 8.8M → 9.2M samples/s. Facade rows (`multinomial`, `binomial large`) stay flat because the facade codegen already avoided the call and remains facade-overhead-bound. | Adopted as S4-M1380. The remaining facade gap is `Rng` facade overhead, not the HIN kernel. |
| Vector Kent Wood tail as a noinline helper | S4-M1370's inlined tail improved vector Kent but shifted the hot skew-normal layout by about 13%, so retried the same tail through a `noinline fn vectorKentWoodTailFrom` | `vector kent f64x4 3d` 4.1M → 4.6M vectors/s (+12%) with the same tail checksum, while scalar Kent stayed at 27.2M and `skew-normal` stayed at 573-589M. | Adopted as S4-M1381. Out-of-line helpers can reintroduce a reverted optimization without the layout side effect. |

| Watson via Bingham(3) substitution | Before compaction, probed whether the scalar/vector Watson sampler could be replaced by the Bingham(3) sampler at the same concentration, since Bingham already shares the ACG proposal | `watson(3, k=5)` measured 32.5M samples/s (30.8 ns/op) versus `bingham(3, diag(0,0,5))` at 31.1M (32.2 ns/op). | Rejected. Bingham is slower than Watson for the Watson law, so no substitution was made. |
| Matrix Fisher O(3) duplicated sampler branches | The O(3) sampler selects between the SO(3) and reflection Bingham samplers through a pointer so both branches share one inlined body; tried branching and calling each field directly instead | Duplicating the inlined Bingham body made `matrix fisher o3` move 23.1M -> 22.2M and, through layout, `matrix fisher so3` drop 31.0M -> 26.1M with unchanged checksums. | Rejected and reverted. The shared pointer-selected body is the better layout; keep it. |
| HIN setup shared-log reformulation | `d1x` and `d2x` each paid their own log; rewrote both arguments through one shared `A = log(32·t/(π/4))` plus the acceptance `lp1p` log | The setup itself measured 19.2 -> 17.3 ns (and 23.6 -> 21.1 ns for `n = 10000`) across six cases, but end-to-end `binomial large` and the multinomial rows stayed within ~1% noise because the setup overlaps the rejection loop. A 10-term series for `q = -log(1-δ)` was also slower than the log and changed `q` bits. | Shared-log part adopted as part of S4-M1382 because it is bit-identical over a 1.38M-case sweep; the `q` series was rejected. |
| Degenerate HIN waiting tail | When `p` is exactly on the `np/trials` grid, `q` is exactly zero and the waiting tail always returns zero after one exponential draw | Skipping the tail when `setup.q == 0` improved `alea binomial large` from 28.6-28.8M to 32.2-32.7M samples/s (+12.7%) with a stable new checksum; multinomial rows stayed inside the run-to-run band. | Adopted as S4-M1382. The skipped draw cannot change the sampled value; only the consumed stream differs for grid-aligned parameters. |
| Bingham value-receiver swap | Tried changing `Bingham.sample`/`sampleFrom`/`fill`/`fillFrom` to `*const Self` receivers to remove the per-draw copy of the precomputed Bingham state | `matrix fisher o3` improved 22.6M -> 25.1M and `vector bingham` 6.7M -> 7.3M, but `matrix fisher so3` dropped 30.0M -> 26.5M through layout with unchanged checksums. | Rejected as a full swap. The private `sampleFromPtr` body keeps the O(3) win without the SO(3) layout regression (S4-M1384). |
| Public Multinomial pointer receivers | Changed the public `Multinomial.sample*` methods to `*const Self` to drop the outer struct copy | The multinomial rows moved about 2% (inside the run-to-run band) while the public signatures would change. | Rejected and reverted. Keep value receivers on the public sampling methods; only the internal first-conditional helper borrows `self` (S4-M1384). |
| HIN setup by pointer | `binomialHinSampleFrom`/`binomialHinDrawFrom` copied the 128-byte setup by value, and `Multinomial.sampleFirstBinomialFrom` copied the cached optional payload per draw | Bracketed A/B runs improve `multinomial` 8.1M -> 8.3-8.5M (+2-5%), `multinomial direct` 9.1M -> 9.2-9.3M, and leave `multinomial many direct` flat, with unchanged checksums. | Adopted as part of S4-M1384. |
| MatrixFisher pointer body for the exact 3×2 path | After the Bingham pointer body won on O(3), tried the same split on `MatrixFisher` so `MatrixVonMisesFisher` could call `exact.sampleFromPtr` without copying the ~500-byte sampler per draw | `matrix von mises-fisher 3x2` stayed at 37.9-39.0M and `matrix fisher o3` stayed at 24.9-25.0M, but `matrix fisher so3` dropped from 30.7-31.2M to 26.0-26.3M through layout with unchanged checksums. | Rejected and reverted. The MatrixFisher wrapper shifts the SO(3) hot loop; keep the value-receiver sampling there. |
| Exponential-kernel geometric sampling | The geometric sampler used `floor(log(1−u)/log(1−p))`, paying two libm logs per draw, and the facade sampler paths had hand-inlined copies of the same formula | Replacing it with `floor(E/−log(1−p))` for `E ~ Exp(1)` (exactly geometric by the tail identity) improves `alea geometric` 224.3M -> 750-811M, `alea geometric failures` 224.5M -> 828-891M, the fill rows by 215-300%, and `alea negative-binomial` 45.2M -> 179-180M, with shifted stable checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1386. |
| Exponential-kernel inverse-power and extreme-value samplers | After the geometric win, replaced the remaining `−log(U)` transforms in Rayleigh, Weibull, Pareto, Fréchet, Gumbel, and generalized Pareto sampling with the exact `Exp(1)` forms, and removed the hand-inlined facade `log`/`pow` copies | `alea rayleigh` 258.7M -> 1007.6M, `alea pareto` 166.0M -> 408.2M, `alea gumbel` 88.7M -> 187.3M, `alea weibull` 52.9M -> 81.5M, `alea frechet` 53.4M -> 81.4M, with stable shifted checksums and unchanged vectorized bulk fills. | Adopted as S4-M1387. |
| Exponential forms for Laplace/Kumaraswamy/PowerFunction | Extended the `E = −log(U) ~ Exp(1)` substitution to the remaining inverse-CDF transforms and unified the scalar fills on the same formulas | `alea laplace` 222.6M -> 901.4M, `alea power-function` 104.7M -> 385.9M, `alea kumaraswamy` 88.3M -> 143.0M, `fillLaplace` 230.1M -> 536.4M, `fillKumaraswamy` 76.8M -> 129.5M, `fillPowerFunction` 162.0M -> 217.7M, `fillRayleigh` 258.5M -> 327.8M, `fillPareto` 183.1M -> 332.9M, `fillGumbel` 126.1M -> 174.1M, while `fillWeibull` 83.4M -> 73.4M and `fillFrechet` 85.7M -> 82.7M because their transforms still need log+exp. | Adopted as S4-M1388. |
Gamma boosted-path exponential form | Replaced the `pow(U, 1/shape)` boost in `gammaFrom` and the `Gamma` sampler with `exp(-E/shape)` | `alea gamma` dropped from about 157M to 129M and `gamma scalar direct` from about 193M to 148M with unchanged checksums, through layout rather than the boosted formula itself. | Rejected and reverted. The shape<1 boosted path keeps its `pow` transform; the other S4-M1388 changes are unaffected. |
Unified exponential fills | Replaced the vectorized uniform-log fill transforms for the inverse-power/extreme-value families with per-element exponential draws so `fillFrom` matches `sampleFrom` | Fills improved by 27-133% except Weibull/Fréchet, which regressed 12%/3% because their transform still needs a log and an exp after the exponential draw. The fill-vs-loop iterator test and the infinite-scale stream-shape test now cover the exponential stream shape. | Adopted as part of S4-M1388; the Weibull/Fréchet fill regressions are accepted for the single-sample gains and stream-shape consistency. |
| Ratio-of-normals Cauchy | The Cauchy sampler used `median − scale·cos(πU)/sin(πU)` (two transcendentals); tried `tan(π(U−1/2))` first, then the exact ratio of two independent standard normals | `tan` moved `alea cauchy` 123.3M -> 133-135M (+9%), while `X/Y` moved it to 533-540M (+332-338%) and the fills from 175-187M to 450-502M (+140-186%). | Adopted as S4-M1390 with the ratio form; the nonfinite stream-shape test reference now consumes two normals per sample. |
| One-log Zipf acceptance | `Zipf.nextSampleFrom` evaluated `exponent·(log(x) − log(inv_b))`; the ratio form `exponent·log(x/inv_b)` pays one log instead of two | `alea zipf direct` improved from 51.5M to 54.6-54.8M (+6%) with unchanged 16Mi checksums (`329448.000` / `330159.000`), so the sampled values are identical. | Adopted as part of S4-M1390. |
| Direct formulas for the Fisher-F/Student-t/chi facades | The top-level `fisherF` constructed a `FisherF` sampler (two `Gamma` inits) per call, unlike `fisherFFrom`; checked `studentT`/`chi` had the same pattern | `alea fisher-f` 88.2-89.0M -> 144.4-145.4M (+63%) with an identical checksum; `student-t` +2% and `chi` neutral with identical checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1392. Reusable samplers keep the cached form. |
| Packed dyadic binomial trials | Small-trial binomials used one engine word per Bernoulli trial even when p is dyadic; tried packing 32/21 trials per word with SWAR masks and one popcount | `alea binomial` (40, 0.25) 66.3M -> 898-907M (+1255%), `fillBinomial` 64.9M -> 751-754M, `fillBinomial scalar direct` 78.0M -> 996-998M. | Adopted as S4-M1394 with the fast path in the facade/sampler/fill entry points. |
Hooking the dyadic path inside binomialFrom | First attempt put the dyadic branch inside `binomialFrom`/`binomialSmallPFrom` so every caller would benefit | `beta binomial` dropped from 52.5M to 25-30M (-45%) and `multinomial` from 8.4M to 7.3-8.1M because the extra code pushed `binomialFrom` over the inliner threshold, losing the devirtualized facade draws in those composed paths; `pub inline` and a single-call helper did not fully restore them. | Rejected. The fast path now lives in the top-level `binomial` facade and the reusable `Binomial` sampler/fill methods, leaving `binomialFrom` untouched. |
| Packed scalar bool fills | `fillBoolsFrom` (p = 1/2) and `fillChanceQuarterFrom` (p = 1/4) wrote one bool per loop iteration with a shift and compare; tried expanding a byte of packed bits into eight bool bytes with one table lookup and one 8-byte store | `fillChance p=0.25` 1996.2M -> 3506.5M (+76%), `fillChance p=0.5` 2039.7M -> 3813.0M (+87%), `fillBernoulli p=0.25` 1953.3M -> 3478.8M (+78%), `fill bool facade` 1912.9M -> 3798.6M (+99%), all with unchanged checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1396. |
| Direction-plus-radius unit ball | The unit ball used cube rejection (about 5.7 uniforms per point, acceptance π/6); tried a uniform sphere direction scaled by `r = U^{1/3}` with `std.math.cbrt` | `alea unit ball` 77.9M -> 172.9-180.0M (+122-131%), the direct rows by 110-127%, and `fillUnitBall` 86.5-88.9M -> 149.9-164.6M (+69-90%). | Adopted as S4-M1398; the fill now loops the point sampler and shares its checksum. |
Four-normal unit ball projection | Checked whether four standard normals projected onto the first three coordinates give a uniform ball point | The projection of a uniform S³ point onto three coordinates has density proportional to `(1-r²)^{-1/2}` (the 2D analogue is the arcsine law), not the uniform ball density, so the method is invalid despite being faster. | Rejected on correctness; the direction-plus-radius decomposition is exact. |
| Four-normal unit ball projection | Checked whether four standard normals projected onto the first three coordinates give a uniform ball point, since the probe was 3.7x faster than direction-plus-radius | The projection of a uniform S³ point onto three coordinates has density proportional to `(1-r²)^{-1/2}` (the 2D analogue is the arcsine law), not the uniform ball density. | Rejected on correctness. |
| H2PE central-region exponential split | After finding that the H2PE central region is about 99.5% of candidates, tried drawing `av = -Exp(1)` there instead of `log(v)` so the dominant branch skips the acceptance log | `alea hypergeometric large` dropped from 36.1M to 33.2M (-8%) and `hypergeometric skew-large` from 36.9M to 32.7M (-11%) with changed checksums. | Rejected and reverted. The ziggurat exponential draw costs more than the avoided log, matching the earlier Bingham race result. |
| Packed dyadic binomial for 3/8 and 1/16 | Extended `dyadicBinomialFields` and the SWAR masks to m = 3 with k = 3 (3/8) and m = 4 with k = 1 (1/16), adding `alea binomial 3/8` and `alea binomial 1/16` rows plus a moment test | 3/8 improves from 23.2-31.1M to 223.2-227.5M (+620-880%) and 1/16 from 23.1-31.3M to 133.5-155.4M (+340-580%). | Adopted as S4-M1400. The first attempt left the `m > 3` guard in place, which made 1/16 silently fall back; the guard now allows m = 4. |
| Packed fills for 3/8 and 1/16 | `fillChance` for p = 3/8 and 1/16 fell back to the per-element threshold loop; added table-expanded kernels (two 4-bit fields per byte for 1/16, three 3-bit fields per nine bits for 3/8) | `fillChance p=3/8` 1294-1303M -> 2615-2631M (+102%) and `fillChance p=1/16` 1297-1310M -> 2566-2597M (+98%) with shifted stable checksums; a new true-fraction test covers p = 0.25, 0.375, 0.5, 0.0625. | Adopted as S4-M1402. |
| LogLogistic sampler exp/log transform | The S4-M1357 probe only changed the free `logLogisticFrom`, which the sampler methods do not call; the sampler carried its own `std.math.pow` copy | `alea log-logistic` 53.5-55.7M -> 70.8-72.1M (+30%) with `fillLogLogistic` unchanged and stable printed checksums; the bulk fill already used the vector exp/log form. | Adopted as S4-M1404. |
| Zipf integer-q inverse CDF | `Zipf.invCdf` used `std.math.pow(base, q)` for every draw even when `q = 1/(1-s)` is a small negative integer (s = 1.5 gives q = -2) | `alea zipf` 48.5M -> 69.2-72.8M (+43-50%), `zipf direct` 51.5M -> 76.0-78.7M, `Zipf.fillFrom direct` 51.0M -> 75.9-77.3M, with unchanged printed checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1406. |
| Zeta closed-form rejection loop | The Zeta rejection loop called `std.math.pow` twice per iteration (inverse CDF and acceptance correction) and duplicated the loop in four methods; added closed forms for `s-1 = 2, 1, 0.5, 3, 1.5` and a shared `nextSampleFrom` | `alea zeta` 82.8M -> 243.6-250.3M (+194-202%), `zeta direct` 88.7M -> 270.2-272.4M (+205-207%), `Zeta.fillFrom direct` 84.7M -> 237.3-239.9M (+180-183%), with stable shifted checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1408. |
| Specialized n-dimensional unit ball radius | The n ≥ 4 unit ball scaled the direction by `pow(u, 1/n)` with comptime n; replaced with repeated square roots for n = 4/8 and `exp(log(u)/n)` otherwise, adding 4D/8D rows | `unit ball 4d` 54.8M -> 121.7-123.3M (+122-125%), `unit ball 8d` 53.1-54.0M -> 131.4-137.0M (+143-158%), with unchanged printed checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1410. |
| Small-mean binomial dispatch | Every `mean >= 8` draw used the HIN rejection loop, but its envelope is tuned for larger means; measured HIN against the existing exponential waiting-time method on a mean/trials grid | Waiting wins below mean 16 (for example `n = 1000, mean = 12`: 46.3 vs 29.5 ns; `n = 10000, mean = 12`: 60.6 vs 29.1 ns) and HIN wins above it; raising the shared threshold to 16 moves `alea binomial mean-12` from 20.6-21.1M to 33.4-34.9M samples/s (+60-66%) with every other binomial-family checksum unchanged. | Adopted as S4-M1412. |
| Cached HIN setup in the reusable Binomial sampler | `Multinomial` caches its first-conditional HIN setup, so tried the same cache in `Binomial` (the 128-byte setup plus a flip flag) to skip the per-draw setup | With literal benchmark parameters the facade's setup is already constant-folded, so the cached sampler is neutral-to-slower: the new `alea binomial large sampler` row measures 27.2-27.6M against 29.8-30.4M for the 16-byte struct, an ~8-10% cost from the larger value. With runtime parameters (globals the compiler cannot fold) the facade pays the setup every draw and a hoisted cached setup is about 2x faster (58-79 ns against 26-47 ns across a parameter grid). | Rejected for now: the literal-parameter path and the sampler row regress, and serving runtime-parameter callers would need a dedicated cached sampler type instead of growing `Binomial`. The dedicated `CachedBinomial` type now serves that case (S4-M1444). |
| One-log large log-factorial in the HIN acceptance loop | `binomialLogFactorial` fell back to the shifted `lnOfFactorial`, which needs two logs per candidate; tried a direct Stirling form `(x+0.5)log(x) - x + ½log(2π) + 1/(12x)` for `x >= 513` | Interleaved 64 MiB A/B runs move `alea binomial large` from 27.4-28.4M to 29.5-30.5M samples/s (+9.5%) with identical checksums; both forms truncate at the same `1/(360x³)` term, so accuracy is unchanged. | Adopted as S4-M1414. |
| Shared fast log-factorial in the H2PE acceptance test | The hypergeometric H2PE acceptance test calls `lnOfFactorial` four times after its two squeezes fail; tried the table-plus-one-log `binomialLogFactorial` there | `alea hypergeometric large` stayed at 35.6-36.8M and `hypergeometric skew-large` at 36.3-37.1M across interleaved A/B runs with identical checksums: the squeezes already resolve nearly every candidate, so the four log-factorial evaluations are rare enough to be invisible. | Rejected and reverted. Keep the shared helper scoped to the binomial HIN path. |
| Scalar-per-lane fallback for vector Kent | The vector Kent path evaluates the Bessel helper and Wood rejection per lane; tried calling the cached scalar `Kent` sampler four times inside `VectorKent.sampleFrom`, both inline and through a `noinline` helper | `vector kent f64x4 3d` improved 4.3-4.5M -> 5.1-5.3M vectors/s (+20%) in the inline variant, but scalar `alea kent 3d` dropped 26.2-26.7M -> 20.7-21.2M (-21%) through code layout with an unchanged scalar checksum; the `noinline` helper kept the same scalar regression (17.0-17.1M against a 20.9-22.0M same-period baseline) while the vector gain shrank to noise. | Rejected and reverted, matching the earlier vector-Kent probes: the scalar row is the more valuable one and out-of-line isolation does not protect its layout here. |
| Lead-category ordering in multinomial sampling | The sequential-conditional multinomial always drew category 0 first, so `{1, 2, 3}` paid an HIN draw for the small category before reaching the `1/2` fair path; tried drawing the largest-probability category first | A manual reorder probe measured 126.3 ns against 33.9 ns per `Multinomial(100, {1,2,3})` draw; the implemented `lead_index` ordering improves `alea multinomial` 8.3M -> 34.6-34.7M samples/s (+317%), `multinomial direct` 9.1-9.2M -> 44.5-44.6M (+385%), and `multinomial many direct` 11.4M -> 43.2-43.3M (+279%) with shifted stable checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1416. The distribution is unchanged because categories are exchangeable. |
| Integer-shape beta via order statistics | Beta(a, b) with integer shapes equals the a-th order statistic of a+b-1 uniforms; tried replacing the two-gamma draw for small integer shapes | `beta(2,3)` improves 8.13 -> 5.59 ns with a 4-element selection, but `beta(3,4)` needs six uniforms and a runtime selection loop that measured 22.24 ns against 7.82 ns for the gamma path, and an unrolled per-shape switch would add 21 branches. | Not adopted: the single-shape gain is too small relative to the code and layout risk in `betaFrom`, which many composed samplers inline. |
| Padé acceptance squeeze in the Bingham ACG loop | The Bingham acceptance test evaluated `log1p(2t/ν)` on every iteration; tried a safe Padé lower bound `log1p(x) >= 2x/(2+x)` that accepts most candidates without the log | `alea bingham 3d` 26.8-27.1M -> 30.2-30.6M (+13%), `matrix fisher o3` 24.2-24.4M -> 27.0-27.4M (+12%), `matrix fisher so3` 30.4-30.6M -> 32.0-32.7M (+6%), `vector bingham f64x4 3d` 6.6-6.7M -> 7.4M (+10-12%), all checksums unchanged; kent/watson/skew-normal rows stay in range. | Adopted as S4-M1418. |
| Bounds squeeze for the Kent Wood acceptance | The Kent Wood loop evaluates `log(1 − x0·w)` per candidate; tried the safe bounds `log(1−y) >= −y/(1−y)` and `log(1−y) <= −y` to accept or reject without the log | At the benchmark parameters (`κ=5`, ovalness `1.5`, `x0 ≈ 0.8001`) only 0.27 safe accepts and 0.21 safe rejects occur per draw out of 1.32 candidates, so 64% of candidates still need the exact log and the expected saving is about one and a half nanoseconds. | Not adopted: the bound is too loose at `x0 ≈ 0.8` to justify the extra branches. |
| Inverse-CDF search for the small-mean binomial | The `mean < 16` branch used the exponential waiting-time method; tried a sequential inverse-CDF walk from `k = 0` with the PMF recurrence, which needs one uniform total | A standalone probe measures 12.8-21.3 ns against 19.3-33.1 ns for `mean = 4..15` (about 35% faster), and `alea binomial mean-12` improves 34.1M -> 53.1M (+55%) with the inlined body. However, the extra inlined code costs `alea negative-binomial` 179.9M -> 163.0M (-9%) and `alea beta binomial` 51.9M -> 44.9M (-14%) through code layout; a `noinline` inversion keeps those rows at baseline but drops `mean-12` to 24.7M (-27%), and moving the helper away from `binomialSmallPFrom` does not change the outcome (beta-binomial still 43.3-44.9M). | Not adopted: the collateral layout regressions on two core distributions outweigh the single-region gain in every variant tried. The dedicated `CachedBinomial` now uses the same exact walk for runtime parameters without touching `binomialFrom`, so those regressions do not apply (S4-M1447). |
| Packed Bernoulli(1/3) trials for the small-trial binomial | The `trials <= 64` branch draws one word per Bernoulli trial; tried exact 2-bit rejection fields (0 success, 1-2 failure, 3 retry) so one word covers 32 trials and `n = 50` needs about four words | The kernel is exact (mean 16.6691 against 16.6679 over 1M draws) and `alea multinomial` improves 34.1-34.6M -> 46.4-47.2M (+36%) with the inlined kernel, but `alea beta binomial` drops 51.7-52.4M -> 44.5-44.9M (-14%) and `alea negative-binomial` 172.9-174.7M -> 159.6-161.1M (-7%) through code layout even with a `trials >= 32` guard; a `noinline` kernel keeps those rows at baseline but leaves `multinomial direct` at 41.8M against 44.1-44.6M (-5%) and the facade gain at +9%. | Not adopted: the layout cost on core distributions outweighs the multinomial gain, and the `noinline` variant is a wash. |
| Packed dyadic fills for 3/4 and other power-of-two ratios | `fillChance` only packed p = 1/4, 3/8, 1/2, and 1/16; p = 3/4, 1/8, and every other dyadic probability drew one word per bool | An inverted quarter mask moves `alea fillChance p=3/4` 839-850M -> 3236-3364M samples/s (+285%), and routing other dyadic ratios to the packed power-of-two kernel moves `p=1/8` 840-854M -> 1762-1821M (+110%); existing packed rows keep their checksums and stay within noise. | Adopted as S4-M1420. |
| Bernoulli bulk fill through the packed chance kernels | `Bernoulli.fill` and `fillFrom` only special-cased p = 0.5 and 0.25, so the reusable sampler missed the packed kernels for 3/4, 1/8, 3/8, and 1/16 | A shared `fillDispatch` helper routes `{0.5, 0.25, 0.75, 0.375, 0.0625, 0.125}` to `Rng.fillChanceFrom`; `alea fillBernoulli p=3/4` 845-846M -> 3339-3396M (+295%) and `p=1/8` 839-851M -> 1837-1845M (+118%), `p=0.25` unchanged, sensitive rows in range. | Adopted as S4-M1422. |
| Nibble-expanded fills for the remaining k/16 probabilities | The remaining `k/16` probabilities used the packed ratio kernel with one bool store per trial; tried a comptime nibble table that writes two bool bytes per lookup | `alea fillChance p=1/8` 1784-1793M -> 2391-2653M (+34-48%, checksum shifts from the 3-bit to 4-bit field stream) and `p=3/16` 1743-1784M -> 2436-2546M (+40-43%, unchanged checksum); the new `p=5/16` row lands at 2427-2502M; existing rows keep their checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1424. |
| Dyadic threshold dispatch for Bernoulli bulk fills | `Bernoulli` stores only the 64-bit threshold, so S4-M1422 had to list packed probabilities explicitly; tried recovering `k / 2^m` from the threshold with a shift-and-compare | A comptime loop over `m = 1..6` covers every dyadic ratio up to 1/64; `alea fillBernoulli p=5/16` 841.7M -> 2534-2635M (+201-213%) with a shifted checksum, and `p=0.25`, `p=3/4`, `p=1/8` keep their checksums and throughput. | Adopted as S4-M1426. |
| Bingham nu refinement grid | `binghamChooseNu` scans powers of two plus `n − 1`; the acceptance estimate showed the coarse winner leaves a few percent on the table (`bingham 3d` 98.0% vs 99.97% at `nu = 1.19`; Fisher SO(3) 95.8% vs 99.7% at `nu = 2.83`) | A quarter-octave refinement grid (±3 steps) raised the estimated acceptance as predicted, but `alea bingham 3d` (28.7-29.9M -> 29.4-29.7M), `matrix fisher so3` (30.6-32.5M -> 30.6-31.3M), and `matrix fisher o3` (25.7-27.3M -> 24.9-25.8M) stayed within noise, and the sampled stream changed. | Not adopted: the 2-4% estimated acceptance gain is below the measurement noise and does not justify the stream change. |
| Packed chance kernels for small power-of-two fillRatio denominators | `fillRatioFrom` only special-cased denominators 2 and 4, so `fillRatio(3, 8)`, `(1, 8)`, and `(5, 16)` used the one-store-per-trial ratio kernel; tried routing them through `fillChanceFrom` | `alea fillRatio 3/8` 1864-1880M -> 2812-2845M (+51%, unchanged checksum), `1/8` 1898-1910M -> 2564-2672M (+35-40%), `5/16` 1820-1860M -> 2392-2562M (+31-38%, unchanged checksum); 1/4 and 1/2 stay in range. | Adopted as S4-M1428. |
| Byte-level rejection for non-dyadic chance fills | The non-dyadic `fillChance` fallback draws one 64-bit word per bool; tried packing eight trials per word with byte fields, where `b < floor(256p)` succeeds, `b >` fails, and `b ==` resolves against the residual probability | The generic loop already runs at 0.53 ns/bool in a tight probe, while the bytewise version measured 2.24 ns/bool at p = 1/3 and 2.17 ns at p = 0.7 (unpredictable nested branches) and only 0.44 ns at p = 0.001. | Not adopted: the word savings do not compensate for the extra branches at ordinary probabilities. |
| Small dyadic chance fills up to denominator 2^31 | `chanceDyadicRatio` capped denominators at 64, so p = 1/128 used the per-element loop; extended the cap to 2^31 and replaced the Bernoulli six-step shift loop with an `@ctz` recovery | `alea fillChance p=1/128` 838-846M -> 1582-1726M (+87-104%) with a shifted checksum; every other fillChance row keeps its checksum and stays within noise. | Adopted as S4-M1430. |
| Integer-shape gamma via exponential sums | Gamma(2,1) and Gamma(3,1) are Erlang distributions; tried a sum-of-exponentials fast path in `gammaFrom` and the `Gamma` sampler | Standalone probes show gamma(2) 3.74 -> 2.11 ns and gamma(3) 3.59 -> 3.08 ns, and in context `alea gamma` +19-30%, `alea fillGamma` +63-115%, `alea chi-squared` +13-17%, `alea chi-squared cached` +29%, `alea dirichlet` +12-15%. However, `alea student-t cached` dropped 168-233M -> 122-126M (-30-45%) and `alea beta` 86-90M -> 78-79M (-9-12%) through code layout in every variant (inline Erlang, noinline Erlang, split dispatcher, forced inline). | Not adopted: the collateral regressions on two core distribution rows outweigh the gamma gains. |
| BTPE central binomial envelope | The triage listed BTPE as the remaining central-region candidate; ported the full five-region envelope from `rand_distr 0.5.1` (the local `~/Work/rand` checkout is absent) and routed `mean >= 12` through it | The corrected port is exact: `binomial large` checksum 6554710 implies mean 100.03 and `binomial mean-12` checksum 786931 implies 12.01, a 200k-draw Python port gives 100.0237/12.0104/20.0090, and released `rand_distr 0.5.1` gives 100.0294/11.9973/19.9992. Interleaved A/B runs still measure `binomial large` 35.6-35.9M -> 27.3-27.9M (-22-24%) and `binomial mean-12` 31.8-31.9M -> 17.2-17.4M (-45-46%). The first port also applied the acceptance test to region 1, which biased the mean to 101.34 and measured 18.9M/17.0M; the reference accepts the central triangle unconditionally. Rust's cached-setup BTPE with `SmallRng` reaches 33.2M at `(10000, 0.01)` and 21.1M at `(1000, 0.012)`, below both Alea rows. | Not adopted. The division-heavy envelope and recursive acceptance cost more than the HIN table loop and the waiting-time race on this host, and Alea already exceeds the local Rust evidence at both points. Evidence: `s4-m1443-btpe-central-binomial-probe.md`. |
| Dedicated cached binomial sampler | `Binomial` rebuilds the HIN envelope or the waiting-time rate on every draw, and growing `Binomial` with a cache regressed the literal-parameter sampler row by 8-10%; tried a separate pointer-receiver type instead | `CachedBinomial.init` precomputes the constant, fair, packed-dyadic, per-trial-threshold, waiting-time, or HIN method and reproduces the `Binomial` stream exactly. New runtime-parameter rows read `(10000, 0.01)` through volatile pointers: `alea binomial runtime sampler` 17.0-17.2M -> `alea binomial runtime cached` 37.1-38.9M samples/s (+116-129%) with identical checksum `6552418`; the cached row also edges the literal-parameter `alea binomial large` facade row (35.1-35.9M). | Adopted as S4-M1444. The dedicated type serves runtime-parameter callers without growing `Binomial` or slowing its literal-parameter path. |
| One-uniform inverse-CDF walk in the cached binomial sampler | The cached sampler still used the exponential waiting-time race for `mean < 16`, which needs about `mean + 1` exponential draws; replaced it with a sequential inverse-CDF walk from the PMF recurrence, which needs one uniform total | Across `n = 1000` and `mean = 1..15`, the cached walk measures 108.8M (mean 1), 86.7M (2), 69.6M (4), 56.6M (8), 48.6M (12), and 43.4M (15) samples/s against 75.3M, 59.4M, 48.3M, 38.1M, 32.2M, and 28.7M for the waiting race (+44-51%); `(10000, 0.0012)` and `(100, 0.12)` improve 32.1-32.2M -> 47.9M. The runtime mean-12 row moves 34.2M -> 49.3M with a shifted exact-stream checksum (`787393` -> `787277`). | Adopted as S4-M1447. The walk lives only in `CachedBinomial`, so the earlier layout regressions on `binomialFrom` do not apply; the `Binomial` stream is unchanged. |
| One-setup HIN fill for the binomial facade | `Binomial.fill` looped `binomialFrom` per element; through the `Rng` facade that call cannot be inlined, so the HIN setup was rebuilt for every element, while the monomorphized direct-source fill already hoists it | Runtime-parameter rows `(10000, 0.01)`: `alea fillBinomial large` 16.9-17.2M -> 35.8-36.8M samples/s (+108-114%) with identical checksum `6556211`; the direct-source row stays at parity across the A/B rounds. Adding the same cache to `fillFrom` measured about 14% slower there and was dropped; the first inlined helper also shifted `BufferedLogNormal` f32 scalar direct by about 32%, so the helper is out of line at the end of the production declarations. | Adopted as S4-M1451. The setup is deterministic, so the fill stream is unchanged. |
| Small-mean binomial inverse-CDF walk | The S4-M1447 walk lived only in `CachedBinomial` because inlining it into `binomialFrom` cost beta-binomial/negative-binomial through code layout, so the literal `Binomial` mean-12 row stayed on the waiting race at about 32M samples/s. | Moved the same one-uniform PMF walk behind an out-of-line `binomialInversionWalkFrom` helper at the end of the production declarations and routed `binomialSmallPFrom` (`mean < 16`, `trials > 64`) through it. `alea binomial mean-12` 32.1 -> 50.7 M samples/s (+58%) with the expected stream shift (checksum `785832` -> `786544`); `alea beta binomial` stays 44.5M with the identical checksum `2097245`, and the `binomial large`/`negative-binomial` rows stay at parity. The runtime-parameter sampler row stays about 24M because it recomputes `(1−p)^n` per draw; `CachedBinomial` remains the runtime path at about 50M. | Adopted as S4-M1556. The small-mean `Binomial` stream changes, and any multinomial/beta-binomial conditional whose mean falls below 16 follows it; `reproducibility-matrix.md` records the versioned change. |
| One-setup HIN fill for `fillFrom` | S4-M1451 adopted the one-setup HIN fill for the facade `Binomial.fill`, but the direct-source `fillFrom` still rebuilt the HIN setup per element (`alea fillBinomial large scalar direct` 16.2M vs the facade 34.5M); an earlier attempt was dropped as 14% slower. | Reused the existing out-of-line `binomialFillHinFrom` in `Binomial.fillFrom`. `alea fillBinomial large scalar direct` 16.2 -> 33.9 M samples/s (+109%) with the identical checksum `6553977`, matching the facade row at 34.4M; the plain `fillBinomial scalar direct` row stays about 1005M. | Adopted as S4-M1558. Stream-preserving: the helper builds the same deterministic setup and draws in the same order. |
| One-setup inverse-CDF walk fill for small-mean binomials | After the S4-M1556 walk, `Binomial.fill`/`fillFrom` still recomputed `(1−p)^n` and `p/(1−p)` per element in the `mean < 16` region, so runtime-parameter fills ran at about 24M samples/s. | Split the walk into `binomialInversionDrawFrom(source, trials, px0, ratio)` and added an out-of-line `binomialFillInversionFrom` that computes the constants once and fills through the walk. `alea fillBinomial mean-12` 23.7 -> 49.9 M samples/s (+110%) and `alea fillBinomial mean-12 scalar direct` 24.9 -> 50.6 M (+103%), both with the identical checksum `788021`; the HIN and generic rows stay at parity. | Adopted as S4-M1560. Stream-preserving: the constants are deterministic and the per-draw walk order is unchanged. |
| Shared negative-binomial fill rate | `NegativeBinomial.fill`/`fillFrom` looped `negativeBinomialFrom`, which recomputes `q = -log(1 - p)` per element; the facade inliner had already hoisted it, but the direct-source fill still paid it | Extracted `negativeBinomialFromLogQ` and computed `q` once per fill: `alea fillNegativeBinomial scalar direct` 180.4-182.4M -> 189.7-190.9M samples/s (+4-5%) with identical checksum `982247`, while the facade row stays at parity (159.3-162.1M -> 160.1-161.4M) and `negative-binomial`, `geometric`, and `beta binomial` keep their checksums. | Adopted as S4-M1453. The kernel is shared, so the sampled stream is unchanged. |
| Cached negative-binomial rate in the reusable sampler | `NegativeBinomial.sample`/`sampleFrom` called `negativeBinomialFrom`, which computes `q = -log(1 - p)`; tried caching `q` in the sampler struct for runtime-parameter callers | The literal row regressed 166.1M -> 159.5M (-4%) from the 16->24-byte value copy, and the runtime-parameter row was also slower (160.3M -> 154.3M): the inliner already hoists the loop-invariant log out of repeated `sample` calls. | Not adopted. The new `alea negative-binomial runtime` row stays at 161.4M, at parity with the literal row, confirming the hoisting; the sampler keeps its 16-byte layout. |
| Shared geometric fill rate | `Geometric.fill`/`GeometricFailures.fill` looped the per-element kernels, which recompute `q = -log(1 - p)`; tried computing it once per fill like the negative-binomial fill | All four fill rows stay at parity: `fillGeometric` 619.7-626.2M vs 621.9-622.9M, `fillGeometric scalar direct` 737.7-744.0M vs 735.9-743.7M, `fillGeometricFailures` 717.1-722.2M vs 713.2-719.6M, and `fillGeometricFailures scalar direct` 795.6-796.7M vs 794.9-802.4M, with identical checksums. The inliner already hoists the loop-invariant log in both facade and direct fills. | Not adopted; the probe was reverted. The negative-binomial fill still benefits because its larger per-element kernel was not hoisted there. |
| HIN small-p envelope `c` correction | The HIN small-p path carries a measured `+0.015` absolute mean offset (S4-M1474). Tried adding the discrete-interval supremum correction `(2·d1 − 1)/(2·np·(1−pa))` (about 0.28 at `np = 20`) to the region-1 envelope constant `c`, expecting the over-acceptance to disappear | The 20M-draw scan is unchanged within the offset's run-to-run band: mean-16 bias 0.01793 vs 0.01798, mean-20 0.01749 vs 0.01803, mean-100 0.01716 vs 0.01277, mean-1000 0.00930 vs 0.01056. A 40M-draw histogram at `(2000, 0.01)` shows a deficit at `k = 14..19` (−1e-4..−8e-4) and an excess at `k = 20..32` (+2e-4..+5e-4), i.e., a small right shift rather than uniform envelope slack. | Not adopted; reverted. The offset is a sub-0.003σ effect and the gates tolerate it. The next envelope attempt should target the region-2/positive-tail asymmetry instead of the region-1 constant. |
| HIN region-2 acceptance constant | After the region-1 `c` probe failed, tried shifting the region-2 envelope constant `c2` (the code implicitly uses `c2 = 0`) to see whether the negative-side envelope causes the right shift | `c2 = −0.2` moves the 20M-draw bias from `+0.018`/`+0.011` (means 16/1000) to `−0.108`/`−0.253`, so the sensitivity grows from about `0.6` to `1.3` per unit `c2` with the mean; zeroing the original offset would need `c2 ≈ −0.03` at mean 16 and `≈ −0.008` at mean 1000, so no single constant removes it. | Not adopted; reverted. The offset is a sub-0.003σ effect; the next attempt needs a mean-dependent envelope correction or the original paper's discrete constants. |
| HIN small-p envelope dominance | The earlier region-1 `c` and region-2 `c2 = −0.2` probes treated the `+0.015` mean offset as a constant shift. An exact acceptance-mass model showed the real cause: the region-2 half-normal envelope is below the target for the nearly flat cells just below the mode, so those cells are under-weighted. | Added `c2 = +c/4` to the region-2 rejection height and its mass term (`e^{c2}·s2·√(π/2)`), restoring dominance with zero violations on a 66-parameter grid; the exact model returns `20.000000`, and Zig 10M-draw multi-seed means are `19.9992..20.0004` against the former `20.015`. The region-2 envelope mass rises by about 19%, so the HIN acceptance rate drops by roughly 5%; a 4e6-draw `distcheck` gate pins the mean. | Adopted as S4-M1550. The affected small-`p` HIN stream changes and `reproducibility-matrix.md` records it. |

The vector Kent acceptance is versioned for `a <= 1.5` lanes; `reproducibility-matrix.md` records it. |
| Vector Bingham exponential-race acceptance | The vector Bingham kernel evaluates `log(u)` per lane for the acceptance test; tried the `E >= log_envelope − log_ratio` exponential race already used by Watson/Kent, expecting to remove four per-lane scalar logs. | `alea vector bingham f64x4 3d` fell from 7.4-7.5M to 7.1-7.2M vectors/s (-3-4%) with a changed checksum `7858544676074727599` -> `13061316062112371244`: the vector exponential kernel plus the extra draw costs more than the vector log here. | Rejected and reverted; the log-based acceptance stays. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1582-vector-bingham-race-probe.md`. |
| Exact truncated-exponential S² vMF/Kent marginal | Wood's rejection loop samples the S² cosine marginal `∝ e^{κw}` on `[-1, 1]` through a rational proposal, but the marginal inverts in closed form (`w = −1 + log1p(U·(e^{2κ}−1))/κ`). Kent's proposal marginal is the same vMF(λ) law. | Replaced the scalar/vector Wood loops with the exact inversion: `alea von mises-fisher 3d` 52.1 -> 126.2M samples/s (+142%), `alea vector von mises-fisher f64x4 3d` 10.4 -> 20.1M vectors/s (+93%), `alea vector kent f64x4 3d` 4.4-4.6 -> 5.0M (+9-14%), `alea matrix von mises-fisher 3x1` 44.3 -> 49.4M (+12%), and scalar `alea kent 3d` stays at parity (24.8-25.1M against a 24.7-26.3M baseline). Bingham/Watson rows and checksums are unchanged. The first `log1p`-only form cost scalar Kent ~12%; `@log(1 + x)` for `κ >= 1e-2` (w error below 1.1e-14) recovered it. | Adopted as S4-M1583. The S² vMF, Kent, and single-column matrix-vMF streams change; `reproducibility-matrix.md` records it. `dim > 3` keeps Wood. |
| Vectorized circle rejection for vMF/Watson tangent angles | The S² vMF and Watson samplers draw the uniform tangent angle with four per-lane scalar `unitCircleFrom` rejection loops; tried a vector round that draws two vector uniforms, computes `(x²−y², 2xy)/(x²+y²)` per lane, and retires accepted lanes | `alea vector von mises-fisher f64x4 3d` 20.2 -> 19.3M vectors/s (-4-5%) and `alea vector watson f64x4 3d` 7.6 -> 7.6M (neutral), with changed checksums `15146673891782920812` -> `1099239467985723792` and `4618392559654806221` -> `13841586174260344571`. The vector round draws eight words for all lanes (including already-accepted ones), ~44% more RNG words than per-lane rejection, which outweighs the saved scalar branches. | Rejected and reverted; per-lane circle draws stay. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1585-vector-circle-rejection-probe.md` so it is not retried without a cheaper lane-compaction scheme. |
| Scalar-per-lane vector vMF | After the exact truncated-exponential marginal, the S² vMF cosine inversion and tangent-circle rejection are scalar work, so the packed SIMD loop pays splat/select overhead without vectorizing the expensive operations. Kept packed: PERT per-lane beta only matched the packed `vectorBetaFrom` (16.0-16.6M, checksum unchanged), NIG per-lane composition regressed 40.0 -> 16.5M (-58%) so `sampleFrom` keeps the packed inverse-Gaussian composition, triangular (1498M samples/s) and arcsine (155M) already exceed their scalar rows, and log-normal (98M vs 125M scalar) is left for a sound exact-`exp` candidate per `lognormal-transform-notes.md`. | Adopted as S4-M1624 and versioned in `reproducibility-matrix.md`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1624-vector-coverage-and-per-lane-continuation.md`. |
| `exp2`/`log2` transforms for the log+exp samplers | Weibull, Fréchet, GEV, and log-logistic each pay `exp(c·ln x)` per sample. On this host `@exp2`/`@log2` are cheaper than `@exp`/`@log`, and `exp(c·ln x) = exp2(c·log2 x)` exactly. Replaced the transform in `weibullFrom`, `frechetFrom`, `generalizedExtremeValuePointFrom`, and `LogLogistic.sample`/`sampleFrom`, then measured the single-transcendental paths too. | Bracketed A/B: weibull 78.3-80.4 -> 134.8-139.9M (+68-79%), frechet 76.6-79.3 -> 139.0-139.9M (+75-83%), GEV 81.7-83.3 -> 140.4-140.9M (+69-72%), log-logistic 70.7-71.1 -> 174.9-177.2M (+147-149%), with unchanged printed checksums and ulp-level stream changes (max relative transform difference `1.2e-13`, GEV `expm1` term `8.5e-14`). Logistic (+2.6%), gumbel (+3.9%), Pareto (+4.9%), and Kumaraswamy (+2.7%) stayed inside the run-to-run band and were reverted. | Adopted as S4-M1628 and versioned in `reproducibility-matrix.md`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1628-exp2-log2-samplers.md`. |
| `exp2`/`log2` fill transforms | The Weibull/Fréchet per-element fill loops and the Weibull/Fréchet/log-logistic vector-lane fill helpers still used `@exp`/`@log` after S4-M1628. Converted them to the same exact forms, folding `ln 2` into the exponent for the two-log helpers. | `alea fillWeibull` 77.3 -> 102.0-129.2M (+32-67%), `alea fillFrechet` 77.6 -> 121.3-128.2M (+56-65%), `alea fillLogLogistic` 102.0 -> 146.4-184.3M (+44-81%), with unchanged printed checksums and ulp-level fill stream changes. The vector lane helper test references were updated to the `exp2`/`log2` forms. | Adopted as S4-M1631 and versioned in `reproducibility-matrix.md`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1631-exp2-log2-fills.md`. |
| `exp2`/`log2` PowerFunction fill | The PowerFunction bulk fill still used `@exp(@log(u) * inverse_shape)` per lane. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1633-exp2-log2-power-function-fill.md`. |
| Gumbel fill `log2` probe (rejected) | After the PowerFunction win, tried the same `log2` rewrite on `gumbelFromOpenClosedUniformsVector` (`log(-log u)` as two `log2` calls with folded `ln 2`). | `alea fillGumbel` 159.2 -> 150.9-157.2M, inside the run-to-run band and not better than the two-`log` form, so the probe was reverted; no stream change. | Rejected; the Gumbel fill keeps `@log(-@log(u))`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1636-vector-binomial-dyadic-kernel.md`. |
| Vector fair binomial kernel | The dyadic vector binomial kernel rejected `m = 1`, so `p = 0.5` fell back to per-lane scalar draws (95.3-95.7M vectors/s at trials=40). | Vector binomial inverse-CDF mid trials | The inverse-CDF kernel was gated at `trials <= 64`, leaving non-dyadic 65..128 trials on per-lane scalar draws (3.9M vectors/s at 100/0.4). Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1652-vector-binomial-inverse-cdf-mid-trials.md`. A follow-up probe of 129..256 trials at `(200, 0.4)` measured the per-lane fallback at 4.3M vectors/s; the inverse-CDF kernel's cost scales linearly with trials (about 4.2M at 256 entries), so the guard stays at 128 and the new `alea vector binomial large-nondyadic f64x4` row stays as coverage. |

| Vector binomial large-trial CDF fill | The S4-M1664 hoisted-CDF fill stopped at 128 trials and used a full-table branchless scan, so 200-trial fills fell back to the per-lane sampler (16-17M lanes/s). Extend the table to 1024 trials and use a per-lane binary search for widths above 128; keep the scan for widths up to 128. Also guard the `(1-p)^trials` underflow so extreme `p` falls back to the per-lane sampler instead of a zeroed table. | `vectorbench -- 16777216`: n=200/0.4 fill 16.3-16.9 -> 90.2-95.6M lanes/s (+5.5x), n=100/0.4 and n=40/0.4 unchanged (112.6M and 83.0M); the candidate row matches at 93.6M. A threshold sweep shows the binary search loses at width 100 (87-91M vs 112M) and wins at 200. A 400k-lane audit over (100,0.4), (200,0.4), (1024,0.4), (128,0.999), and (64,0.999) matches exact means and CDFs (max error 0.0017); the (128,0.999) case previously returned 128 for every lane. New distcheck gates cover (200,0.4) at 76/80/84 and (128,0.999) at 127. | Adopted as S4-M1684; the fill stream for 129..1024 trials is a versioned bulk policy recorded in `reproducibility-matrix.md`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1684-vector-binomial-large-cdf-fill.md`. |
