| Inverse-Gaussian quantile bracket gate (rejected) | Tried a cheap pre-check to gate the inverse-Gaussian quantile bracket expansion, aiming to skip the bracket scan when the start is already close. | The inverse-Gaussian quantile row was unchanged inside the run-to-run band with the unchanged checksum. | Rejected; the quantile keeps the bracket expansion. |
| ex-Gaussian density reuse in the quantile derivative (rejected) | The ex-Gaussian Newton loop needs both the CDF and the density; tried reusing the density term computed for the derivative in the CDF evaluation (the S4-M1840 wrapped/skew pattern). | The ex-Gaussian quantile row was unchanged inside the band with the unchanged checksum; the ex-Gaussian CDF already shares the `phi` evaluation with the derivative. | Rejected; the quantile keeps the current structure. |
| Wrapped CDF rule-noise floor raise (rejected) | The wrapped 20-point rule drops terms below a 5e-12 absolute floor; tried raising the floor to 5e-11 to stop the image loop earlier. | The wrapped skew-normal quantile moved -1% in the paired bracket with unchanged checksums, and the looser floor gives up accuracy for nothing. | Rejected; the floor stays 5e-12. |
| Ten-point Owen rule for small skew (rejected) | The skew paths use the 20-point Gauss-Legendre rule for small `h` magnitudes; tried the 10-point rule there to halve the node work. | The worst error at `h = 0.6` is 1.26e-11, which exceeds the 1e-12 skew CDF contract. | Rejected for accuracy; small `h` keeps the 20-point rule. |
| Wrapped endpoint z caching (rejected) | `WrappedNormalCdfCache.ensureTerms` evaluates each endpoint `normCdf(z_end)`; tried caching the endpoint z values and walking them incrementally instead of recomputing the affine image. | Both variants measured neutral to slower on the wrapped quantile rows with unchanged checksums; the endpoint evaluation, not the affine image, dominates. | Rejected; the cache keeps the direct endpoint evaluation with the S4-M1826 saturation clamps. |

A 10-point rule was rejected for the public CDF (8.2e-11 worst error over the quantile domain) but a fixed-rule audit showed the 12-point rule is accurate to 1.55e-12 there. | Same-host four alternating pairs: `skew-normal quantile` 3.374/3.833/4.183/3.711M -> 5.376/5.603/5.615/5.617M (median about +49%) and `wrapped skew-normal quantile` 1.558/2.048/1.973/1.854M -> 2.088/2.278/2.158/2.166M (median about +13%), every candidate run above every baseline, with unchanged checksums (`2174.360`, `4282.426`). The wrapped contract grid stays bit-equal at 5.014e-10 worst round trip and the plain skew grid at 8.889e-6 worst relative; a 2080-point value diff moves only deep wrapped tails (worst absolute 4.79e-7 where the density is about 3e-6). | Adopted as S4-M1854: `OwenTGlTable12` is used only by the quantile paths for `0 < |alpha| <= 2`; public CDFs keep 20 points and wider shapes keep 24. A 393,060-point grid shows worst CDF changes of 3.09e-12 (skew) and 9.69e-12 (wrapped), and the contract grids stay bit-equal. | Adopted as S4-M1855: both public CDFs use `OwenTGlTable12` for `0 < |alpha| <= 2`; wider shapes keep 24 points and the unused 20-point code was removed. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1855-public-skew-cdf-twelve-point.md`. |

| Packed bulk fill for the standard charsets | `Charset.fill`/`fillFrom` drew one bounded `u64` per character (128-bit multiply-high plus a near-never rejection), so `alea alphanumeric` sat at 1.02x local Rust while Rust's `Alphanumeric` takes six bits from a `u32` per character. Fills of the standard 10/26/52/62-byte alphabets now take 4/5/6-bit chunks from one `u64` per 10-16 characters with rejection; `sample`/`sampleFrom` keep the bounded draw and other lengths keep the generic loop. | Same-host four alternating pairs: `alea alphanumeric` 999.8/889.3/863.0/757.7M -> 1269.8/1213.7/1261.1/1261.5M (median about +44%), every candidate run above every baseline, with the versioned checksum `182252759` -> `182224526`. A standalone probe of the kernel measured 715.8M -> 1023.6M (about 1.43x). | Adopted as S4-M1856; the versioned bulk-fill policy is recorded in `reproducibility-matrix.md`. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1856-packed-charset-fill.md`. |

| Polynomial erf replacement for the half-integer gamma anchor (rejected) | `gammaHalfErfTaylor` dominates the half-integer `gammaIncompleteP/Q` (the chi-squared quantile's CDF and the wrapped skew upper tail). Tried a minimax polynomial `erf(y)` over the used range `y in [0, 2.83]` to replace the ~27-35-term Taylor. | A pure-Python Chebyshev interpolation needs degree 22 for 1.5e-13 worst absolute error and stalls near 5e-14 at degree 24-26, versus the Taylor's ~1e-17. That is a 6x accuracy loss in the half-integer recurrence anchor for a projected ~10-15% gain on diagnostic quantile rows, so the fit was not carried into the library. | Rejected on accuracy grounds; the erf Taylor (with the S4-M1852 reciprocal table) stays. A Cephes-style rational erfc would be the next candidate if this path ever needs more headroom. |## Next Candidate

| Reciprocal-sigma substitution in the wrapped caches (rejected) | Both wrapped CDF caches and the public wrapped skew CDF divide `(x - mu + k*2pi)` by `sigma` per image per evaluation; tried a precomputed `1/sigma` multiply (bit-identical at the bench's `sigma = 1`). | Three alternating pairs: `wrapped normal quantile` 4.932/4.975/4.533M vs 5.392/4.833/4.936M, `wrapped skew-normal quantile` 1.820/2.088/2.133M vs 2.003/1.898/2.129M, and `wrapped skew-normal cdf` 5.202/4.875/5.438M vs 5.148/4.817/4.760M — mixed to slightly worse, inside the loaded-host noise band. | Rejected and reverted; the divisions are not the bottleneck (the Owen T and `normCdf` work dominates). |

| Vectorized cos polynomial for the Owen geometry build | The 12/24 libm `@cos` calls in `owenTGlTable12`/`owenTGlTable` cost about 3.3 ns each, so runtime-shape calls pay 40-80 ns of setup; literal-shape benches folded it and hid the gap (skew CDF 38.4 ns literal vs 86.8 ns runtime). Replaced `cos(theta)` with a degree-7 minimax polynomial in `theta^2` over `[0, atan(6)]`, evaluated four angles at a time with `@Vector(4, f64)`. | Polynomial worst absolute error 5.6e-16; a 393,060-point plain/wrapped skew CDF grid shows zero changed values at 17 digits. New `skew-normal cdf runtime` row: three alternating pairs 11.623/11.073/11.293M -> 14.633/13.932/14.345M (about +27%, unchanged checksum `1643.182`); the runtime quantile is flat within noise and the literal rows are unchanged. | Adopted as S4-M1858. Recorded in `compare/results/s4-m1858-owen-cos-polynomial.md`. |Continue performance triage on the remaining focused watch items only when a

| Lane-relative Kent fallback threshold (rejected) | `vectorKentPointFrom` finishes the last pending lanes with the scalar `kentSampleWFrom` once `active_count <= 4`; for an f64x4 vector that means the dense loop runs once and every survivor falls back. Tried `active_count <= L / 2` so the dense loop keeps running while at least half the lanes are active. | Three alternating pairs: `vector kent f64x4` 6.0/5.7/5.5M -> 5.5/5.2/5.2M, every candidate below every baseline (about -8%) with the expected stream change. Redrawing full vectors for two or three active lanes costs more than the scalar marginal sampler. | Rejected and reverted; the `<= 4` threshold stays. The Kent row is dominated by the per-lane `kentSampleWFrom` Bessel work, which needs a dense Bessel kernel rather than a threshold change. |new concrete hypothesis is available.

- Component costs (measured on the current host, loaded window): Owen T
  12-node ~21.7 ns, `normCdf` ~4.2 ns, `normPdf` ~2.2 ns, `gammaIncompleteQ(0.5, y)`
  ~20 ns (Taylor, y <= 4.5), ~46 ns (continued fraction, 4.5 < y < 18), ~21 ns
  (Mills, y >= 18); `gammaIncompleteQ(2, 4)` ~15 ns while the a=2.5 continued
  fraction near `x = a + 1` costs ~82 ns (the half-integer recurrence P is ~28 ns,
  which is why it stays). The wrapped skew quantile's per-eval cost is roughly
  40% Owen T, 28% CDF base, 11% density; further gains need fewer Owen nodes
  (accuracy-limited) or a faster exp (already rejected as slower in this loop).
- Reusable CDF/quantile models: the free functions rebuild their per-parameter
  setup on every call. Measured setup shares: `vonMisesCdf` ~127 ns/call with
  about 100 ns of `VonMisesCdfCache` build (a cached model would be ~3x for
  repeated calls), `wrappedSkewNormalCdf` ~200 ns with 60-80 ns of endpoint and
  geometry terms (~1.5x), and the runtime-shape `skewNormalCdf` ~70 ns with
  ~28 ns of geometry (~1.6x). A `CachedBinomial`-style model type
  (`init`/`cdf`/`pdf`/`quantile`) would amortize these; it is a feature/API
  decision rather than a kernel change, so it is left as the next feature
  candidate after the current evidence bar.
- Vector trig: a range-reduced degree-8 `cos` polynomial over
  `[0, pi/2]` matches libm to 3.3e-16 and costs 4.8 ns per f64x4 vector
  versus 9.9 ns for vector `@cos`. The directional kernels (`vonMisesVectorFrom`,
  `vectorVonMisesFisherFrom`, `vectorWatsonFrom`, `vectorBinghamFrom`,
  `vectorKentFrom`) call vector `cos`/`sin` once per accepted lane and are
  the slowest vector rows (4.9-7.1M vectors/s). S4-M1860 adopted it for the
  f64x4 von Mises proposal (`vector von mises` 7.1M -> 7.75M, +9%) with a
  versioned stream entry; the same substitution for the
  vMF/Watson/Bingham/Kent `cos`/`sin` pairs is the remaining candidate and
  is expected to be smaller because those rows are Bessel/ACG dominated. A
  custom vector `acos` (used by the von Mises acceptance) would be the
  larger win and the larger accuracy risk.
- Long-run bar: the same-session statistical bar is a 50M-sample long run
  (`alea-distcheck --long 50000000`: 28 blocks x 50M draws, about 27 min),
  recorded in S4-M1859. The 1e9 run is the next stricter bar and needs
  roughly 7-8 hours, so it runs in the background across turns; the
  S4-M1813 1e9 evidence came from such a long window. After the 1e9, the
  next rungs are a 4e9-sample soak (about 30 hours) or a 24-hour wall-clock
  soak with the gate block repeated at intervals, and a fresh 128GiB
  PractRand pass once PractRand is installed. Broader platform runners
  (QEMU, Wine, Wasmtime) stay blocked until that infrastructure is
  installed. `s4-m4-remaining-gaps.md` summarizes the
current blocker audit and the requirements for a successful next candidate:

- LogNormal: the inlined branchless exponential is now the exact default
  (S4-M1773 deliberate versioned mapping change), lifting the default to
  345.5M facade / 334.8M ScalarPrng direct f64 and 364.6M stddev=1 at 128MiB
  versus local Rust 236.2M / 116.2M. `LogNormalApproxF32`,
  `LogNormalExp2F32`, and `LogNormalNativeExp2F32` remain explicit bounded f32
  approximation paths, and `LogNormalFastExp` remains as a profile name for
  the default mapping. See `lognormal-transform-notes.md`,
  `compare/results/s4-m1761-lognormal-fast-exp.md`, and
  `compare/results/s4-m1773-lognormal-inlined-exp-default.md`.
- OpenClosed f64: exact endpoint-grid bulk fill now exceeds local Rust evidence
  after the `@mulAdd` raw-word conversion; keep `openclosed-endpoint-notes.md`
  for regression history and future portability checks.
- SIMD distributions: f32x8 repair is useful evidence, but standard,
  parameterized, same-candidate, all-accepted, block-fallback, FastPrng, and
  Alea4x64 lane-local repair rows all failed to beat current scalar lane-fill
  defaults. The S4-M1636/S4-M1640/S4-M1642/S4-M1646 packed discrete kernels
  are the first dense candidates to clear the bar (vector binomial fair,
  dyadic small, and dyadic large trials plus vector geometric), and the
  S4-M1628/S4-M1631/S4-M1633 `exp2`/`log2` transforms lifted the log+exp
  continuous rows. The S4-M1662 coverage sweep showed the remaining continuous
  vector rows at or above scalar per-sample throughput except `vector
  fisher-f` and the directional Bessel/Bingham rows; S4-M1736 aligns the
  FisherF harness engines and measures 37.8-39.1M vectors/s (151-156M lanes/s)
  with `ScalarPrng` against 149-154M scalar direct, and the real-harness
  direct row is 133.6M lanes/s (within ~10-12% of scalar direct), so the
  earlier ~88M-vs-114-129M comparison mixed facade/direct and engine choices;
  the vector direct lanes are at scalar parity. `VectorStudentT` was lifted +52-56% by the per-lane free function,
  while the same substitution for Maxwell and normal-inverse-Gaussian was
  neutral. Future work needs a dense kernel for the remaining per-lane
  samplers (Poisson, hypergeometric, Zipf/Zeta, negative-binomial,
  Bessel/Bingham directional) that meets `simd-distribution-kernel-notes.md`.
- Vector discrete samplers: the dyadic/fair space is closed for the current
  bar. `vector binomial` covers fair (S4-M1646, +125-145%) and dyadic
  small/large trials (S4-M1636/S4-M1640, +57-70x and ~7x) with pmf-binned 1e9
  gates (S4-M1638/S4-M1648), and `vector geometric`/`geometric-failures`
  cover dyadic probabilities (S4-M1642, +66-91%) with its own 1e9 pmf gate
  (S4-M1644). The negative-binomial dyadic sum and the scalar dyadic
  geometric kernel were measured and rejected (S4-M1643-adjacent probes);
  Poisson, hypergeometric, and Zipf/Zeta remain per-lane at scalar parity and
  need a different dense rejection/inversion shape.
- Packed chance fills: the dyadic series is closed for the current bar
  (`fillChance` up to denominator 2^31, `fillRatio` power-of-two
  denominators, the `Bernoulli` threshold dispatch, and the nibble kernels
  from S4-M1420..S4-M1430). Remaining ideas are non-power-of-two `fillRatio`
  denominators, which need retry-position bookkeeping and measured only about
  2x in a standalone byte-rejection probe, and per-trial branch costs that
  make the bytewise non-dyadic chance fill slower than the one-word loop.
- Vector directional samplers: S4-M1583 removed the S² vMF/Kent Wood
  rejection loop with the exact truncated-exponential inversion, lifting
  `vector von mises-fisher` to 20.1M and `vector kent` to 5.0M vectors/s.
  The remaining slow rows are `vector kent` and `vector bingham`, whose
  per-lane Bessel/`@log`/ACG work still scalarizes; scalar-per-lane fallbacks
  and noinline isolation both regress the scalar rows through code layout.
  Future work needs a dense vector candidate for the remaining Bessel or
  Bingham acceptance work, or a layout-stable way to share the scalar tails.
- Central binomial rejection: the full BTPE envelope was implemented,
  cross-checked against `rand_distr 0.5.1`, and rejected (S4-M1443). It is
  exact but measures -22-24% at `binomial large` and -45-46% at
  `binomial mean-12`, and Rust's cached BTPE also stays below both Alea rows.
  HIN and the exponential waiting-time race remain the right central paths;
  the small-`p` HIN mean bias is closed by the region-2 dominance fix
  (S4-M1550), and the remaining binomial pressure is `Rng` facade overhead
  and the small-mean inverse-CDF layout problem, not a missing central
  algorithm.
- Quantile throughput: the S4-M1737..M1749 Newton sweep lifted the diagnostic
  quantiles: wrapped normal 0.068 -> 0.78-0.81M/s, von Mises 0.063 ->
  0.75-0.78M/s, skew-normal 0.005 -> 1.43M/s (S4-M1752 Owen T fast path), Rice 0.018 ->
  2.33M/s (S4-M1754 Marcum recurrence), Hoyt 0.043 -> 0.54-0.62M/s, beta 0.575 -> 3.1-3.5M/s, gamma
  0.298 -> 3.9-4.2M/s, chi-squared 0.191 -> 1.8-1.9M/s, PERT 0.355 ->
  1.6-1.7M/s, StudentT 0.194 -> 0.73-0.75M/s, FisherF 0.112 -> 1.0M/s,
  ExGaussian 0.269 -> 2.6M/s, InverseGaussian 0.247 -> 3.0M/s, and wrapped
  skew-normal 0.091M quantiles/s (0.731M cdf/s) before the S4-M1762 image fast
  path, which lifts it to 0.269M quantiles/s and 1.326M cdf/s at the same
  1GiB-equivalent count with unchanged checksums. The S4-M1763 wrapped normal
  cache lifts the wrapped normal quantile 0.821M -> 3.777M quantiles/s (4.6x),
  and the shared Newton-correction early exit lifts von Mises 0.791M ->
  0.979M, Hoyt 0.641M -> 0.775M, Rice 2.358M -> 2.769M, and skew-normal
  0.891M -> 1.037M with unchanged checksums. The S4-M1764 integer-df fast
  path lifts StudentT 0.778M -> 10.250M quantiles/s (13.2x) by using the exact
  small-integer CDF recurrence instead of the beta inversion, and the gamma
  Newton-correction exit lifts chi-squared 1.884M -> 2.693M and gamma
  4.327M -> 4.996M. The S4-M1765 integer-shape recurrences lift Hoyt
  0.728M -> 4.521M quantiles/s (CDF 4.146M -> 82.674M cdf/s), FisherF
  1.051M -> 13.596M, beta 3.588M -> 20.097M, and PERT 1.712M -> 20.399M with
  unchanged checksums. The S4-M1766 von Mises cache lifts that quantile
  0.967M -> 1.568M with an unchanged checksum by hoisting the Bessel ratios
  and density normalization and walking the series sine/cosine with a
  resynced angle recurrence. The slowest remaining diagnostic quantiles are
  now wrapped skew-normal 0.342M (S4-M1768 tightened the quantile-only image
  skip and S4-M1771 replaced the far-tail adaptive Simpson with a closed-form
  `T(h,∞) = Q(h)/2` minus tail rule, lifting the wrapped CDF 2.11x and the
  quantile 1.20x) and skew-normal 1.016M, both Owen-T/transcendental limited. S4-M1769 hoisted the
  constant gamma density normalization out of the Newton loop, lifting
  chi-squared 2.693M -> 3.226M quantiles/s (the equivalent beta hoist was
  rejected after a 24% generic-path regression). The
  evidence is `compare/results/s4-m1737-wrapped-normal-newton-quantile.md`,
  `s4-m1739-von-mises-newton-quantile.md`, `s4-m1741-quantile-newton-sweep.md`,
  `s4-m1743-fisher-f-beta-quantile.md`, `s4-m1745-beta-newton-quantile.md`,
  `s4-m1747-gamma-chi-squared-newton-quantile.md`, and
  `s4-m1749-exgaussian-inverse-gaussian-newton-quantile.md`; the wrapped
  skew-normal is recorded in
  `s4-m1758-wrapped-skew-normal-circular-distribution.md` and
  `s4-m1762-wrapped-skew-normal-quantile.md` and
  `s4-m1763-quantile-newton-fast-paths.md` and
  `s4-m1764-student-t-integer-quantile.md` and
  `s4-m1765-integer-shape-recurrences.md` and
  `s4-m1766-von-mises-quantile-cache.md` and
  `s4-m1768-1e7-long-run-refresh.md` and
  `s4-m1769-gamma-quantile-density-hoist.md` and
  `s4-m1771-owen-t-far-tail.md`.
- Long-run scaling: `alea-distcheck --long N` draws N samples in each of 28
  long-run blocks, so `--long 1000000` costs about 29 s and `--long
  4000000` about 131 s of CPU; a 1e9 run needs roughly 7-8 hours, not one.
  Use 10M-50M for same-session evidence and reserve 1e9 for overnight runs.
- Quantile sweep status (post S4-M1862): the S4-M1823..M1862 series lifted
  the diagnostic quantile rows to roughly wrapped skew-normal 2.2-2.6M/s,
  von Mises 4.2M/s, chi-squared 4.8M/s, skew-normal 5.6M/s, wrapped
  normal/Rice/Hoyt 6.5M/s, gamma 7.2M/s, ex-Gaussian 9.4M/s, and
  inverse-Gaussian 10.5M/s; the public skew-normal CDF reaches about
  28-41M calls/s with a literal shape and about 14M calls/s with a runtime
  shape (S4-M1858: the literal rows fold the Owen geometry build), and the
  wrapped skew-normal CDF is about 5-6M calls/s. S4-M1854 moved the skew
  quantile paths to a 12-point Owen rule, S4-M1855 moved the public CDFs to
  the same rule, and S4-M1858 vectorized the geometry build, so CDF/quantile
  round trips no longer mix rules and runtime shapes skip the libm cos
  setup. The vector directional rows were also touched: S4-M1860 lifted
  `vector von mises f64x4` 7.1M -> 7.75M (about +9%) with the same cos
  polynomial, and S4-M1862 added the long-run lane gate for its versioned
  stream. The remaining pressure is the wrapped quantile's
  endpoint/density/normCdf work, the plain skew path's
  Owen-T/transcendental cost, and the Kent/Bingham Bessel kernels. Evidence:
  `compare/results/s4-m1823-half-integer-gamma-cdf.md` through
  `s4-m1855-public-skew-cdf-twelve-point.md`.
- Wrapped skew-normal sampling: 401.6M samples/s scalar and 60.8M vectors/s
  (243M lanes/s) for f64x4, reusing the skew-normal sampler and the circular
  wrap. Evidence: `compare/results/s4-m1758-wrapped-skew-normal-circular-distribution.md`.
- Platform runners: QEMU, Wine, and Wasmtime are not installed on the current
  host, so second-platform execution beyond the Node WASI suite and the six
  crosscheck compile targets stays blocked until infrastructure exists.
  `zig build runtimecheck` confirms `required found=3 missing=0;
  opportunities found=0 missing=10` (qemu-aarch64/riscv64/x86_64 and their
  `-static` forms, wine/wine64, wasmtime, wasmer). To raise the platform bar,
  install any one of them (`brew install qemu`, `brew install --cask
  wine-stable`, or `brew install wasmtime`), re-run `zig build runtimecheck`
  (it fails when an opportunity runner appears, prompting a blocker-audit
  refresh), then run the crosscheck matrix and record the results.
